- **`CalculationFrictionEvidence` (`frictionEvidence.ts`) is the core new signal**: for one concept and student, graded attempts are split into a high-`computationalLoad` slice (`>= HIGH_COMPUTATIONAL_LOAD_THRESHOLD`) and a low-load slice; each slice's accuracy is `null` below `MASTERY_CONSTANTS.MIN_OBSERVATIONS_FOR_COMPONENT` observations (never a ratio from too few data points); `frictionDetected` is `true` only when BOTH slices are sufficiently observed AND `lowLoad.accuracy - highLoad.accuracy >= CALCULATION_FRICTION_ACCURACY_GAP`. This is the three-way distinction made structural, not just documented: a concept with equally-low accuracy on BOTH slices (conceptual weakness — `@ipmat/adaptive-selection`'s territory) never triggers `frictionDetected`; raw slowness with no load-conditioning (a future Speed Lab's territory) is never read by this provider at all.
- **`evaluateCalculationGym()` (`applicability.ts`) is the ONE authoritative applicability decision**, called only through `runTrainingSystemProvider()`. Fails closed with two distinguishable reasons — `insufficient_evidence` (no concept has both slices observed enough to compare) vs. `no_calculation_friction_detected` (evidence exists, no meaningful gap) — never guesses. Among concepts showing friction, the target is the single largest observed accuracy gap, lexicographic concept name as the final deterministic break — one directly-observed number, not a weighted composite.
- **`CalculationGymRequirement` (`types.ts`) is a structural superset of the shared `TrainingRequirement`**, adding `targetConceptName`, `stage`, `minComputationalLoad`, `requireMultiStep`, `requireTimePressured` — provider-specific metadata that does not belong in the common contract (D-053 item 3), while still satisfying `TrainingSystemSelectionOutcome.requirement: TrainingRequirement` with no cast needed at the outcome boundary.
- **`determineCalculationTrainingStage()` (`progression.ts`) gates three named, closed, PROVISIONAL stages — `foundational` -> `mixed` -> `time_pressured`** (`CALCULATION_TRAINING_STAGES`, grows deliberately, same discipline as `RecommendedTrainingMode`/`TrainingActionType`) — with EACH transition requiring its OWN independent evidence slice at `STAGE_MASTERY_ACCURACY`: `foundational -> mixed` requires enough low-load, non-multi-step attempts; `mixed -> time_pressured` requires its own SEPARATE high-load, non-time-pressured slice. A student who has only ever attempted foundational-shaped questions (even at 100% accuracy) advances to `mixed` but can never reach `time_pressured` on that evidence alone — a dedicated regression test proves this directly. Under insufficient evidence at any gate, the function returns the highest stage whose own gate actually cleared, defaulting to `foundational`.
- **Selection (`selection.ts`) is provider-local** (delegating to `selectNextQuestion()` would silently discard the one computationalLoad-specific signal this provider exists to act on) and uses EXACTLY the four approved steps, nothing else: filter by `CalculationGymRequirement` (concept match, load floor, `requireMultiStep`/`requireTimePressured`, published, structurally valid) -> least-exposed-first (`computeLocalExposureCounts()`, a small, independently-implemented per-questionId counter — NOT imported from `@ipmat/adaptive-selection`'s `computeExposureCounts()`, since this provider is a peer, not a coordinator like `@ipmat/training-orchestration`) -> computational-load closest to the requirement's own floor -> lexicographic questionId. `CalculationGymProvider.select()` runtime-checks that the `requirement` it receives actually is a `CalculationGymRequirement` (structurally) and returns `status: "error", code: "invalid_context"` if not — the one legitimate use of `error` here (an impossible call path, never an ordinary lack of matching content).
- **No composite score, no confidence/motivation field, no "priorityScore," ever** — enforced by 5 dedicated `@ts-expect-error` regression-guard tests on `ComputationalLoadSlice`/`CalculationFrictionEvidence`/`CalculationGymRequirement`, extending D-051/D-052/D-053's discipline to this concrete provider.
**Consequences:** A new domain package, 40 new tests (5 friction-evidence, 7 progression, 6 applicability, 9 selection, 4 end-to-end provider/contract-integration, 5 compile-time regression guards, 2 dependency-boundary, 2 auto-discovered by `domainBoundary.test.ts`) — 641 across the repo (601 + 40), typecheck/lint/build clean across all 16 workspaces. `npm install` was required once more for the new workspace. Deliberately, explicitly out of scope for this unit: any change to `@ipmat/training-orchestration`/`TrainingActionType` (no wiring of Calculation Gym into a real selection flow yet), any HTTP/API route, any UI, any AI/ML call, any finer calculation-skill taxonomy (fractions/ratios/decimals/estimation/shortcuts — still unrepresentable in current DNA), any real-data calibration of `HIGH_COMPUTATIONAL_LOAD_THRESHOLD`/`CALCULATION_FRICTION_ACCURACY_GAP`/`STAGE_MASTERY_ACCURACY` (all still provisional policy, same as every other threshold in this codebase). Speed Lab, Trap Lab, Novelty/Pressure/Revision/Mock providers remain unbuilt.

## D-055 — Speed Lab (Phase 5E-3) is the SECOND concrete `TrainingSystemProvider`: a general time-efficiency provider whose applicability rule is an immutable, correctness-independent denominator over correct-and-slow evidence, with a count-based (never rate-based) progression gated on a DISJOINT conceptual-load slice, and a `time_constrained` stage deliberately scoped narrower than a future Pressure Training system

**Status:** Accepted
**Context:** With Calculation Gym (D-054) as the first concrete provider, Speed Lab is the second — proving `@ipmat/training-systems` (D-053) supports genuinely different training modes, not just one shape repeated. Speed Lab's question is explicitly different from Calculation Gym's: "is there evidence this student takes too much time relative to expected time, independent of whether calculation is the bottleneck" — never a second calculation-friction engine, never reading `computationalLoad`. The read-first inspection found: no package stores a precomputed per-attempt `speedRatio`; `@ipmat/autopsy`'s `AUTOPSY_THRESHOLDS.SLOW_SPEED_RATIO`/`FAST_SPEED_RATIO` is the one canonical, already-in-use "slow"/"fast" boundary (used for `BehaviorSignals` derivation), reused directly rather than redefined; `difficultyDimensions.conceptualLoad` (the field Calculation Gym does NOT use — it uses `computationalLoad`) is the honest, existing hook for excluding "slow because the concept itself is unfamiliar" from clean speed evidence; the codebase already has an established rule (`@ipmat/mastery`'s `PressureBreakdown` doc comment) that "pressure" is read only from `testingModes.includes("time_pressured")`, never inferred from raw timing — Speed Lab's final stage follows this same rule rather than inventing a numeric proxy from `difficultyDimensions.timePressure`.

The initial design was reviewed and revised before implementation, per three explicit adjustments: (1) the third progression stage, originally "exam_pressure", was renamed to `time_constrained` and scoped narrowly — it means only "this question carries the existing `time_pressured` testingMode," explicitly NOT the future, broader Pressure Training system (which will own exam-condition pressure such as sustained sequences, section constraints, switching between question types); (2) the tie-break's "lowest `expectedTimeSeconds`" step is documented ONLY as a deterministic preference for the tighter available expected-time budget, never claimed to represent a harder/better/maximum training stimulus, since `expectedTimeSeconds` is uncalibrated content metadata; (3) the applicability denominator was made explicit and immutable — `eligibleGradedCount` (submitted, `isCorrect !== null`, validly timed with `expectedTimeSeconds > 0`, hint-free, non-`time_pressured`, `conceptualLoad` below the provisional threshold) counts ALL graded attempts in that population **regardless of correctness**; only `correctSlowCount` (the subset with `isCorrect === true` and `speedRatio >= AUTOPSY_THRESHOLDS.SLOW_SPEED_RATIO`) is the numerator. An incorrect-but-otherwise-eligible attempt therefore dilutes the fraction (counted in the denominator, never the numerator) rather than being silently dropped; anything outside the 7-condition population has zero effect on it.

A genuine design flaw was found and fixed during implementation, before any test was left contradictory: an initial rate-based progression gate ("75% of a stage's own attempts must be good-paced") is the near-exact mathematical complement of the applicability trigger ("50%+ of the same population must be correct-and-slow") — algebraically, for any nonnegative attempt counts, a concept can never simultaneously satisfy both a >=50% correct-slow fraction and a >=75% good-pace rate over the identical population. This meant the original design could never actually recommend `mixed_pace`/`time_constrained` while the provider was still `applicable` — a live bug, not just a hard-to-write test. Fixed by making BOTH progression gates COUNT-based (has the student accumulated `MASTERY_CONSTANTS.MIN_OBSERVATIONS_FOR_COMPONENT` good-paced attempts — reusing the same evidence-sufficiency constant a third time) over a slice DISJOINT from the one the OTHER gate reads, mirroring Calculation Gym's own foundational/mixed (low-vs-high-`computationalLoad`) split: the `steady_pace` gate reads low-`conceptualLoad` good-pace count, `mixed_pace`'s gate reads the complementary (moderate/high) `conceptualLoad` slice — mathematically independent of the low-load applicability trigger, so both can hold at once.

**Decision:**
- **New package `packages/domain/speed-lab` (`@ipmat/speed-lab`)**, database-free, depending on exactly `@ipmat/training-systems` (the contract), `@ipmat/mastery` (`MASTERY_CONSTANTS.MIN_OBSERVATIONS_FOR_COMPONENT`, reused a third time), and `@ipmat/autopsy` (`AUTOPSY_THRESHOLDS.SLOW_SPEED_RATIO` — reusing the SAME canonical "slow" definition already used for behavior-signal derivation, avoiding a second, competing number). Deliberately does NOT depend on `@ipmat/calculation-gym`, `@ipmat/adaptive-selection`, `@ipmat/repair-selection`, or `@ipmat/training-orchestration` — verified both by an explicit `package.json` dependency-set assertion and a source-text scan for forbidden import specifiers (`dependencyBoundary.test.ts`, the same pattern D-054 established).
- **`SpeedEvidenceSlice` (`speedEvidence.ts`) is the core new signal**, over the EXACT immutable 7-condition denominator above. `correctSlowCount` is the only value that can trigger applicability; `incorrectSlowCount` is computed from the SAME population and surfaced in `notes` as diagnostic-only, explicitly stating that wrongness may reflect a conceptual or calculation difficulty this provider cannot, and does not claim to, distinguish from observable data alone — the concrete, tested answer to "how are incorrect attempts treated."
- **`evaluateSpeedLab()` (`applicability.ts`) is the ONE authoritative applicability decision**, called only through `runTrainingSystemProvider()`. Fails closed with two distinguishable reasons — `insufficient_evidence` (no concept has enough eligible attempts) vs. `no_speed_inefficiency_detected` (evidence exists, fraction too low) — a single slow attempt can never trigger this, since `slowFraction` is `null` below `MASTERY_CONSTANTS.MIN_OBSERVATIONS_FOR_COMPONENT`. Among concepts showing inefficiency, the target is the single largest observed slow fraction, lexicographic concept name as the final deterministic break.
- **`SpeedLabRequirement` (`types.ts`) is a structural superset of the shared `TrainingRequirement`**, adding `targetConceptName`, `stage`, `maxConceptualLoad`, `requireTimePressured` — and deliberately has NO "target speed ratio" field: speed ratio is an attempt OUTCOME, not a static property of an unattempted candidate, so it cannot be a selection filter (enforced by a dedicated `@ts-expect-error` regression test).
- **`determineSpeedLabStage()` (`progression.ts`) gates three named, closed, PROVISIONAL stages — `steady_pace` -> `mixed_pace` -> `time_constrained`** (`SPEED_LAB_STAGES`, grows deliberately) — with disjoint, count-based evidence at each transition, per the fix above. `time_constrained` requires `testingModes.includes("time_pressured")`, documented explicitly as representing only "this question carries a time constraint," never the full future Pressure Training domain.
- **Selection (`selection.ts`) is provider-local** (delegating to `selectNextQuestion()` would discard the conceptualLoad/time-pressure-specific filter this provider exists to apply) and uses exactly three approved steps: filter by `SpeedLabRequirement` (concept match, `maxConceptualLoad` ceiling when set, `requireTimePressured`, published, structurally valid) -> least-exposed-first (`computeLocalExposureCounts()`, independently reimplemented — NOT imported from `@ipmat/calculation-gym`'s or `@ipmat/adaptive-selection`'s exposure helpers, since this provider is a peer, not a coordinator) -> lowest `expectedTimeSeconds` (documented per adjustment 2, above, as an uncalibrated preference only) -> lexicographic questionId. `SpeedLabProvider.select()` runtime-checks that its `requirement` is actually a `SpeedLabRequirement` and returns `status: "error", code: "invalid_context"` otherwise — the one legitimate use of `error` here.
- **No composite score, no confidence/motivation field, no "priorityScore"/"targetSpeedRatio," ever** — enforced by 5 dedicated `@ts-expect-error` regression-guard tests on `SpeedEvidenceSlice`/`SpeedLabRequirement`, extending D-051/D-052/D-053/D-054's discipline to this second concrete provider.
**Consequences:** A new domain package, 52 new tests (11 speed-evidence incl. every inclusion/exclusion condition individually, 9 progression, 7 applicability, 10 selection, 5 end-to-end provider/contract-integration, 6 compile-time regression guards, 2 dependency-boundary, 2 auto-discovered by `domainBoundary.test.ts`) — 693 across the repo (641 + 52), typecheck/lint/build clean across all 17 workspaces. `npm install` was required once more for the new workspace. Deliberately, explicitly out of scope for this unit: any change to `@ipmat/training-orchestration`/`TrainingActionType`, any HTTP/API route, any UI, any AI/ML call, any real-data calibration of `LOW_CONCEPTUAL_LOAD_THRESHOLD`/`SLOW_FRACTION_THRESHOLD`/`GOOD_PACE_SPEED_RATIO` (all still provisional policy), the future Pressure Training system itself (Speed Lab's `time_constrained` stage is deliberately narrower). Trap Lab, Novelty/Pressure/Revision/Mock providers remain unbuilt.

## D-056 — Trap Lab (Phase 5E-4) is the THIRD concrete `TrainingSystemProvider`: candidate-level "trap-associated failure recurrence" evaluated at the error-taxonomy-CODE level, deliberately cross-concept and cumulative-with-no-decay, with no progression vocabulary and a candidate-level epistemic boundary enforced through wording, not a confidence field

**Status:** Accepted
**Context:** With Calculation Gym (D-054, computationalLoad-conditioned accuracy) and Speed Lab (D-055, timing-relative-to-expected) as the first two concrete providers, Trap Lab is the third and answers a genuinely different question: "is there observable evidence that this student repeatedly encounters a specific *represented* error/trap pattern, and if so, should we deliberately train that pattern." The read-first inspection found: `ErrorTaxonomy.code` (restated in the domain layer as `AutopsyQuestionContext.trapErrorTaxonomyCode: string | null`) is the finest-grained represented error unit in the current data model — strictly finer than `ErrorCategory`'s 5 broad values. `@ipmat/autopsy`'s `deriveCandidateErrorEvidence()` already establishes the exact deterministic pattern this provider generalizes: for a submitted incorrect attempt, if the question's designated trap code is present, propose it as a CANDIDATE (never confirmed) error — Trap Lab independently re-derives this same rule over bulk history (not imported; `@ipmat/autopsy` is not a dependency for this function) to compute cross-attempt recurrence. No confirmed-diagnosis data (`RepairPlan`, confirmed `Autopsy.confirmed`) is reachable from `TrainingSystemContext` at all — this rules out "one confirmed diagnosis is enough" as a V1 trigger, not by choice but by what the shared contract structurally provides.

The design went through three review passes before implementation, converging on:
1. **No mandatory concept scoping.** `ErrorTaxonomy` and `Concept` are distinct dimensions in Question DNA; a code such as `base_confusion` may legitimately recur across different concepts. Recurrence is evaluated **at the error-taxonomy-code level only, aggregated across the student's ENTIRE supplied attempt history regardless of concept** — concept is optional, selection-time-only contextual scope (`TrapLabRequirement.targetConceptName?`), never part of a trap's identity, and V1's `evaluate()` never populates it.
2. **Distinct-questionId recurrence is the only hard gate; surface diversity (taxonomy cell/pattern family) is diagnostic-only.** The current single-chapter content model may not have enough distinct qualifying cells per code to justify a second hard gate yet — two distinct questions on the SAME cell still establish recurrence.
3. **Cumulative history, explicitly disclosed, never solved with an invented decay/confidence score.** Once a code reaches recurrence, later correct/resisted attempts do not remove or reduce it; this is documented as an intentional V1 limitation, deferred to a future Revision/Overtraining/Exposure-control system.
4. **Optional `context.errorTaxonomy` enrichment must never change the applicability decision.** The raw `trapErrorTaxonomyCode` string is always the authoritative grouping key; a supplied-but-incomplete taxonomy list only changes diagnostic wording (a `taxonomy_enrichment_missing:<code>` note), never the recurrence count or target — verified by a dedicated invariance test (identical decision whether `context.errorTaxonomy` is omitted, empty, or missing the relevant code).
5. **Epistemic language is tightened by wording, not by adding a confidence field.** Trap Lab observes "repeated incorrect attempts on questions whose authors designated the same trap-taxonomy code" — never the student's private reasoning, a confirmed error, or a diagnosed misconception. Every Trap-Lab-owned explanation/notes/diagnostic string is regression-tested to avoid "confirmed"/"proven"/"diagnos"/"student's reasoning," and the applicable-path explanation is asserted to contain "trap-associated"/"trap-tagged" phrasing instead. The mandated disclaimer sentence ("This reflects repeated failure on trap-tagged questions, not a confirmed diagnosis of the student's reasoning") lives in type/doc-comment prose for developers, not in the runtime-facing strings the regression test scans — those two audiences are handled differently on purpose.

**Decision:**
- **New package `packages/domain/trap-lab` (`@ipmat/trap-lab`)**, database-free, depending on exactly `@ipmat/training-systems` (the contract) and `@ipmat/autopsy` (`AUTOPSY_THRESHOLDS.REPEATED_EVIDENCE_MIN_COUNT` — reused directly for the recurrence gate; the SAME constant already used for `HistoricalSignals`' own repeated-X fields, avoiding a second competing minimum). **Notably, `@ipmat/mastery` is NOT a dependency** — a first for this provider family: Trap Lab's recurrence gate is a discrete distinct-question count, not a statistical-reliability floor over a continuous measure, so `MASTERY_CONSTANTS.MIN_OBSERVATIONS_FOR_COMPONENT` is the wrong fit. **Zero new provisional numeric constants are introduced** — also a first; every threshold this provider uses is reused, not invented. Deliberately does NOT depend on `@ipmat/calculation-gym`, `@ipmat/speed-lab`, `@ipmat/adaptive-selection`, `@ipmat/repair-selection`, or `@ipmat/training-orchestration` — verified both by an explicit `package.json` dependency-set assertion and a source-text scan for forbidden import specifiers.
- **`TrapAssociatedFailureRecurrence` (`trapEvidence.ts`) is the core new signal**, computed per error-taxonomy code, aggregated across ALL concepts: `distinctFailingQuestionIds` (SET-derived; a single question retried repeatedly contributes at most one entry) is the only field that gates applicability; `distinctConceptNames`/`distinctPatternTaxonomyCellIds`/`distinctPatternFamilyNames` (surface-diversity) and `resistanceQuestionIds`/`hintAssistedResistanceQuestionIds` (correct-attempt tracking, hint-free vs. hint-assisted, never subtracted from recurrence) are diagnostic-only. Different codes never combine, even when they share an `ErrorCategory` — grouping is strictly by the exact code string.
- **`evaluateTrapLab()` (`applicability.ts`) is the ONE authoritative applicability decision**, called only through `runTrainingSystemProvider()`. Fails closed with two distinguishable reasons — `insufficient_evidence` (zero incorrect trap-tagged attempts anywhere) vs. `no_recurring_trap_detected` (such attempts exist, but no code reaches the distinct-question threshold) — a single incorrect attempt, or the same question retried any number of times, can never trigger this. Among codes independently reaching recurrence, the target is the largest `distinctFailingQuestionIds` count, then lexicographic `errorTaxonomyCode` — one directly-observed integer, never a weighted composite.
- **`TrapLabRequirement` (`types.ts`)**: `{ targetErrorTaxonomyCode: string; targetConceptName?: string }` — a structural superset of `TrainingRequirement`. `targetConceptName` is honored by `select()` only when a caller explicitly supplies one; V1's own `evaluate()` never does, so default selection is cross-concept — a deliberate, documented consequence of treating the code as the pattern's true identity, not an oversight.
- **No progression vocabulary.** Unlike Calculation Gym/Speed Lab, no continuous, graduated DNA dimension exists for "trap intensity" to gate stage transitions on — a speculative exposure→discrimination→resistance vocabulary would either be unevidenceable (no signal for "discrimination") or just re-encode the resistance diagnostic already tracked without adding information. Resistance remains a named diagnostic field, never a gate, mastery percentage, or score.
- **Selection (`selection.ts`) is provider-local**, filtering by trap-code match, optional concept match, published, structurally valid, then tie-breaking: unseen-taxonomy-cell partition preferred (the anti-memorization "repeat the trap, vary the surface" rule — cell exposure derived from BOTH correct and incorrect prior attempts against the same code) → least prior exposure (`computeLocalExposureCounts()`, independently reimplemented, not imported from any sibling provider) → lexicographic questionId. `TrapLabProvider.select()` runtime-checks its requirement's shape and returns `status: "error", code: "invalid_context"` otherwise — the one legitimate use of `error`.
- **No composite score, no confidence/motivation/psychological field, ever** — enforced by 6 dedicated `@ts-expect-error`/type regression-guard tests, plus a dedicated epistemic-language regression test scanning every Trap-Lab-produced `explanation`/`notes`/diagnostic string for confirmation-implying wording across 7 representative scenarios.
**Consequences:** A new domain package, 69 new tests (20 evidence incl. every inclusion/exclusion condition and the enrichment-invariance proof, 12 applicability incl. cross-concept and category-non-combination cases, 13 selection incl. variation/exposure/concept-filter behavior, 6 end-to-end provider/cross-domain-independence, 7 compile-time regression guards, 7 epistemic-language, 2 dependency-boundary, 2 auto-discovered by `domainBoundary.test.ts`) — 762 across the repo (693 + 69), typecheck/lint/build clean across all 18 workspaces. `npm install` was required once more for the new workspace. No implementation-time design contradiction was found this pass (unlike D-055's progression-gate fix) — the three prior design-review passes already resolved the concept-scoping, recurrence-semantics, and epistemic-language questions before any code was written. Deliberately, explicitly out of scope for this unit: any change to `@ipmat/training-orchestration`/`TrainingActionType`, any HTTP/API route, any UI, any AI/ML call, confirmed-diagnosis-based recurrence (would require a `TrainingSystemContext` extension — a separate, later decision), any recency/decay/exposure-control model, Novelty/Pressure/Revision/Mock providers.

## D-057 — Training Lab Playground (Phase 5E-PLAYGROUND) is a new top-level `apps/training-playground` (Vite + React), an INTERNAL development/debug surface only, that dispatches deterministic fixture scenarios directly to the real public domain entry points and displays their real, unmodified output — never a second decision engine, never the production student app

**Status:** Accepted
**Context:** By 5E-4 (D-056), the repository has real deterministic training intelligence (`@ipmat/calculation-gym`, `@ipmat/speed-lab`, `@ipmat/trap-lab`, `@ipmat/training-orchestration`) but no way to inspect how any of it actually behaves short of reading test files. The goal was a local internal tool to feed deterministic fixture scenarios into the existing systems and inspect their actual outputs — explicitly not the production student dashboard (`/apps/web`, still not built), not a new decision engine, and not real AI/DB integration. Repository inspection found no existing frontend/app of any kind (`ls apps` — no such directory) and no frontend dependencies anywhere in the workspace, so there was no existing stack to reuse; the user was asked directly rather than guessed at, and chose Vite + React as a new top-level `apps/training-playground`, kept structurally separate from the still-unbuilt production `/apps/web`.
**Decision:**
- **New top-level workspace `apps/training-playground` (`@ipmat/training-playground`)**, added to the root `workspaces` array (now `packages/*`, `packages/domain/*`, `apps/*`), the root `vitest.config.ts` include glob (now also matches `apps/**/test/**/*.test.ts`), and the root `lint` script (now `eslint . --ext .ts,.tsx`, the first `.tsx` source in the repo — scoped via an `.eslintrc.cjs` `overrides` entry for `apps/*/src/**` rather than widening the base config's env/parserOptions for every domain package). Depends on exactly the domain packages it actually calls: `@ipmat/training-systems`, `@ipmat/calculation-gym`, `@ipmat/speed-lab`, `@ipmat/trap-lab`, `@ipmat/training-orchestration`, `@ipmat/autopsy`/`@ipmat/mastery` (types only), plus `react`/`react-dom`/`vite`/`@vitejs/plugin-react` — no Prisma, no Anthropic SDK, no test-library/jsdom (no component-rendering tests were required, so none was added).
- **`src/domain/runScenario.ts` is the ONLY place the playground calls into the domain layer**, and it contains ZERO decision logic — two pure input-shape adapters (`toTrainingSystemContext()`/`toTrainingOrchestrationInput()`, mapping a shared `TrainingPlaygroundFixture` onto the exact input type each real entry point already declares) plus a one-branch dispatcher calling either `runTrainingSystemProvider()` (for `calculation-gym`/`speed-lab`/`trap-lab`) or `orchestrateNextTrainingAction()` (for `training-orchestration`) and returning the result completely unmodified. Verified by a regression test asserting the file never redeclares any domain package's own named threshold constant, and calls each real entry point exactly once.
- **A scenario may exercise MULTIPLE systems independently against the SAME fixture** (`scenario.systemsToRun: TrainingPlaygroundSystemId[]`) — e.g. the "mixed evidence" scenario runs `calculation-gym`, `speed-lab`, `trap-lab`, AND `training-orchestration` separately and displays all four real, unmerged outcomes side by side, proving multiple systems can be independently applicable while orchestration's own unmodified `REPAIR_PRECEDES_ADAPTIVE` policy is what actually decides. This is explicitly NOT a second orchestration layer — nothing in the playground ranks or merges these four outcomes; the UI simply renders each one.
- **10 required scenarios**, a centralized typed catalog (`src/domain/scenarios.ts`): calculation friction, speed inefficiency, trap recurrence, repair first, repair fallback, adaptive coverage gap, insufficient evidence, candidate filtering, no eligible question, mixed evidence. Every scenario's fixture data was independently verified against the REAL domain calls (not just hand-calculated) via `test/integration.test.ts`; one genuine cross-contamination issue was found and fixed during fixture construction — the "mixed evidence" scenario's speed-lab-shaped attempts initially diluted below `SLOW_FRACTION_THRESHOLD` once combined with the other three evidence types sharing the same low `conceptualLoad`, fixed by giving the calculation-gym-shaped and trap-shaped batches a higher `conceptualLoad` (a legitimate, independent DNA dimension) to keep them out of Speed Lab's own evidence population, without changing any expected outcome.
- **`expectedOutcomes` on each scenario is for regression/testing purposes only** — the UI always renders the actual `TrainingPlaygroundRunResult` a run produces, never this field.
- **Candidate-level eligibility is shown honestly, within the real contract's limits**: the public `TrainingSystemDiagnostics`/`TrainingOrchestrationDiagnostics` types report AGGREGATE exclusion counts (`excludedMalformedCount`/`excludedIneligibleCount`), never a per-candidate reason — the UI shows exactly that (aggregate counts, plus which single candidate a `status: "selected"` outcome actually picked), and does not attempt to reverse-engineer a per-candidate breakdown by reimplementing any provider's own filter predicate in React.
- **Epistemic-language discipline extends to the fixture catalog's own prose**: `evidenceSummary`/`description` text is regression-tested to avoid claiming proof or an affirmative "confirmed" claim for genuinely candidate-level (calculation/speed/trap) evidence — while "confirmed" remains accurate, and is used, when describing an actual `RepairPlan`, which IS a real confirmed diagnosis per D-006/D-038. This mirrors, at the fixture-authoring layer, the exact distinction D-056 already enforces at the domain layer.
- **No composite score, confidence, motivation, emotion, predicted-ability, or psychological-inference field anywhere in the playground** — enforced by a dedicated regression test scanning every playground source file's identifiers, plus a dependency/import scan forbidding `@prisma/client`, `@ipmat/db`, and any Anthropic/OpenAI SDK reference.
**Consequences:** A new top-level app, 31 new tests (6 scenario-catalog structure, 5 adapter behavior, 15 integration-against-real-domain-contracts incl. full-chain proofs for repair-first/repair-fallback/mixed-evidence/insufficient-evidence/no-eligible-question, 5 regression guards) — 793 across the repo (762 + 31), typecheck/lint/build clean across all 19 workspaces (18 domain/infra + this app). No component-rendering tests exist (not required by the brief; the adapter/domain layer is what's exercised by tests, matching "no domain logic duplicated in UI" — there is no UI logic worth unit-testing beyond what TypeScript's own structural checks already guarantee). Deliberately, explicitly out of scope: 5E-5 or any further training system, the production student UI (`/apps/web`), any API/database integration, any real AI/Anthropic call, any authentication.

## D-058 — Novelty Training (Phase 5E-5) is the FOURTH concrete `TrainingSystemProvider`: exposure-first (not weakness-first) applicability over Question DNA's existing `noveltyLevel` vocabulary, computed ENTIRELY from attempt history — candidate composition has zero effect on `evaluate()` — with a concept-specific standard-exposure baseline and no progression

**Status:** Accepted
**Context:** With Calculation Gym/Speed Lab/Trap Lab (D-054/D-055/D-056) all triggering on some form of *struggling* (accuracy gap, slowness, recurring failure), Novelty Training is the first provider whose trigger is fundamentally different in kind: **exposure**, not weakness. It answers "has this student been deliberately exposed to this unfamiliar presentation style yet" — never "did they do well on it." The read-first inspection found `NoveltyLevel` (`@ipmat/question-engine`: `"standard" | "novel_representation" | "novel_combination" | "novel_context"`) is a real, closed, **authoritative** DNA field — distinct from `TestingMode`'s coincidentally-named `"novel_representation"`/`"represented_differently"` values (a different field, different purpose, never conflated) and from `difficultyDimensions.representationNovelty` (a continuous, `"provisional"`-calibration estimate, D-021, correctly excluded from gating per the same categorical-over-continuous precedent Speed Lab already established for `time_pressured`). `@ipmat/mastery`'s `noveltyHandling` and `@ipmat/adaptive-selection`'s `novelty_gap` are both **accuracy** measures competing in a 10-reason global ranking — genuinely different from an exposure-count signal, never duplicated here.

The design went through two review passes before implementation. The first design mistakenly scoped which novelty levels to consider from `context.candidates` (whichever levels happened to appear in the supplied pool) — corrected before implementation: **applicability must be a property of the student, never of whatever candidates happen to be supplied**, so `evaluateNoveltyTraining()` was redesigned to always evaluate all three authoritative levels from `context.attemptRecords` alone, with candidates mattering only inside `select()`. The same review pass also tightened the exposure-count semantics (0 = "limited prior exposure," 1–2 = "underexposed," 3+ = "sufficient exposure," all keyed off one reused gate, replacing an earlier ambiguous "a single unseen question must not trigger" framing that incorrectly implied count 1 should be excluded) and made the standard-exposure baseline's concept-specificity an explicit, tested invariant.

**Decision:**
- **New package `packages/domain/novelty-training` (`@ipmat/novelty-training`)**, database-free, depending on EXACTLY `@ipmat/training-systems` (the contract) and `@ipmat/mastery` (`MASTERY_CONSTANTS.MIN_OBSERVATIONS_FOR_COMPONENT`, reused for BOTH the baseline gate and the underexposure gate — no second competing threshold). Deliberately does NOT depend on `@ipmat/calculation-gym`, `@ipmat/speed-lab`, `@ipmat/trap-lab`, `@ipmat/adaptive-selection`, `@ipmat/repair-selection`, or `@ipmat/training-orchestration` — verified both by a `package.json` dependency-set assertion and a source-text import scan. **Zero new provisional numeric constants introduced** — the second provider, after Trap Lab, to reach this.
- **The authoritative flow is fixed and tested**: `attempt history → computeNoveltyExposureEvidence() → evaluateNoveltyTraining() → { targetConceptName, targetNoveltyLevel } → selectNoveltyTrainingQuestion() → selected | no_eligible_question`. `evaluateNoveltyTraining()` reads only `context.studentId`/`context.attemptRecords`; `context.candidates` is read for the first time inside `selection.ts`. A dedicated candidate-pool-invariance test proves identical attempt history produces an identical applicability decision and requirement whether `candidates` is empty or richly populated; a source-level regression test (stripping comments first, so the invariant's own doc-comment prose doesn't trip a literal scan) proves `applicability.ts`'s actual code never references `context.candidates`.
- **`computeNoveltyExposureEvidence(studentId, conceptName, attemptRecords)` takes NO `candidates` parameter at all**, and always returns exactly three `NoveltyDimensionExposure` entries (one per `NON_STANDARD_NOVELTY_LEVELS` value), even when a level has zero observed exposure — absence is itself meaningful evidence, never omitted. Only graded (`submitted`, `isCorrect !== null`) attempts count; correctness plays no role in the count (an incorrect attempt on a non-standard-novelty question is still exposure — this is an exposure model, not an accuracy model).
- **Concept-specific baseline invariant, stated and tested explicitly**: `standardExposureCount` for concept X counts only attempts where `question.conceptName === X` AND `question.noveltyLevel === "standard"`. Standard-level activity in any other concept never contributes — a student can simultaneously clear Percentages' baseline while Ratio's stays at zero, and Novelty Training can never target Ratio until Ratio earns its own baseline.
- **Two-gate `evaluateNoveltyTraining()`**: baseline gate (`standardExposureCount >= 3`, per concept) then underexposure gate (`distinctQuestionIds.length < 3` on at least one of the three levels, for a concept that cleared its baseline). Fails closed: `not_applicable` with reason `insufficient_evidence` (no concept clears baseline at all) or `sufficient_novelty_exposure` (baseline cleared, nothing underexposed). Target selection among qualifying `(concept, level)` pairs: globally lowest exposure count, then lexicographic `conceptName`, then lexicographic `noveltyLevel` — one directly-observed integer comparison, never a blend.
- **`NoveltyTrainingRequirement`**: `{ targetConceptName: string; targetNoveltyLevel: "novel_representation" | "novel_combination" | "novel_context" }` — mandatory `targetConceptName` (unlike Trap Lab's optional field; a novelty level is not a portable cross-concept identity the way an error-taxonomy code is). No `targetPatternFamily`/`targetTaxonomyCell`/`targetTransformation`/`requireUnseenCombination`/`requireUnseenRepresentation` fields — none justified as a gating fact; taxonomy-cell identity stays selector-internal, mirroring Trap Lab's own tie-break.
- **Selection (`selection.ts`) is provider-local**, exactly four stages: eligibility (published, structurally valid) → exact concept+noveltyLevel match (binary, no partial credit) → unseen-taxonomy-cell-at-this-level partition preferred, falling back to the full qualifying pool when nothing is unseen (Trap Lab's exact fallback shape) → least exposure, then lexicographic questionId. `NoveltyTrainingProvider.select()` runtime-checks its requirement's shape and returns `status: "error", code: "invalid_context"` otherwise — the one legitimate use of `error`.
- **No progression** — the three novelty levels are peer categories, not an ordered ladder (no dimension analogous to `computationalLoad`/`conceptualLoad` exists to gate stages on); always targeting the globally lowest-exposure `(concept, level)` pair already produces an emergent rotation across all three without any stage machine, the same reasoning Trap Lab already established for itself.
- **Epistemic language**: every `explanation`/`notes` string uses only "limited prior exposure"/"underexposed"/"sufficient exposure" vocabulary, regression-tested (case-insensitive) against "confirmed"/"proven"/"confus"/"anxious"/"confidence"/"intelligen"/`\bability\b`/"predicted"/"psycholog"/"private reasoning" across 4 representative end-to-end scenarios.
**Consequences:** A new domain package, 53 new tests (11 evidence incl. cross-student/cross-concept isolation and `combinesWithConcepts` diagnostic behavior, 13 applicability incl. the candidate-pool-invariance and source-level-invariance proofs, 11 selection, 5 end-to-end provider, 7 compile-time regression guards, 4 epistemic-language, 2 dependency-boundary) — 848 across the repo (793 + 53 + 2 auto-discovered by `domainBoundary.test.ts`), typecheck/lint/build clean across all 20 workspaces. `npm install` was required once more for the new workspace. Two hand-calculated test fixtures were found wrong during implementation (not code bugs) — both forgot that an unlisted `NoveltyDimensionExposure` defaults to a count of zero, which is lower than any explicitly-set count of 1+, changing which `(concept, level)` pair the deterministic tie-break actually picks; fixed by explicitly setting every dimension a test cares about excluding, rather than relying on "just not mentioning it." Deliberately, explicitly out of scope for this unit: any change to `@ipmat/training-orchestration`/`TrainingActionType`, any HTTP/API route, any UI, any AI/ML call, any finer novelty taxonomy, any real-data calibration of the reused thresholds. Pressure/Revision/Mock providers remain unbuilt.

## D-059 — Pressure Training (Phase 5E-6) is explicitly DEFERRED — no package created; the current architecture has no persisted session/sequence/block identity to build a genuinely distinct provider on

**Status:** Accepted (deferral). *(SUPERSEDED — preserved below as the accurate historical record of this decision AT THE TIME. The prerequisite this entry names was built in D-060; Pressure Training itself was subsequently designed and implemented in D-061, the fifth concrete `TrainingSystemProvider`; and it was wired into `@ipmat/training-orchestration` in D-062. Pressure Training is no longer deferred.)*
**Context:** A full design review was conducted for a fourth-and-a-half concrete `TrainingSystemProvider`, "Pressure Training," intended to cover sustained multi-question sequences, section switching, cumulative time constraints, and reduced recovery between questions — deliberately kept conceptually separate from `@ipmat/speed-lab`'s existing, narrower `time_constrained` stage (D-055), which is single-question time efficiency only. The review inspected the actual current contracts and schema (`@ipmat/training-systems`, `@ipmat/speed-lab`, `@ipmat/calculation-gym`, `@ipmat/trap-lab`, `@ipmat/novelty-training`, `@ipmat/training-orchestration`, `@ipmat/attempt`, `@ipmat/mastery`, `@ipmat/autopsy`, `@ipmat/question-engine`, `@ipmat/examiner-lens`, and `packages/db/prisma/schema.prisma`) rather than assuming what data might exist.

**The review established these facts directly from the repository:**
- No `sessionId`/`practiceBlockId` field exists anywhere in `MasteryAttemptRecord`, `AttemptMasteryContribution`, `TrainingSystemContext`, or the Prisma schema.
- No persisted construct groups attempts into an intentional sequence — a repository-wide search for "session"/"sequence"/"block" found `"sequence"` used only to mean "an ordered list" (answer-change history, accuracy history), and `"session"` appearing exactly twice, both marking explicitly future, unbuilt work (a comment in `@ipmat/attempt` and the Prisma schema, both about a not-yet-built `abandoned`-attempt session/timeout close-out trigger).
- No cumulative, block-level time budget exists anywhere — `expectedTimeSeconds` is a per-question field only.
- No session-level switching state exists — there is no mechanism to know whether two attempts belong to the same sustained block, only their bare `finalizedAt` timestamps.
- `AutopsyQuestionContext.sectionName` is **static, per-question exam-section metadata** (e.g. `"Quant"`), authored once when the question was written — it is not a dynamic "the student's session moved into a new section" marker, and it must never be treated as one.
- `finalizedAt` (the only timestamp on an attempt) cannot safely define an intentional training sequence — timestamp adjacency does not distinguish "two questions attempted 90 seconds apart in one sitting" from "two questions that happen to be adjacent in all-time chronological order, attempted weeks apart." Inferring a sequence boundary from it would be an unjustified heuristic.
- `testingModes.includes("time_pressured")` — the one genuinely pressure-adjacent DNA fact that IS safely available — is **already owned by `@ipmat/speed-lab`'s `time_constrained` stage** (D-055).

**Decision:** Attempting to implement Pressure Training now would do one of exactly two things, both rejected: (1) **duplicate Speed Lab** — since the only safely-available signal (`time_pressured` exposure) is already Speed Lab's own gate, a "Pressure Training" built only on that signal would just be a renamed copy of `time_constrained`, not a distinct system; or (2) **invent unsupported session semantics** — deriving "sequence"/"switching"/"sustained block" from raw timestamp adjacency would be exactly the kind of unjustified inference this codebase's own discipline (D-005, D-034, D-035, and every provider's epistemic-boundary regression test) already prohibits for psychological claims, extended here to structural claims about training sessions that the data does not actually represent. **No package is created. No code is written. `packages/domain/pressure-training` does not exist.**

**Minimum future prerequisite, recorded for whenever this is revisited:** a general **Practice Session / Block abstraction** supporting session identity, attempt membership, start/end boundaries, a configured question-count target, a configured cumulative time budget, block-progress state, and — eventually, once multi-section content exists — section/block configuration. **This abstraction must be designed as GENERAL infrastructure, not Pressure-Training-specific plumbing** — it will also be the prerequisite for a future Mock/Simulation system and any other grouped-practice feature, so scoping it narrowly to Pressure Training's own needs would just mean re-doing the same design work again later for Mock.

**Consequences:** No new package, no new tests, no dependency or contract changes. `TrainingSystemContext`, `TrainingActionType`, `@ipmat/training-orchestration`, the `Attempt`/attempt-event schema, and the Prisma schema are all explicitly untouched by this decision. `docs/MASTER_PLAN.md`'s existing "named, not built" status for Pressure Training is preserved, now backed by a documented architectural reason rather than an unexplained placeholder. Designing the Practice Session/Block abstraction itself is explicitly deferred to a separate, future architectural unit — not decided here.

## D-060 — Practice Session / Block Foundation (Phase 5F): a GENERAL `Enrollment -> PracticeSession -> PracticeBlock -> Attempt` grouped-practice abstraction, the exact D-059 prerequisite, implemented alongside the pre-existing, unchanged `Enrollment -> Attempt` (ordinary practice) path — no Pressure Training, Mock, UI, or orchestration wiring

**Status:** Accepted
**Context:** D-059 named a general Practice Session/Block abstraction as the minimum prerequisite before Pressure Training (or a future Mock/Simulation system) could be revisited — explicitly NOT Pressure-Training-specific plumbing. This unit designs and implements exactly that abstraction, in isolation from any concrete future consumer. The design went through four review rounds before implementation:
1. **Round 1 (initial proposal) was rejected**: a single-entity `PracticeBlock` collapsing session and block together fails Mock's own "several ordered sections" architectural test — Mock needs two levels (a session containing several ordered blocks/sections), not one. Two separate entities, `PracticeSession` and `PracticeBlock`, were required instead, alongside the pre-existing, unchanged `Enrollment -> Attempt` path for ordinary practice.
2. **Round 2** resolved completion semantics (always an explicit action, never automatic), attempt membership (exactly ONE authoritative relationship on `Attempt`, never two independently-mutable paths to the same fact), ordering (two explicit, server-assigned dimensions — never inferred from timestamps), database integrity (a structurally-unrepresentable illegal partial-membership state, backstopped by a DB `CHECK`), removed a speculative `configuration: Json?` column that had no concrete use, and precisely traced transaction ownership against the ACTUAL `PracticeLoopService -> @ipmat/attempt domain function -> AttemptRepository` flow rather than an idealized one.
3. **Round 3** resolved `PracticeSession` ownership (`enrollmentId`-only chain, deliberately NOT duplicating `Attempt`'s own redundant `studentId`), the exact Serializable-retry contract (grounded in D-049's precedent, no automatic retry), the exact deletion/historical-integrity policy, and the precise `@ipmat/attempt` request-vs-persisted-state contract boundary — including a self-correction: an initial claim that `AttemptRepository.save()`'s signature "does not change at all" was found imprecise on deeper analysis (there is genuinely no other layer where both halves of a new `blockMembership` pair are simultaneously available) and corrected to one minimal, optional parameter addition.
4. **Round 4 (final micro-revision)** corrected two remaining issues before implementation: (a) an earlier mixed `Cascade`/`Restrict` deletion design depended on an unverified assumption about cascade-ordering interaction between `Attempt`'s existing `Cascade` and the new `PracticeBlock`-side `Restrict` — replaced with a uniform `Restrict` across all three new/touched relationships, so each constraint is independently trivial and no multi-path interaction is ever assumed; (b) an asserted algebraic relationship between wall-clock duration and active solving time ("duration minus solving time equals the aggregate of inter-attempt gaps") is not generally true — a block/session can contain idle time before its first attempt, after its last attempt, or time never captured by `timeSpentSeconds` at all — corrected to three independently-derived, non-summing time measures with no claimed relationship between them.

**Decision:**
- **Conceptual model**: `Enrollment -> PracticeSession -> PracticeBlock -> Attempt`, held alongside the pre-existing, entirely unchanged `Enrollment -> Attempt` path — ordinary, ungrouped practice remains valid and untouched.
- **Two new, database-free domain packages, `@ipmat/practice-session` and `@ipmat/practice-block`, each with ZERO dependencies** (not even on each other, not even on `@ipmat/attempt`) — deliberate siblings, not a hierarchy either package hard-codes knowledge of. `PracticeSessionState`/`PracticeBlockState` mirror `AttemptStatus`'s exact D-035 lifecycle discipline: one non-terminal `"active"` value, `"completed"`/`"abandoned"` both equally final, nothing transitions a finalized session/block back to `"active"` or between terminal states. `PracticeSessionState` deliberately has NO `studentId` field — ownership resolves only through `enrollmentId -> Enrollment.studentId`, a single authoritative chain, unlike `Attempt`'s own redundant `studentId`+`enrollmentId` (justified there by `Attempt`'s demonstrated high-frequency direct-query need, which `PracticeSession` does not share). `PracticeBlockState` has no `studentId`/`enrollmentId` duplicated either — ownership resolves via the parent session. Cross-entity lifecycle checks (session completion needs "no active child block"; block creation needs "parent session is active") take the other package's fact as a plain caller-supplied boolean (`hasActiveBlock`, `sessionIsActive`) — proven by a dedicated dependency-boundary test per package, mirroring every prior provider's own boundary test.
- **Lifecycle**: completion/abandonment is ALWAYS an explicit call (`completePracticeSession`/`abandonPracticeSession`/`completePracticeBlock`/`abandonPracticeBlock`) — reaching `PracticeBlock.targetQuestionCount` never auto-completes anything; `deriveBlockProgress()`'s `targetReached` flag is purely informational, read by nothing that acts on it automatically. Session completion/abandonment refuses (`has_active_block`) unless the caller confirms no child block is still `active`, re-verified INSIDE the same transaction as the status write (never trusting an earlier, separate read). "Terminal session with an active child block" is therefore structurally unreachable as a CONSEQUENCE of this check, not a separately-enforced rule — `@ipmat/practice-block` has no session reference to re-check against by design.
- **Attempt membership — exactly ONE new relationship**: `AttemptState` gains `blockMembership: { practiceBlockId: string; blockSequenceNumber: number } | null` — a single nullable OBJECT, not two independently-nullable fields, making the illegal partial-pair state structurally unrepresentable at the TypeScript level (the same exclusion technique `TrainingSystemSelectionOutcome` already uses for `not_applicable`/D-053). Deliberately NO `practiceSessionId` on `Attempt` — session is always derived via `practiceBlockId -> PracticeBlock.practiceSessionId`, never a second, independently-mutable path to the same fact. `startAttempt()` (`@ipmat/attempt`) is entirely UNCHANGED and stays block-unaware — it always sets `blockMembership: null`; every other lifecycle function (`recordAttemptEvent`/`submitAttempt`/`skipAttempt`/`finalizeAttempt`) preserves whatever value it was given, unread, via its existing object spread. A retry's `blockMembership.practiceBlockId` must equal its `retryOfAttemptId`'s exactly (both null, or both the same block) — checked server-side, fails closed both directions (a block retry saved without a block, and an ordinary retry saved with one, are both rejected).
- **Ordering — two explicit, server-assigned dimensions, never inferred from timestamps**: `PracticeBlock.sequenceNumber` (unique per session, `@@unique([practiceSessionId, sequenceNumber])`) and `Attempt.blockSequenceNumber` (unique per block, `@@unique([practiceBlockId, blockSequenceNumber])`) — both computed as `(current max for the parent) + 1` inside a `Serializable`-isolated transaction, backstopped by the DB-level unique constraints.
- **`AttemptRepository.save()` gains exactly one optional parameter**: `save(state, blockAllocationRequest?: { practiceBlockId: string })`. This is the ONLY place in the entire system a non-null `blockMembership` is ever constructed — always inside `save()`'s existing transaction, for a BRAND-NEW attempt id only (block membership is immutable once a row exists, mirroring `retryOfAttemptId`'s own immutability; a second `save()` call for an existing attempt with `blockAllocationRequest` set, or with a manually pre-populated `blockMembership` and no allocation request, is refused with `PersistenceError("invalid_record")`). It re-verifies the referenced `PracticeBlock` is `active` (throws `PracticeBlockLifecycleError("already_finalized")` otherwise), re-derives and verifies OWNERSHIP (below), allocates the next `blockSequenceNumber`, and checks retry inheritance — all inside the SAME `Serializable` transaction the row write already used (`PrismaAttemptRepository`) or the same synchronous call (`InMemoryAttemptRepository`, sharing the exact same checks against a `practiceBlocks` map keyed by `{status, enrollmentId, studentId}`).
- **AUTHORITATIVE ownership verification (security-fix addendum, post-implementation review)**: the first implementation of `blockAllocationRequest` verified the referenced `PracticeBlock`'s existence/active-status/retry-inheritance but NOT that it actually belongs to the attempt's own `studentId`/`enrollmentId` — a genuine gap letting a caller allocate an attempt into ANY existing active block regardless of ownership. Fixed by re-deriving the block's REAL owning chain from scratch, inside the SAME transaction, every time: `PracticeBlock.practiceSessionId -> PracticeSession.enrollmentId -> Enrollment.{id, studentId}`, then requiring BOTH `enrollment.id === state.enrollmentId` AND `enrollment.studentId === state.studentId`, failing closed with `PersistenceError("ownership_mismatch")` (a new, dedicated `PersistenceErrorCode`, distinct from `invalid_record`/`missing_reference`) otherwise. This check is NEVER skipped because a caller's own claimed `studentId`/`enrollmentId` might "look" consistent — it is always re-derived from the persisted rows, never trusted from the incoming `AttemptState`. No ownership column was added anywhere redundantly: `PracticeBlock` still has no `studentId`, `Attempt` still has no `practiceSessionId` — the authoritative chain remains exactly `Attempt -> PracticeBlock -> PracticeSession -> Enrollment -> Student`.
- **New repository interfaces (`@ipmat/db`), dedicated atomic methods, never a generic overloaded `save()`**: `PracticeSessionRepository.{create, findById, complete, abandon}` and `PracticeBlockRepository.{create, findById, findBySessionId, complete, abandon}` — `complete()`/`abandon()` each re-read the row (and, for sessions, re-count active blocks) INSIDE one fresh transaction and hand both facts to the REAL pure domain functions (`completePracticeSession()`, etc.) as the actual source of truth, the same "domain function decides, repository only supplies a fresh read and applies the result" pattern `PrismaQuestionPublicationRepository.decide()` already established for `decidePublication()` (D-049). `PracticeBlockReader` (`{findById(): Promise<{id, status, enrollmentId, studentId} | null>}`) mirrors `QuestionReader`'s exact role (D-048) — the fast, non-transactional ownership pre-check `PracticeLoopService.startAttempt()` performs before ever calling `save()`; `enrollmentId`/`studentId` are resolved by a real join through `PracticeBlock -> PracticeSession -> Enrollment`, never columns stored redundantly on `PracticeBlock`. This pre-check is explicitly NOT the authoritative check — it exists only to fail fast with a clear error; `save()`'s own transaction re-derives and re-verifies the identical ownership chain from scratch regardless of whether this pre-check ran at all, proven by a dedicated test (`attemptRepositoryBlockMembership.test.ts` test F) that calls the repository directly, bypassing `PracticeBlockReader`/`PracticeLoopService` entirely.
- **`PracticeLoopService.startAttempt()` gains one optional field, `practiceBlockId?: string | null`**, on its existing request shape — omitting it is ordinary, unchanged practice. When supplied: a fast pre-check via the injected (optional, backward-compatible) `PracticeBlockReader` rejects a nonexistent (`practice_block_not_found`), non-`active` (`practice_block_not_active`), or ownership-mismatched (`practice_block_ownership_mismatch` — comparing the block's resolved `enrollmentId`/`studentId` against the request's own) block BEFORE calling the still-unchanged, still block-unaware `startAttempt()`; the id is then passed through as `AttemptRepository.save()`'s `blockAllocationRequest`, which performs the real, authoritative allocation and re-verifies ownership independently.
- **Time semantics — three independently-derived, NON-SUMMING measures, no asserted relationship between them (Round 4 correction)**: `wallClockDuration = endedAt - startedAt` (null while `active`); `activeSolvingTime = sum(Attempt.timeSpentSeconds)` over the caller-supplied attempts (reuses the existing event-sourced field, D-034; an `in_progress` attempt contributes 0); `interAttemptGap`, computed ONLY between consecutive attempts (ordered by `blockSequenceNumber`) within the SAME block, NEVER for ordinary attempts, NEVER inferred from raw timestamp adjacency across different blocks. `startedAt`/`endedAt`/`sessionTimeBudgetSeconds`/`blockTimeBudgetSeconds`/`targetQuestionCount` are the ONLY persisted fields — no cumulative elapsed-time/progress field is ever cached; every derived measure is computed fresh on read (`deriveSessionWallClockDurationSeconds()`, `deriveBlockActiveSolvingTimeSeconds()`, `deriveInterAttemptGapsSeconds()`, `deriveBlockProgress()`), the same "derive, never cache" discipline as mastery/coverage/Examiner-Lens combinations (D-015).
- **Concurrency — `Serializable` isolation, NO automatic retry anywhere in the repository layer (Round 3, grounded in D-049's precedent)**: `runSerializableTransaction()` (`@ipmat/db`) wraps every new/touched transaction (`PrismaPracticeSessionRepository`, `PrismaPracticeBlockRepository`, `PrismaAttemptRepository.save()`) and maps a genuine Postgres serialization failure (SQLSTATE 40001, surfaced by Prisma as `PrismaClientKnownRequestError` code `P2034`) to ONE named, deterministic `SerializationFailureError`, propagated unchanged to the caller — retry policy (bounded attempts, backoff) is explicitly out of scope, deferred to a future HTTP/API layer that doesn't exist yet.
- **Deletion — uniform `Restrict`, no cascade-ordering assumption (Round 4 correction)**: all three new/touched relationships (`PracticeSession.enrollmentId -> Enrollment`, `PracticeBlock.practiceSessionId -> PracticeSession`, `Attempt.practiceBlockId -> PracticeBlock`) are `onDelete: Restrict`. No application code anywhere deletes a `PracticeSession`/`PracticeBlock`. This makes each constraint independently trivial rather than depending on an unverified multi-path interaction with `Attempt`'s own PRE-EXISTING, untouched `studentId`/`enrollmentId -> Student`/`Enrollment: Cascade` — Student/Enrollment deletion now transitively, atomically fails if grouped-practice history exists, as a simple, single-constraint consequence, never a claim about cascade ORDERING. Flagged honestly: this has never been verified against a live database (none has ever been reachable in this environment), same as every other Prisma-level guarantee in this codebase.
- **Migration `0006_practice_session_block`**: two new enums (`PracticeSessionStatus`, `PracticeBlockStatus`, each `active | completed | abandoned`), two new tables (`practice_sessions`, `practice_blocks`), two new nullable columns on `attempts` (`practice_block_id`, `block_sequence_number`), the three `Restrict` FKs above, both `@@unique` constraints, and a hand-written `CHECK` constraint (`attempts_block_membership_pair`, `(practice_block_id IS NULL) = (block_sequence_number IS NULL)`) — Prisma's schema DSL has no CHECK syntax, the same precedent as `questions_published_requires_provenance` in `0001_init` — as the DB-level backstop for what `AttemptState.blockMembership` already makes structurally unrepresentable at the TypeScript level.
**Consequences:** 2 new domain packages (`@ipmat/practice-session`, `@ipmat/practice-block`), 6 new files in `@ipmat/db` (`serializable.ts`, `prismaPracticeSessionRepository.ts`, `inMemoryPracticeSessionRepository.ts`, `prismaPracticeBlockRepository.ts`, `inMemoryPracticeBlockRepository.ts`, `prismaPracticeBlockReader.ts`), extensions to `@ipmat/attempt` (`AttemptState.blockMembership`), `@ipmat/db` (`AttemptRepository.save()`, `PrismaAttemptRepository`, `InMemoryAttemptRepository`, `validation.ts`, `errors.ts`'s new `ownership_mismatch` code), and `@ipmat/practice-loop` (`PracticeLoopService.startAttempt()`, `practice_block_ownership_mismatch` error code), one migration (schema unchanged by the security-fix pass — no new column, no new table), 110 new tests (44 domain-package unit tests across both new packages, 8 dependency-boundary incl. 4 auto-discovered by `domainBoundary.test.ts`, 19 `AttemptRepository` block-membership/retry-inheritance/immutability/ownership tests incl. the security-fix suite's A/B/C/D/F/G/H cases, 8+9 `PracticeSessionRepository`/`PracticeBlockRepository` tests, 3 `runSerializableTransaction` mapping/no-retry tests, 9 static schema/migration-invariant tests, 10 `PracticeLoopService` integration tests incl. the fast-pre-check ownership-mismatch cases B/C/D and the end-to-end retry case G) — 958 tests across the repo (up from 848), typecheck/lint/build clean across all 22 workspaces (`npm install` was required once more for the two new workspaces; `prisma generate` was required once to pick up the new schema models before the Prisma-backed repositories would typecheck). **No live database has ever been reachable in this environment** — the `Restrict`-deletion behavior, the `CHECK` constraint, the `Serializable` conflict detection, the ownership-chain join, and every other Prisma-level guarantee here are correct by construction against documented Postgres/Prisma semantics, exactly like every other Prisma repository in this codebase, but have never been exercised against a real database; this is flagged, not silently assumed. Deliberately, explicitly out of scope for this unit: Pressure Training itself (still deferred, D-059), Mock/Simulation (still future), any HTTP/API route, any UI, any change to `@ipmat/training-orchestration`/`TrainingActionType`, any speculative JSON configuration column (the earlier-proposed `configuration: Json?` was explicitly dropped in Round 2), any redundant ownership column (`PracticeBlock.studentId`/`Attempt.practiceSessionId` were both explicitly rejected — ownership stays derived through the one authoritative chain). One deliberate simplification, noted rather than silently assumed: `completePracticeBlock()`/`abandonPracticeBlock()` do not re-check for in-progress child attempts — not explicitly required by the finalized design, and orthogonal to the session-level "no active child block" invariant this unit does enforce.

## D-061 — Pressure Training architecture specification: resolves the `TrainingSystemContext` block-evidence gap by adding ONE new, optional, generically-named field (`practiceBlocks`) rather than extending `MasteryAttemptRecord`/`AttemptMasteryContribution` — ARCHITECTURE DECIDED **AND IMPLEMENTED** (`@ipmat/pressure-training` now exists, the fifth concrete `TrainingSystemProvider`)

**Status:** Accepted and implemented. *(This entry originally read "architecture only — no code written," reflecting the design-only pass that produced this specification. A separate, later implementation pass built exactly what is specified below — `@ipmat/pressure-training` — with zero architectural deviation from this entry; see the "Micro-specification" section below for the exact, final, implemented formulas/constants, and see D-062 for the follow-up orchestration-wiring decision.)*
**Context:** D-060 built the Practice Session/Block infrastructure D-059 named as Pressure Training's prerequisite. Before implementation could start, a design review (this same pass) found the infrastructure alone is insufficient: `TrainingSystemContext` (`@ipmat/training-systems`, D-053) — the one input every `TrainingSystemProvider.evaluate()` reads — has no way to carry block-grouped evidence. `context.attemptRecords: MasteryAttemptRecord[]` wraps `AttemptMasteryContribution` (`@ipmat/attempt`, constructed only by `toMasteryContribution()`), which has no `blockMembership`-shaped field at all, even though `AttemptState` gained one in D-060 — `toMasteryContribution()` does not surface it. This review read `@ipmat/training-systems`, `@ipmat/mastery`, `@ipmat/attempt`'s `masteryContract.ts`, `@ipmat/training-orchestration`, and `@ipmat/speed-lab`'s full source directly before deciding, rather than assuming.

**Decision — Option B (a new field on `TrainingSystemContext`, sibling to `attemptRecords`), NOT Option A (extending `MasteryAttemptRecord`/`AttemptMasteryContribution`):**

1. **The contract extension.** `TrainingSystemContext` (`@ipmat/training-systems`) gains one new, optional field:
   ```ts
   practiceBlocks?: TrainingPracticeBlockContext[];
   ```
   with a new, purely-primitive restated type (no import of `@ipmat/practice-block`/`@ipmat/db` needed — mirrors exactly how `AutopsyQuestionContext`/`MasteryAttemptRecord` are already restated in this same file rather than imported):
   ```ts
   export interface TrainingPracticeBlockContext {
     practiceBlockId: string;
     /** attemptId, in blockSequenceNumber order — ordering IS the evidence; every id here MUST also appear in context.attemptRecords (correlated by AttemptMasteryContribution.attemptId). A provider correlates by id, never duplicates correctness/timing into this type. */
     attemptIdsInOrder: string[];
     targetQuestionCount: number | null;
     blockTimeBudgetSeconds: number | null;
     /** null while the block is still active — mirrors deriveBlockWallClockDurationSeconds()'s own null-while-active contract exactly. */
     wallClockDurationSeconds: number | null;
     activeSolvingTimeSeconds: number;
     /** One entry per finalized-then-next-started consecutive pair — mirrors deriveInterAttemptGapsSeconds()'s own contract exactly (may be shorter than attemptIdsInOrder.length - 1). */
     interAttemptGapsSeconds: number[];
   }
   ```
   `wallClockDurationSeconds`/`activeSolvingTimeSeconds`/`interAttemptGapsSeconds` are ALWAYS computed by calling `@ipmat/practice-block`'s existing `deriveBlockWallClockDurationSeconds()`/`deriveBlockActiveSolvingTimeSeconds()`/`deriveInterAttemptGapsSeconds()` directly — never a second, independent reimplementation of the same math, and never cached/persisted anywhere new. **Assembling a real `TrainingPracticeBlockContext[]` from live data is explicitly NOT part of this decision or its authorized implementation** — that is a future orchestration-layer concern (there is no such layer yet; `TrainingSystemContext` has never had an "assembly" implementation inside `@ipmat/training-systems` for ANY of its existing fields either).
   Named `practiceBlocks`/`TrainingPracticeBlockContext` deliberately generically (not `pressure`-prefixed) so a future Mock/Simulation provider — named in D-059/D-060 as sharing the same D-060 prerequisite — can read the identical field later without a second contract change.

2. **Why NOT Option A, with evidence:**
   - `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md §6` draws mastery's input boundary at exactly three things: "`mastery` is a pure function of `attempts` + `question DNA` + time." Block/session organization is a fourth, different kind of fact — folding it into `AttemptMasteryContribution` (even as an optional field) blurs a boundary this codebase has held since Phase 1, and every mastery consumer would need to explicitly document "and we ignore this."
   - `AttemptMasteryContribution` is built by exactly ONE function, `toMasteryContribution()` (`@ipmat/attempt`) — extending it touches THREE packages minimum (`@ipmat/attempt`, `@ipmat/mastery`, `@ipmat/training-systems`) and would require a compatibility pass across `@ipmat/mastery`'s own full test suite (`masteryState.test.ts`, `componentDetail.test.ts`, `persistence.test.ts`, `crossPackageIntegration.test.ts`). Option B touches exactly ONE package (`@ipmat/training-systems`) and needs no such pass, because nothing there currently reads block data at all.
   - Ownership meaning is cleaner under Option B: `practiceBlocks` entries are trusted, by the SAME convention every other `TrainingSystemContext` field already uses, to have been pre-scoped to `context.studentId` by whichever caller assembled the context — no new trust boundary, no re-verification needed inside `@ipmat/training-systems`/`@ipmat/pressure-training` (both stay pure and database-free; the one real ownership gate is, and remains, D-060's `AttemptRepository.save()` check, upstream of anything this contract ever sees).
   - Blast radius: Option B is additive-only and structurally invisible (TypeScript optional field) to `@ipmat/mastery`, `@ipmat/adaptive-selection`, `@ipmat/training-orchestration`, and all four existing providers — none of them reference `practiceBlocks`, so none need to change. Confirmed by reading `@ipmat/training-orchestration/src/types.ts` directly: it does not even import `@ipmat/training-systems` today, so `TrainingActionType`/`TrainingOrchestrationInput` are provably untouched by this decision.

**Pressure Training behavior specification (design only — not implemented):**
- **Will measure** (all genuinely block-level, unavailable to any existing provider): (a) reduced recovery — a short median `interAttemptGapsSeconds` within a block; (b) within-block performance degradation — second-half accuracy meaningfully below first-half accuracy across a block's `attemptIdsInOrder`, correlated against `context.attemptRecords`; (c) budget consumption — `activeSolvingTimeSeconds` at or beyond a block's configured `blockTimeBudgetSeconds`.
- **Will NOT measure**: single-question `speedRatio` in isolation (Speed Lab's own, already-owned `time_constrained` signal — D-055); section switching (no multi-chapter content exists in this MVP — a permanent boundary, not a temporary gap); any inferred emotional/psychological state ("stressed," "fatigued" as a claim about the student — D-005/D-034/D-038 discipline unchanged); `testingModes.includes("time_pressured")` as a PRIMARY trigger (remains Speed Lab's territory, D-055).
- **Structural non-duplication guarantee**: `evaluate()` is `not_applicable: insufficient_evidence` unconditionally whenever `context.practiceBlocks` is absent/empty, REGARDLESS of how much single-question Speed-Lab-shaped evidence exists in `context.attemptRecords` — Speed Lab's own types never reference `practiceBlocks` at all, so the two providers are structurally incapable of firing off the same evidence, not merely tested to avoid it.
- **No progression vocabulary proposed for V1** — no real, evidenced graduated dimension was found (mirrors Trap Lab/Novelty Training's own finding); can be revisited later with real data, not invented now.

**MICRO-SPECIFICATION (follow-up pass, eliminating every implementation-time ambiguity a read-only verification pass found in the first cut above):**

- **`PRESSURE_TRAINING_CONSTANTS` — exact values** (own file, `constants.ts`, mirroring `SPEED_LAB_CONSTANTS`'s exact doc-comment discipline: named, provisional, NOT empirically calibrated — no live attempt-timing data has ever existed in this environment to calibrate against, same honest caveat as every other threshold in this codebase):
  - `SHORT_RECOVERY_GAP_SECONDS = 5` (unit: seconds). Chosen as a low, round, defensible value — short enough that a student almost certainly did not pause, re-read, or mentally reset between two attempts, in the same spirit as `SPEED_LAB_CONSTANTS.GOOD_PACE_SPEED_RATIO: 1.0`'s own "round, defensible, uncalibrated" precedent. No repository evidence (no live attempt data, no timing telemetry) justifies a more precise number; this is stated explicitly rather than implied.
  - `DEGRADATION_ACCURACY_DROP_THRESHOLD = 0.4` (unit: a fraction, i.e. a 40-PERCENTAGE-POINT drop — `firstHalfAccuracy - secondHalfAccuracy`, a DIFFERENCE of two proportions, not a proportion itself; deliberately NOT the same kind of quantity as `SPEED_LAB_CONSTANTS.SLOW_FRACTION_THRESHOLD`). Comparison operator: `firstHalfAccuracy - secondHalfAccuracy >= DEGRADATION_ACCURACY_DROP_THRESHOLD`. Chosen deliberately large/conservative BECAUSE the minimum qualifying block size (3 total attempts) can produce a half as small as 1 graded attempt (see below), where accuracy swings between 0%/50%/100% are common by pure chance — a small threshold would trigger on noise. This is a disclosed, honest limitation of the V1 threshold at the floor block size, not a hidden gap — the same "provisional, not yet calibrated, disclosed" character every other threshold in this codebase already has (D-021's `difficultyCalibrationStatus`).
  - Budget consumption needs no new constant — `activeSolvingTimeSeconds >= blockTimeBudgetSeconds` is definitional, not a judgment call.

- **Median (`interAttemptGapsSeconds`) — exact deterministic algorithm**: copy the array (`[...values]`, NEVER mutate the caller's array or assume it is pre-sorted — it is in attempt-sequence order, not magnitude order), sort ascending, then: empty array → `null` (no gap evidence — the block contributes nothing to the reduced-recovery dimension, but is NOT excluded from the other two dimensions on this basis alone); one value → that value; odd length → the middle element after sorting; even length → the arithmetic mean of the two middle elements after sorting (same "population stddev, mean-of-middle-two" register `@ipmat/mastery`'s own `stdDev()`/`mean()` helpers already use). Non-finite (`!Number.isFinite`) or negative values anywhere in the array make the ENTIRE containing `TrainingPracticeBlockContext` invalid (see Block Evidence Validation below) — median is never computed over a partially-invalid array, and invalid values are never silently dropped or clamped.

- **Within-block first-half/second-half split (`attemptIdsInOrder`) — exact rule**: `half = Math.floor(attemptIdsInOrder.length / 2)`; first half = the first `half` ids, second half = the LAST `half` ids. For an ODD-length block, the single middle attempt belongs to NEITHER half (excluded from both — never arbitrarily assigned to one side, and never double-counted). The two halves therefore never overlap by construction. Minimum block size for the degradation dimension to be computable at all is effectively 2 attempts (`half = 1` on each side); the qualifying-block-size floor of 3 (below) means the smallest REAL half in practice is exactly 1 graded attempt per side — the noise caveat the threshold value above already discloses. If `half === 0` (a block with 0 or 1 attempts — cannot occur for a qualifying block, since qualifying requires >= 3, but stated for completeness), the degradation dimension is `null`/not computable for that block.

- **Accuracy — exact numerator/denominator/qualifying rule**: an attempt QUALIFIES for the accuracy computation iff, correlated via `context.attemptRecords[i].contribution.attemptId`, its `contribution.status === "submitted"` AND `contribution.isCorrect !== null` (the SAME "submitted, graded" floor `@ipmat/mastery`'s own `accuracyStability.sequence`/`meanAccuracy` and `@ipmat/speed-lab`'s `isEligible()` both already use). Skipped and abandoned attempts NEVER count in the accuracy numerator or denominator (both have `isCorrect === null` by construction — D-034/D-035 — so the same "graded only" filter excludes them without a separate special case). Hint-assisted attempts DO count (deliberately, unlike Speed Lab's hint-free requirement — hint usage under sustained sequence pressure is itself part of the degradation story this dimension exists to observe, not noise to filter out). Denominator per half = count of QUALIFYING attempts in that half (not the half's raw attempt count); numerator = count of qualifying attempts with `isCorrect === true`. If a half has ZERO qualifying (graded) attempts, that half's accuracy is `null` and the degradation dimension is `null`/not computable for that block (fails closed — never treated as `0%` or `100%`, mirroring `MasteryComponentMeasures`' own "`null` means insufficient data, never a coerced `0`" discipline, D-043).

- **Qualifying attempt** (one exact rule, used ONLY for the accuracy/degradation dimension): `contribution.status === "submitted" && contribution.isCorrect !== null`.

- **Qualifying block** (one exact rule, used for the evidence-sufficiency count): a `TrainingPracticeBlockContext` with `attemptIdsInOrder.length >= 3` AND that PASSES Block Evidence Validation (below) in full AND has an attributable concept (below). A block failing either condition contributes to NOTHING — not the qualifying-block count for any concept, not any of the three trigger dimensions — and never throws or halts evaluation of other blocks.

- **Concept attribution — exact rule, replacing "overwhelmingly share"**: for a block that has passed validation, resolve `context.attemptRecords[i].question.conceptName` for every id in `attemptIdsInOrder`, and count occurrences per concept name. The block is attributed to a concept ONLY if that concept holds a STRICT MAJORITY — `count > attemptIdsInOrder.length / 2` — of the block's attempts. A strict majority is used specifically because it is structurally TIE-FREE (two different concepts can never both exceed 50% of the same total), so no separate tie-break rule is needed.
