If no concept reaches a strict majority, the block has NO attributed concept: it is IGNORED for concept-scoped evidence (contributes to no concept's qualifying-block count or dimensions) — this does NOT make the whole evaluation `insufficient_evidence`; every OTHER concept's evidence is entirely unaffected. Under the current one-chapter MVP, every real block's attempts share exactly one concept (100%), so this rule is a conservative no-op today, exactly as the original design note observed — it is now precise rather than descriptive.

- **Block Evidence Validation — exact fail-closed pipeline** (applied to every `TrainingPracticeBlockContext` BEFORE it may contribute to anything; ANY failure excludes that block entirely — from every dimension AND from the qualifying-block count — and never throws, never partially-uses a block, never crashes evaluation of the remaining blocks):
  1. `attemptIdsInOrder.length === 0` → invalid.
  2. Duplicate ids within `attemptIdsInOrder` → invalid.
  3. Any id in `attemptIdsInOrder` has no matching `attemptId` among `context.attemptRecords` → invalid (an unresolvable correlation — this block's evidence cannot be trusted at all).
  4. Ordering: among the ids that DO resolve and have a non-null `contribution.finalizedAt`, their timestamps must be non-decreasing in `attemptIdsInOrder` order → invalid otherwise (the claimed sequence contradicts the attempts' own recorded timeline).
  5. `interAttemptGapsSeconds`: every element must be finite and `>= 0`, and `interAttemptGapsSeconds.length <= attemptIdsInOrder.length - 1` (mirrors `deriveInterAttemptGapsSeconds()`'s own "omitted, never zero-filled, when the earlier attempt was never finalized" contract — MORE entries than that upper bound is invalid) → invalid otherwise.
  6. `activeSolvingTimeSeconds`: must be finite and `>= 0` → invalid otherwise.
  7. `wallClockDurationSeconds`: if non-null, must be finite and `>= 0` → invalid otherwise. Null is always valid (block still `active`) — and this field is NOT used by any V1 trigger formula at all (reserved for future diagnostics/notes only), so a null value never blocks evaluation of any dimension.
  8. `blockTimeBudgetSeconds`: if non-null and NOT finite-and-positive (i.e. `NaN`/`Infinity`/`<= 0`), the BUDGET dimension only is skipped for this block (treated identically to `null`) — this does NOT invalidate the block as a whole; the reduced-recovery and degradation dimensions are computed independently of budget validity.
  9. `targetQuestionCount` is not validated or used by any V1 trigger formula — reserved for future diagnostics only.

- **Budget consumption — exact rule**: only blocks with a validly-configured budget (`blockTimeBudgetSeconds` finite and `> 0`, per validation step 8 above) participate in this dimension; all others simply don't contribute to it (not excluded from the block as a whole). Exact comparison: `activeSolvingTimeSeconds >= blockTimeBudgetSeconds` (`>=`, "at or beyond budget," not `>`). `wallClockDurationSeconds` plays NO role in this comparison. `activeSolvingTimeSeconds` MAY exceed the budget with no cap and still triggers (no clamping). An `active` (not yet `completed`/`abandoned`) block — `wallClockDurationSeconds === null` — CAN trigger this dimension: nothing in the comparison requires the block to be finished, and catching over-budget pressure while a block is still in progress is the most actionable case, not an excluded one.

- **Applicability reason codes** (own const array, mirroring `NOVELTY_TRAINING_NOT_APPLICABLE_REASONS`'s exact style):
  ```ts
  export const PRESSURE_TRAINING_NOT_APPLICABLE_REASONS = ["insufficient_evidence", "sufficient_blocks_no_pressure_detected"] as const;
  ```
  Precedence: `insufficient_evidence` is checked FIRST, across ALL concepts — if NO concept has >= 3 qualifying, attributed blocks (`MASTERY_CONSTANTS.MIN_OBSERVATIONS_FOR_COMPONENT` reused as the block-count floor, same discipline as Trap Lab/Novelty Training), the result is `not_applicable: insufficient_evidence` immediately, without evaluating any trigger dimension. Only once at least one concept clears that floor are the three trigger dimensions evaluated, across every concept that cleared it; if none of those concepts trigger any dimension, the result is `not_applicable: sufficient_blocks_no_pressure_detected`. The `applicable: true` case carries no separate reason string (the shared `TrainingSystemApplicability` type's `applicable: true` branch has no `reason` field at all, only `requirement`/`explanation` — confirmed by direct read of `@ipmat/training-systems/src/types.ts`); the fact of WHICH dimension triggered is carried entirely by `PressureTrainingRequirement.evidencedDimension`.

- **Target-dimension precedence — exact, including the multi-trigger case**: dimension tiers, in this fixed order: `within_block_degradation` (tier 0, most direct behavioral proof) > `reduced_recovery` (tier 1) > `budget_consumption` (tier 2). For EACH qualifying concept, compute whether it has ANY block triggering EACH dimension; the concept's own tier is the LOWEST (highest-priority) tier it has at least one triggering block for — a concept that triggers MULTIPLE dimensions is classified ONLY by its single best (lowest-numbered) tier, and `evidencedDimension` on the resulting requirement reports that ONE tier only, never a list or a blend (matching D-051's "reason codes, never a numeric score" discipline exactly). Final target selection: sort qualifying-and-triggering concepts by (tier ascending, then `conceptName` lexicographic ascending); pick the first.

- **Selection — exact provider-local predicates and tie-break sequence** (restated in full, not "same as siblings"):
  1. Filter `context.candidates` to: `question.conceptName === targetConceptName` AND `validationState === "published"` AND structurally valid (non-empty `patternTaxonomyCellId`, a real `difficultyTier`, a non-empty `testingModes` array — the same minimal well-formedness floor every sibling provider's own "structurally valid" filter already checks).
  2. Partition the filtered pool into "unseen taxonomy cell at this concept" (the candidate's `patternTaxonomyCellId` does not appear among this student's own attempt history at `targetConceptName`, derived from `context.attemptRecords`) vs. "seen" — prefer the unseen partition; if it is empty, fall back to the full filtered pool (the exact Trap Lab/Novelty Training fallback shape).
  3. Within the chosen partition, compute per-question exposure count (count of `context.attemptRecords` entries for that exact `questionId`, this student) and prefer the LOWEST count.
  4. Final, always-decisive tie-break: lexicographic `questionId` ascending.
  `PressureTrainingRequirement extends TrainingRequirement`: `{ targetConceptName: string; evidencedDimension: "reduced_recovery" | "within_block_degradation" | "budget_consumption"; notes: string[] }` — no numeric target fields (mirrors Speed Lab's explicit exclusion of a "target speed ratio": the evidenced dimension is a fact about past attempts, not a property of an unattempted candidate).

- **Worked examples** (small, deterministic, using the exact constants/formulas above):
  1. *Reduced recovery triggers*: block with 3 attempts `[a1,a2,a3]`, `interAttemptGapsSeconds=[3,4]` → sorted `[3,4]`, even length, median = `(3+4)/2 = 3.5`; `3.5 < 5` → reduced-recovery triggers. Halves: `half=1`, first=`[a1]`, second=`[a3]` (a2 excluded); if both a1/a3 are correct, accuracy drop = `1.0 - 1.0 = 0` → degradation does NOT trigger. No budget configured → budget dimension not evaluated. Net result for this block: `reduced_recovery` only.
  2. *Degradation triggers*: block with 5 attempts `[a1..a5]`, `half=2`, first=`[a1,a2]` both correct (accuracy 1.0), second=`[a4,a5]` both incorrect (accuracy 0.0), a3 excluded; drop = `1.0 - 0.0 = 1.0 >= 0.4` → degradation triggers. Gaps all `>= 5` → reduced-recovery does not trigger.
  3. *Budget consumption triggers*: `blockTimeBudgetSeconds=300`, `activeSolvingTimeSeconds=310` → `310 >= 300` → triggers, independent of gaps/accuracy.
  4. *Does NOT qualify (sufficient evidence, no trigger)*: a concept with exactly 3 qualifying, attributed blocks, each with median gap `>= 5`, accuracy drop `< 0.4`, and no configured budget → `not_applicable: sufficient_blocks_no_pressure_detected`.
  5. *Insufficient evidence*: only 2 qualifying blocks exist for the only concept with any block evidence at all (`2 < 3`) → `not_applicable: insufficient_evidence`.
  6. *Malformed evidence failing closed*: `attemptIdsInOrder=[a1,a2,a1]` (duplicate `a1`) → fails Block Evidence Validation step 2 → the entire block is excluded from every dimension and from every concept's qualifying-block count; evaluation of every OTHER block proceeds unaffected, and no exception is thrown.

**Package boundary (for the next implementation prompt):** new `packages/domain/pressure-training` (`@ipmat/pressure-training`), depending on EXACTLY `@ipmat/training-systems` + `@ipmat/mastery` (for `MASTERY_CONSTANTS.MIN_OBSERVATIONS_FOR_COMPONENT` reuse only) — same two-dependency shape as Novelty Training. The ONE file outside this new package that the implementation is authorized to touch is `packages/domain/training-systems/src/types.ts` (the additive `practiceBlocks`/`TrainingPracticeBlockContext` field). Nothing else — not `@ipmat/mastery`, not `@ipmat/attempt`, not `@ipmat/training-orchestration`, not `@ipmat/db`, not any existing provider.

**Test plan (for the next implementation prompt):** block-evidence derivation unit tests (median gap incl. empty/single/odd/even/non-finite/negative inputs, half-split accuracy incl. odd-length middle-exclusion and zero-qualifying-attempts-in-a-half, budget-ratio comparison, id-correlation, every one of the 9 Block Evidence Validation steps individually, concept-attribution incl. the no-strict-majority case); applicability tests (`insufficient_evidence`, each of the three triggering dimensions individually including the multi-trigger-same-concept tier-precedence case, `sufficient_blocks_no_pressure_detected`, deterministic cross-concept tie-break, all 6 worked examples above reproduced as literal fixtures); selection tests (unseen-cell partition + fallback, exposure tie-break, questionId tie-break); end-to-end provider tests via `runTrainingSystemProvider()`; a dedicated Speed-Lab-non-duplication test (identical `attemptRecords`, no `practiceBlocks` → Pressure Training `not_applicable` while Speed Lab may be `applicable`); a FULL regression run of `@ipmat/mastery`, `@ipmat/calculation-gym`, `@ipmat/speed-lab`, `@ipmat/trap-lab`, `@ipmat/novelty-training`, `@ipmat/adaptive-selection`, `@ipmat/training-orchestration`'s EXISTING test suites, unmodified, proving the additive field changes nothing; dependency-boundary tests (own package + auto-discovered by `domainBoundary.test.ts`); an epistemic-language regression test over every explanation/notes string, same pattern as every sibling.

**Explicitly out of scope, proven not asserted:** section switching (no multi-chapter content — permanent MVP boundary); any HTTP/API route or UI (none exists anywhere in this repo to extend); orchestration wiring (`@ipmat/training-orchestration/src/types.ts` does not import `@ipmat/training-systems` today — confirmed by direct read, so zero change is required there for this unit); any live DB flow (context assembly from real persisted data is a distinct, later, not-yet-designed orchestration concern); any AI call; Mock/Simulation (undesigned; the new field is deliberately generic so Mock can reuse it later, but Mock itself is a separate future unit); `TrainingActionType` (a new `"pressure_training"` value would only be needed once real orchestration wiring happens — a later, separate unit, not this one).
**Consequences:** *(Updated after implementation — see the status note above.)* This design was implemented EXACTLY as specified, with zero deviation: one additive field on `@ipmat/training-systems/src/types.ts` (`practiceBlocks`/`TrainingPracticeBlockContext`), and one new domain package, `@ipmat/pressure-training`, with the exact dependency/file boundary above (`@ipmat/training-systems` + `@ipmat/mastery` only) — nothing else was touched. The two new provisional constants (`SHORT_RECOVERY_GAP_SECONDS = 5`, `DEGRADATION_ACCURACY_DROP_THRESHOLD = 0.4`) were implemented with these exact values, explicitly disclosed as uncalibrated (no live attempt-timing data has ever existed in this environment) rather than presented as measured — consistent with this codebase's "reuse first, invent only when nothing fits, always disclose provisional status" discipline, not a silent scope expansion. 87 tests were added for `@ipmat/pressure-training` itself (43 block-evidence, 11 applicability, 9 selection, 6 provider, 4 epistemic-language, 2 dependency-boundary incl. 2 auto-discovered by `domainBoundary.test.ts`, 2 Speed-Lab non-duplication), plus the pre-existing `@ipmat/training-systems` suite continuing to pass unmodified — 1037 tests across the repo (up from 958), typecheck/lint/build clean across all 23 workspaces. `docs/MASTER_PLAN.md`'s Phase 5E-6 section is marked superseded; the current status lives in Phase 5G (see D-062 for the follow-up orchestration-wiring decision that consumed this provider alongside the other four).

## D-062 — Training Orchestration wiring (Phase 5G): all five concrete `TrainingSystemProvider`s (Trap Lab, Calculation Gym, Speed Lab, Pressure Training, Novelty Training) are consulted by `@ipmat/training-orchestration` as ONE new, generic `training_system_practice` action-type tier — inserted between `targeted_repair` and `adaptive_practice` — in a fixed, named priority order, never a numeric score

**Status:** Accepted and implemented
**Context:** With D-061 completing the fifth concrete `TrainingSystemProvider`, all five providers named across Phase 5E/5F/5G existed, tested, but uncalled by `@ipmat/training-orchestration` (Phase 5D, D-052) — the same "foundation with nothing calling it" state every prior deterministic core was in before its own wiring phase. This unit designed (in a preceding design-only pass) and then implemented the wiring, reading `@ipmat/training-orchestration`'s full existing source (`orchestrate.ts`, `types.ts`, `repairPlanSelection.ts`, `priorExposure.ts`), `@ipmat/adaptive-selection`'s and `@ipmat/repair-selection`'s `types.ts`, and `@ipmat/training-systems`'s contract directly before deciding, rather than assuming. One finding shaped the whole design: `@ipmat/adaptive-selection`'s own `TRAINING_NEED_REASON_CODES` already contains `pressure_gap`, `novelty_gap`, and `speed_weakness` — independent, coarser, mastery-accuracy-based heuristics that predate and have nothing to do with the concrete providers of similar name. This is a real naming collision, documented explicitly below, never resolved or renamed by this unit.

**Decision:**
- **One generic `TrainingActionType` value, `training_system_practice`, NOT five separate per-provider literals** — a deliberate, reasoned departure from D-052/D-053's own doc-comment wording (which anticipated "Calculation Gym, Speed Lab, Trap Lab, Novelty Lab, Pressure Training... named ONLY as future values," implying one literal each). The concrete provider that fired is identified by a new `providerId: string` field on the one new result variant, `TrainingOrchestrationSelectedTrainingSystem` — so a future 6th provider (Mock Simulation, Revision) never requires touching `TrainingActionType` again, only a new entry in the provider registry. Grounded directly in `attemptTargetedRepair()`'s own pre-existing doc comment: "a future additional provider... would follow this exact shape... and slot into the sequence without this function's own logic changing at all."
- **Sequence — repair still first, adaptive still the universal catch-all, ONE new middle tier inserted between them**: `targeted_repair` (unchanged) -> `training_system_practice` (NEW) -> `adaptive_practice` (unchanged) -> `no_action` (unchanged). A confirmed `RepairPlan` remains more certain than any training-system provider's candidate-level evidence (D-005/D-006/D-038's confirmed-vs-candidate distinction applies one layer up), so repair precedes the new tier exactly as it always preceded adaptive.
- **Fixed, named provider priority order — never a numeric score** (requirement explicitly checked against three alternatives — phase/prep-aware, evidence-magnitude-driven, and fixed/named — and fixed/named was the only one that didn't require inventing a cross-provider magnitude comparison the five providers' fundamentally incommensurable evidence types cannot honestly support):
  ```ts
  export const TRAINING_SYSTEM_PROVIDER_PRIORITY_ORDER = ["trap-lab", "calculation-gym", "speed-lab", "pressure-training", "novelty-training"] as const;
  ```
  Ordered by specificity/urgency of the underlying signal — a recurring, specific failure (Trap Lab) > a specific, evidenced accuracy gap (Calculation Gym) > a general pace inefficiency (Speed Lab) > sustained-sequence behavioral evidence (Pressure Training, the newest and most infrastructure-dependent signal) > a deliberate exposure opportunity (Novelty Training, D-058's own "exposure-first, not weakness-first" character — an opportunity, not a problem, mirroring how `@ipmat/adaptive-selection`'s own `difficulty_progression` is its deliberate lowest-priority fallback). Authored policy, not calibrated against real outcome data — the same honest caveat every other priority order in this codebase carries.
- **`attemptTrainingSystems()` (new file, `trainingSystemProviders.ts`) tries each provider, in that order, via `runTrainingSystemProvider()` — the ONLY entry point, never `evaluate()`/`select()` called directly.** Stops at the first `status: "selected"`. Every OTHER outcome (`not_applicable`, `no_eligible_question`, `error`) is recorded in diagnostics and the NEXT provider is tried — a content gap, a mode simply not applying, or one provider's own impossible-execution error never blocks trying the rest, generalizing the pre-existing repair-no_match -> adaptive fallback reasoning to an N-way chain. `toTrainingSystemContext()` maps `TrainingOrchestrationInput` onto `TrainingSystemContext` — the SAME field-for-field restatement `apps/training-playground/src/domain/runScenario.ts`'s own `toTrainingSystemContext()` already performs (a real, working precedent followed, not invented) — and deliberately does NOT forward `activeRepairPlans`.
- **No provider receives `RepairPlan` data, ever** — `TrainingSystemContext` gained no such field, and none should. The repair/training-systems interaction is handled ENTIRELY by orchestration sequencing (repair tried first; if it selects, providers/adaptive are never consulted at all) — never by threading confirmed-diagnosis data into a provider's own applicability decision, which would have widened `@ipmat/training-systems`'s deliberately minimal contract (D-053) for no real benefit.
- **Two new, symmetric, independently-testable decision functions** mirroring the pre-existing `shouldAttemptAdaptivePractice()`'s exact three-branch shape: `shouldAttemptTrainingSystems()` (reuses `TRAINING_ORCHESTRATION_POLICY.ALLOW_ADAPTIVE_FALLBACK_ON_REPAIR_NO_MATCH`, now relocated to gate repair -> training-systems instead of repair -> adaptive directly) and `shouldAttemptAdaptivePracticeAfterTrainingSystems()` (a NEW policy constant, `ALLOW_ADAPTIVE_FALLBACK_ON_NO_TRAINING_SYSTEM_MATCH`, gating training-systems -> adaptive). `shouldAttemptAdaptivePractice()` itself is preserved COMPLETELY UNCHANGED — still exported, still independently correct and tested — simply no longer called from `orchestrateNextTrainingAction()`'s own sequence, since a tier now sits between repair and adaptive.
- **`TrainingOrchestrationDiagnostics` gains four new fields**: `trainingSystemProviderOutcomes` (every provider actually invoked, in priority-tried order, with its RAW unmodified outcome — mirrors `repairOutcome`/`adaptiveOutcome`'s existing "always preserve the raw outcome" discipline), `trainingSystemProviderChosen` (`{providerId, targetConceptName}` — `targetConceptName` is `null`, never a fake value, when the winning provider's own requirement doesn't carry one, e.g. Trap Lab's is optional per D-056), `fallbackPermittedToTrainingSystemsPolicy`, `fallbackPermittedToAdaptiveAfterTrainingSystemsPolicy`. `TrainingOrchestrationSelectedAdaptive` gains `wasFallbackFromTrainingSystems: boolean` alongside its pre-existing, unrenamed `wasFallbackFromRepair` — both can be true simultaneously.
- **`TrainingOrchestrationInput` gains two new optional fields**, `errorTaxonomy?`/`practiceBlocks?`, forwarded unmodified into every `TrainingSystemContext` — the only way Trap Lab's optional enrichment or Pressure Training's required block evidence can ever reach this layer. Both are genuinely new fields; neither existed on this type before this unit.
- **Naming collision, documented not resolved**: `@ipmat/adaptive-selection`'s own `pressure_gap`/`novelty_gap`/`speed_weakness` `TrainingNeedReasonCode`s remain entirely independent of the concrete Pressure Training/Novelty Training/Speed Lab providers — computed from `MasteryStateResult` alone, never reading `practiceBlocks`/exposure counts/block evidence. A dedicated regression test proves mastery-shaped evidence that could drive adaptive-selection's own reason codes leaves the concrete providers `not_applicable` on its own. Reconciling or renaming either vocabulary is an explicit non-goal of this unit.
- **`package.json` gains six new dependencies** — `@ipmat/training-systems` plus all five concrete providers — the FIRST time `@ipmat/training-orchestration` has ever depended on a concrete provider or the shared contract, a real, first-time dependency-direction change fully anticipated by D-053's own doc comment ("Phase 5D would depend on a concrete PROVIDER package later, not on this shared contract directly, though it may end up doing both").
**Consequences:** 10 files changed (`package.json`, `src/types.ts`, `src/orchestrate.ts`, `src/index.ts`, new `src/trainingSystemProviders.ts`, plus 3 new test files and one required literal update in an existing compile-time regression-guard test), zero files touched outside `packages/domain/training-orchestration/**` (verified directly — no provider package, `@ipmat/training-systems`, `@ipmat/mastery`, `@ipmat/attempt`, `@ipmat/db`, Prisma/schema/migrations, UI, API, AI, or `apps/training-playground` was modified; a dedicated test asserts each of the five providers' own `package.json` dependency sets are byte-for-byte unchanged). 29 new tests (9 adapter/registry/sequencing unit tests using injected fake providers for full determinism over the error/fallthrough paths, 17 end-to-end integration tests via real providers incl. each provider selected in isolation, all four adjacent priority-pair orderings, repair-beats-all-providers, all-five-unavailable-falls-to-adaptive, both fallback flags across every relevant combination, and the naming-collision regression, 3 dependency-boundary) — 1066 tests across the repo (up from 1037), typecheck/lint/build clean across all 23 workspaces (unchanged workspace count — no new package, only a new dependency edge). One genuine implementation-time finding, not a design flaw: constructing the "Calculation Gym beats Speed Lab" adjacent-pair test fixture surfaced a real interaction in `@ipmat/calculation-gym`'s own (untouched) progression logic — friction evidence shaped as "100% low-load accuracy, 0% high-load accuracy" always clears the `foundational` stage gate, resolving to `mixed`, which requires a high-computational-load candidate; documented inline in the test as a fixture-construction lesson, not a code defect. Deliberately, explicitly out of scope: section switching, Mock/Simulation (undesigned; the generic `training_system_practice` action type and `practiceBlocks` context field were both deliberately shaped so Mock can reuse them later), any HTTP/API route, any UI, any live DB flow (no caller anywhere assembles real `TrainingOrchestrationInput`/`TrainingSystemContext` fields from persisted data — `orchestrateNextTrainingAction()` still has no caller from a real flow, exactly as it did before this unit), any AI call, resolving the `pressure_gap`/`novelty_gap` naming collision, and any change to `@ipmat/adaptive-selection`, `@ipmat/repair-selection`, or any of the five provider packages.

## D-063 — Training Recommendation Composition (`@ipmat/training-recommendation`) is a new top-level application/domain composition package that reads persisted state and calls `orchestrateNextTrainingAction()` unchanged; verifies enrollment ownership first and re-verifies ownership on every subsequent row a repository returns, rather than trusting either the caller's claim or a scoped query alone

**Status:** Accepted and implemented
**Context:** `@ipmat/training-orchestration` (D-052/D-062) has been complete and tested since Phase 5G, but nothing in the repository has ever called it with real, persisted student state -- every test exercised it with hand-built fixtures. This unit implemented the composition layer whose design (docs/project-memory/37_TRAINING_RECOMMENDATION.md, produced in a prior session) had already been declared implementation-ready: read `Enrollment`/finalized `Attempt` history/confirmed `RepairPlan`s/published questions/concepts/the active `PracticeSession` and its `PracticeBlock`s from `@ipmat/db`, compute fresh mastery via the existing `@ipmat/mastery`, and hand the result to `orchestrateNextTrainingAction()` exactly as its contract requires -- never a second decision engine, never a reimplementation of any selection/ranking logic already owned by `@ipmat/repair-selection`/`@ipmat/adaptive-selection`/`@ipmat/training-systems`'s five providers.

**Decision:**
- **New top-level package, `packages/training-recommendation` (`@ipmat/training-recommendation`)** -- not under `packages/domain/*`, the same reasoning as `@ipmat/practice-loop` (D-047): it depends on persistence ports. Public entry point: `TrainingRecommendationService.recommendNextTrainingAction({ studentId, enrollmentId }): Promise<TrainingOrchestrationResult>`, plus a separately-exported `composeTrainingOrchestrationInput()` for input assembly on its own. Every dependency is injected as a `@ipmat/db` PORT interface (never `@prisma/client` directly), each narrowed with `Pick<...>` to the one read method this layer actually calls -- the type system itself, not just discipline, keeps this package from ever writing anything (it is a snapshot read, never a mutation; no transaction is used).
- **Six new `@ipmat/db` read methods, each with a matching `PrismaXReader`/`InMemoryXReader` implementation**, following the existing `QuestionReader`/`PracticeBlockReader` narrow-port precedent rather than widening an existing repository interface: `EnrollmentReader.findById`, `AttemptHistoryReader.findFinalizedByStudentId`/`findByPracticeBlockId` (a NEW, separate interface -- not added to `AttemptRepository` itself, because `@ipmat/practice-loop`'s existing tests construct `AttemptRepository` as inline object literals that must not be forced to grow methods they don't use), `PracticeSessionRepository.findActiveByEnrollmentId`, `TrainingQuestionReader.findPublishedByExamId`, and `ConceptReader.findWithPublishedQuestionsByExamId`.
- **`ConceptReader` resolves concepts through the persisted `Question.conceptId` relation for this exam's PUBLISHED questions, not `Concept.status`** -- a real, implementation-time finding: every seeded `Concept` row has `status: "curated"`, none is `"published"`, so a status filter would have silently produced zero mastery for every concept. `ConceptStatus`'s relationship (if any) to training/mastery eligibility remains an open question, not resolved by this unit.
- **`TrainingQuestionReader.findPublishedByExamId()` uses `select` (never `include`) so `correctAnswer`/`options`/`solutionSteps`/`groundTruthDerivation`/`body` are never even loaded for this read** -- the same D-020 "independent readers get a narrow, allowlisted view" discipline extended to a new read path. A row whose Question DNA doesn't resolve completely (non-positive `expectedTimeSeconds`, empty `testingModes`, malformed `difficultyDimensions`, an unresolvable trap or combination-concept reference) is excluded by a pure `toTrainingQuestionRecord()` mapper, never coerced.
- **Mastery computation still needs the real answer key** (`toMasteryContribution()`'s required `AttemptQuestionContext`), so the composer loads each distinct attempted question once via the existing D-048 `QuestionReader` -- used ONLY to build that one mapping input, never forwarded into anything passed to `@ipmat/training-orchestration` (`MasteryAttemptRecord` has no answer-bearing field; verified by tests that assert a deliberately unique answer-key string never appears anywhere in the composed input or the returned result).
- **Ownership verification does not stop at the one enrollment check.** `enrollment.studentId === studentId` is the sole authoritative check, run first, before any other read (docs/project-memory/54_SECURITY_AND_OWNERSHIP.md). Every row a subsequent reader returns is THEN re-checked against the already-verified chain -- attempt `studentId`, RepairPlan `studentId`, session `enrollmentId`, block `practiceSessionId`, and each block attempt's `studentId`/`enrollmentId`/`blockMembership.practiceBlockId` -- and a mismatch throws a typed `TrainingRecommendationError("ownership_inconsistency")` rather than being silently filtered out. A row a reader claims is "finalized" but isn't (still `in_progress`, no `finalizedAt`) throws `repository_contract_violation` for the same fail-closed reason.
- **A `PracticeBlock` context is omitted, never trimmed, if it has zero attempts or any attempt absent from the composed `attemptRecords`** (typically a still-`in_progress` attempt, or one whose question fell outside the published/DNA-complete pool) -- `TrainingSystemContext.practiceBlocks` requires every listed attempt id to also appear in `attemptRecords`, and dropping individual ids would misstate that block's own sequence/gap evidence. Only the ONE active session's blocks are ever read (`findActiveByEnrollmentId()` fails closed with `PersistenceError("invalid_record")` if a caller-facing invariant nothing else enforces -- "at most one active session per enrollment" -- is ever violated, rather than silently picking one).
- **`prepPhase` is always `null`; `errorTaxonomy` is omitted** -- unchanged from the design (docs/project-memory/37_TRAINING_RECOMMENDATION.md Section 16), since no function anywhere resolves `Exam.examDateRule` to a concrete date and `CatchUpPlan` has no "active" semantics in the schema.
**Consequences:** New package `packages/training-recommendation/**` (`types.ts`, `ownership.ts`, `readModels.ts`, `compose.ts`, `service.ts`, `index.ts`); six new files in `packages/db/src/repositories/**` (three Prisma readers, three in-memory readers) plus edits to `packages/db/src/repositories/{types,index,validation}.ts` and the existing attempt/session repositories to add the new methods; one new `packages/db` test file. Zero files touched in `packages/domain/training-orchestration`, `repair-selection`, `adaptive-selection`, `training-systems`, or any of the five concrete providers -- confirmed by a dedicated dependency-boundary test asserting `@ipmat/training-recommendation`'s exact approved dependency set and that `@ipmat/db` still does not depend on any orchestration/training-system package. 83 new tests (30 for the new `@ipmat/db` readers, 41 for input composition covering every failure/empty-state/ownership case, 7 service-level end-to-end tests proving the result equals a hand-composed `orchestrateNextTrainingAction()` call, 2 invocation tests, 3 dependency-boundary) -- 1189 tests across the repo, typecheck/lint/build clean across all 25 workspaces. Deliberately, explicitly out of scope (named in docs/project-memory/91_OPEN_BLOCKERS.md): PrepPhase/CatchUp assembly, `errorTaxonomy` composition, persisting freshly-computed `MasteryState`, any HTTP/API route, any UI (`apps/web`'s existing fixture-backed adapter was not touched or switched to this layer), auth, any AI call. Two pre-existing gaps were found (not fixed, since fixing either is a separate design decision): nothing in the schema or `PracticeSessionRepository.create()` enforces "at most one active session per enrollment" (this unit's new reader fails closed on the violation rather than silently picking one); and `Concept.status` is `"curated"` for every seeded concept, never `"published"`, raising an open question about what (if anything) that status is meant to gate.

## D-064 — The first application/API boundary (`@ipmat/practice-api` + `@ipmat/api`): a framework-agnostic application service coordinating `@ipmat/training-recommendation`/`@ipmat/practice-loop`, exposed over a minimal `node:http` transport with no framework and no auth; a new `QuestionContentReader` closes the "no reader exposes safe display text" gap this boundary exposed

**Status:** Accepted and implemented
**Context:** D-063 gave `orchestrateNextTrainingAction()` its first real caller (`@ipmat/training-recommendation`), but nothing in the repository could reach it over a network, and `apps/web` (a Vite SPA with no server of its own) can only ever reach a backend by HTTP, never by importing server-only packages that pull in Prisma. This unit built the smallest boundary that lets `apps/web` eventually consume the real practice system instead of its fixture-backed adapter, without putting any selection/grading logic in the transport or the frontend. No API framework (Express/Fastify/Next.js) existed anywhere in this repository before this unit; none was introduced, since `node:http` was sufficient for a contract this small.

**Decision:**
- **Two new top-level packages, split by responsibility.** `packages/practice-api` (`@ipmat/practice-api`) is the framework-agnostic application service (`PracticeApiService`) — all real coordination, ownership verification, DTO mapping, and error mapping live here, fully unit-testable with no HTTP server involved. `apps/api` (`@ipmat/api`) is a genuinely thin `node:http` transport with zero business logic of its own: every route handler parses a request, calls exactly one `PracticeApiService` method, and serializes the result. Six operations, matching the practice loop end to end: `getNextRecommendation`, `startAttempt`, `submitAttempt`, `skipAttempt`, `getAttemptResult`, `getAutopsyForConfirmation` — exposed as `POST/GET /v1/...` routes.
- **`@ipmat/practice-api` coordinates `@ipmat/training-recommendation` and `@ipmat/practice-loop` — it reimplements neither one's decision logic**, and never imports `@ipmat/repair-selection`/`@ipmat/adaptive-selection`/`@ipmat/training-systems`/any concrete provider/`@prisma/client` (verified by a dependency-boundary test, the same discipline every coordination-layer package in this codebase already follows).
- **A new `QuestionContentReader` (`@ipmat/db`)** — a genuine, previously-missing capability this boundary's own "show the question to the student" requirement exposed: `QuestionReader`/`CanonicalQuestion` (D-048) carries the answer key for server-side grading and has no `body`; `TrainingQuestionReader`/`AutopsyQuestionContext` (D-063) carries Question DNA metadata for selection and also has no `body`. Neither is safe or sufficient to render a question. `QuestionContentReader.findPublishedById()` returns `StudentQuestionRecord` (chapter/concept name, prompt, answerFormat, options, expectedTimeSeconds) — `select`, never `include`, the same D-020 discipline, so `correctAnswer`/`groundTruthDerivation`/`solutionSteps` are never even loaded. Prisma + in-memory implementations, same narrow-port precedent as every other reader in this codebase.
- **The student-safe presentation boundary is a hand-authored translation layer, never a pass-through of internal structures.** `toRecommendationView()` independently re-authors `apps/web/src/adapter/presentation.ts`'s own `toRecommendationViewModel()` translation (this package cannot depend on `apps/web`) — `TrainingOrchestrationResult.explanation`/`providerResult`/`diagnostics` are NEVER forwarded to a client; every `RecommendationView` field is this layer's own authored copy. `AttemptResultView.correctAnswer` is `null` for every status except `"submitted"` — never present before or without a real submission. `PendingAutopsyView` deliberately never reads `modelConfidence` out of persisted evidence (D-038's own rule, re-enforced at this boundary) and only ever surfaces `hypothesisText`/`supportingEvidence` as plain strings. Tests assert a deliberately unique answer-key sentinel and a list of forbidden internal field names (`diagnostics`, `providerResult`, `modelConfidence`, `behaviorSignals`, `actionType`, …) never appear in any response JSON.
- **Two real ownership gaps this boundary closes, neither previously enforced at any layer this boundary calls:**
  1. `PracticeLoopService.startAttempt()` never verified that `enrollmentId` belongs to `studentId` for ordinary (non-block) practice. `PracticeApiService.startAttempt()` closes this by reusing `assertEnrollmentOwnership()` from `@ipmat/training-recommendation` directly — the SAME check that package already uses for its own entry point, never a second, independently-written ownership function.
  2. `AutopsyRepository.findByAttemptId()` has no ownership scoping of its own (a plain id lookup, by design, mirroring `AttemptRepository.findById()`). `getAttemptResult()`/`getAutopsyForConfirmation()` load the attempt first and verify `attempt.studentId === claim.studentId` before reading or returning anything — a cross-student id is rejected, never silently substituted.
  3. `submitAttempt()`/`skipAttempt()` ownership itself is enforced by `@ipmat/attempt`'s own pre-existing `assertOwnership()` — unchanged, just correctly invoked through the claim this boundary builds.
  `now` (which determines `timeSpentSeconds = finalizedAt - startedAt`) is a testing seam only on `PracticeApiService`'s own methods — the HTTP transport (`apps/api/src/server.ts`) never reads a `now` field from any request, so a client can never manipulate a computed duration by supplying a fake timestamp.
- **No authentication exists, and this boundary adds none — D-004 remains open, exactly as before.** `studentId`/`enrollmentId` are accepted as explicit request fields, the identical trust boundary `@ipmat/training-recommendation`/`@ipmat/practice-loop` already have as plain function parameters; this unit verifies the RELATIONSHIP between the two claimed ids (and between an attempt and its claimed owner), never invents a way to authenticate the caller. This is not "production-authenticated" in any sense and must not be described as such.
- **The runtime entry point (`apps/api/src/index.ts`) wires ONLY in-memory dependencies** (`createInMemoryDependencies()`, `wiring.ts`) — no live database has ever been reachable in this environment, and this unit does not change that. `createPrismaDependencies()` (same file) is structurally complete — every `PrismaXRepository`/`PrismaXReader` this boundary needs is wired, including `PrismaAutopsyRepository` — and typechecks, but is never invoked by `index.ts` or exercised against a live database anywhere in this unit; the same "correct by construction, never yet run against a real Postgres instance" stance every `PrismaXRepository` in this codebase already carries.
- **Autopsy support is READ-ONLY and honestly incomplete end to end.** `getAutopsyForConfirmation()` reads a persisted `Autopsy` row via the existing `AutopsyRepository.findByAttemptId()` and maps it to a safe view when one exists and is still `awaiting_confirmation` (`confirmed === null`) — this works today, is tested, and requires no AI call. Two things remain genuinely unsupported by this boundary, for two SEPARATE reasons, neither a shortcut taken here: (1) generating a FRESH hypothesis requires a live AI call (`@ipmat/ai`'s 5th task, `generateHypothesis()`) — explicitly out of scope this unit; (2) applying a confirm/reject/correct RESPONSE requires rehydrating a domain `AutopsyHypothesis` from a persisted `Autopsy` row, and `@ipmat/autopsy` has no such reverse mapper (unlike `RepairPlan`, which has `fromRepairPlanPersistenceRecord()`) — a genuine, pre-existing architectural gap this unit discovered, not created. In the real system today this means `getAutopsyForConfirmation()` will always return `pending: false`, since nothing yet writes an `Autopsy` row outside a test fixture — the same honest "built and tested, nothing populates it yet" pattern this codebase already used for Pressure Training before real block data existed.
**Consequences:** Two new packages — `packages/practice-api/**` (`types.ts`, `errors.ts`, `presentation.ts`, `validation.ts`, `service.ts`, `index.ts`) and `apps/api/**` (`server.ts`, `wiring.ts`, `index.ts`) — plus three new files in `packages/db/src/repositories/**` (`PrismaQuestionContentReader`, `InMemoryQuestionContentReader`, the new `QuestionContentReader`/`StudentQuestionRecord` types) and one new `@ipmat/db` test file. Zero files touched in `apps/web` or any domain/selection package — confirmed by a dependency-boundary test and by direct inspection. 52 new tests (5 `@ipmat/db` reader tests, 36 `@ipmat/practice-api` service tests covering every case named for this unit including answer-key/internal-diagnostics leakage and safe error mapping, 2 dependency-boundary, 9 real HTTP-level integration tests in `apps/api` that boot an actual server on an ephemeral port) — 1241 tests across the repo, typecheck/lint/build clean across all 27 workspaces. The dev server was also booted directly via `tsx` (never only through the test runner) and confirmed to respond correctly over a real socket. Deliberately, explicitly out of scope: any change to `apps/web` (its fixture-backed adapter is unchanged; the next unit is free to replace it with a thin fetch-based implementation of the same `TrainingRecommendationAdapter` shape, since `RecommendationView`/`StudentQuestionView`/`AttemptResultView` were deliberately shaped to match it field-for-field); autopsy hypothesis generation/confirmation (see above); any live database connection; any authentication; any change to `@ipmat/training-recommendation`, `@ipmat/training-orchestration`, or any selection engine.

## D-065 — Phase 3.1 (first adaptive layer): four named "recent attempt" reasons inside `@ipmat/adaptive-selection`, over the existing persisted attempt records — not a new engine, model, or storage

**Context.** After Phase 2 the recommendation was effectively coverage-first: the existing adaptive reasons are driven by mastery measures that stay `null` below `MIN_OBSERVATIONS_FOR_COMPONENT` (3), so one or two attempts changed nothing. The product needs the next recommendation to react to demonstrated performance without pretending to understand the student.

**Decision.** Add four reason codes — `recent_incorrect`, `recent_skip`, `recent_slow`, `recent_correct_on_pace` — to `@ipmat/adaptive-selection`, derived from the student's single most recent finalized attempt (outcome, verdict, server-derived time vs expected time using the EXISTING 1.3 per-attempt ratio, plus Question DNA), read from the `attemptRecords` the recommendation service already supplies. Priority: multi-attempt measured weakness > incorrect/skip/slow > coverage and other gap reasons > progression > `recent_correct_on_pace` (lowest: a correct on-pace answer is not a need). The just-attempted question is never immediately re-served from any bucket while an alternative exists. Student copy is fixed, observation-only text in `practice-api`'s presentation mapping. Nothing is stored; no schema, migration, API contract or frontend change; no new threshold; no confidence/emotion/ability inference.

**Alternatives rejected.** A new scoring/ML model or embedding layer (out of scope, opaque, uncalibrated); storing a per-student "adaptive state" (would drift from the attempt record — recommendations stay a pure function of persisted attempts, so any process makes the same choice); ranking `recent_correct_on_pace` above coverage (changed four established tests' meaning for no product need — demoted instead); claiming a diagnosis from one attempt (that is Autopsy's job, and only once the student confirms it — D-006).

**Consequences.** Two Unit 8 real-database tests that asserted a full coverage tour under deliberately wrong answers/skips were updated to practice with correct answers, because those outcomes now (intentionally) steer the recommendation; this is documented in PHASE_3_ADAPTIVE_PRACTICE.md. The rules are authored policy over a difficulty ordering already labelled provisional (D-021); they have not been calibrated against learning outcomes.

## D-066 — Phase 3.2 (accumulated adaptive evidence): fix the permanence of `repeated_error` (current run, not all-time longest), expose descriptive accumulated-evidence facts, and give the accumulated reasons truthful observation-only student copy — no new mastery system, threshold, reason code, or storage

**Context.** Phase 3.1 reacts to the latest attempt. The multi-attempt reasons (accuracy/speed/repeated-error/progression) already existed and read the recomputed-per-request `@ipmat/mastery` aggregates behind one shared minimum-observation rule (3). Probing them on the real development DNA showed they mostly worked, with three defects: (1) `repeated_error` used `longestIncorrectStreak`, the longest run EVER, so two early misses kept a concept `repeated_error` — the second-highest priority — forever, including right after a correct answer; (2) the accumulated reasons had no student copy, so students were told a question was chosen for "coverage" when it was chosen for repeated incorrect answers; (3) nothing exposed the counts behind an accumulated decision.

**Decision.** `repeated_error` now uses the CURRENT run of consecutive incorrect graded answers, derived from the existing ordered `accuracyStability.sequence` (mastery itself is unchanged and still reports the all-time value). `AdaptiveSelectionResult` gains `accumulatedEvidence` (counts/ratios for the selected concept: graded attempts, incorrect count, trailing streak, speed observations and mean ratio, highest demonstrated tier). The progression rule is refactored into one function shared by the decision and its explanation. `practice-api` maps the accumulated reasons to fixed sentences built from those real numbers, falling back to the neutral coverage copy when a fact is missing. Priority is unchanged and documented: multi-attempt measured reasons > recent problem reasons > coverage/exposure gaps > progression > recent-correct. No new threshold, minimum, reason code, table, or stored state; every reason stays a pure function of persisted attempts.

**Alternatives rejected.** A second mastery/"accumulated state" model (competing source of truth, drift); time-decayed or windowed statistics (a new policy needing calibration data that does not exist); changing mastery's speed computation to exclude skipped attempts (a Phase 5B semantic, out of scope — recorded as a limitation); a numeric or labelled "struggling" state (inferring what the evidence cannot support, D-005).

**Consequences.** Recommendations recover as later answers accumulate instead of being pinned by an old streak. The copy states only observed counts; accumulated evidence remains evidence of performance, not a diagnosis — any "why" is the later Autopsy layer's job and only a student's own confirmation can make it a diagnosis (D-006). Thresholds remain provisional authored policy (D-021) and the development pool is too small to claim calibration.

## D-067 — Phase 3 Unit 3 (trend-aware adaptive evidence): a fixed 3-attempt recent window vs. the earlier graded attempts, exposed as descriptive `trendEvidence` and two change-reactions — no trend score, no decay, no stored state

**Context.** Units 1 and 2 react to the latest attempt and to whole-history aggregates. Whole-history accuracy never forgets: w w w c c c (mean 0.5) kept raising `accuracy_weakness` after three consecutive correct answers, and a strong history followed by a decline was indistinguishable from a history with a few errors. The repository already had the ordered graded correctness sequence and the current-run helper, but no recent-vs-earlier comparison.

**Decision.** Add `trendEvidence.ts` to `@ipmat/adaptive-selection`: the last `TREND_RECENT_WINDOW` (3) graded attempts on a concept vs. all graded attempts before them, classified as `improving` / `deteriorating` / `persistent_difficulty` / `sustained_success` / `null` using the EXISTING 0.6 and 0.8 accuracy thresholds, plus `currentStreak`/`previousStreak`. Skipped/abandoned attempts are not graded (they neither join a window nor break a run); order is `finalizedAt` then `attemptId` (mastery's own sort got the same tie-break). Two reasons react to change (`recent_deterioration`, `recent_improvement`); while the trend is `improving` the whole-history mean stops raising `accuracy_weakness` (the counts stay visible in `accumulatedEvidence` and in the copy). `improving` requires all 3 recent answers correct, so one temporary success is never read as recovery. Student copy states only counts.

**Alternatives rejected.** A composite "trend score" or exponential decay (opaque, needs calibration data that does not exist); a second mastery/trend store (drift from the attempt record); a stored streak counter (process-local/derived state); letting one recent success erase accumulated evidence; inferring why performance changed (that is Autopsy, and only a student's confirmation could make it a diagnosis — D-006).

**Consequences.** Recommendations recover when performance recovers and notice decline after strong history, reconstructed from persisted attempts by any process. Trend evidence describes changes in observed performance; it does not diagnose the student. Window and thresholds are provisional authored policy (D-021); speed trend is not built; the development pool is too small to claim calibration.

## D-068 — Phase 3 Unit 4 (adaptive question selection): a staged, lexicographic selection policy — global no-repeat, a difficulty-fit stage, cell-level coverage — with no composite score

**Context.** A candidate's bucket was decided by its highest reason alone, and a reason says nothing about difficulty or breadth. Inspection found: a student failing at one tier could be served a harder question (accuracy/repeated-error reasons are concept-wide); a student who had demonstrated a tier was served a basic never-seen question because `coverage_gap` outranks `difficulty_progression`; the no-immediate-repeat rule only applied inside the winning bucket; coverage ignored the taxonomy cell and ranked before difficulty fit; repair and the training-system providers could re-serve the just-attempted question.

**Decision.** Keep the reason priority and bucketing, and add explicit stages around them: (1) the just-attempted question is removed from the whole eligible pool when any other eligible candidate exists, with a reported sole-candidate fallback (`repeatFallback`); (2) a difficulty-fit stage (`selectionFit.ts`) that removes remediation reasons from candidates harder than the last answered tier and exploration reasons from candidates off the one-step-up band of a progression-ready concept — each only when an alternative exists, every adjustment returned (`difficultyFitAdjustments`), never touching `repair_priority` or the recent-attempt reasons; (3) within the winning bucket, ordered lexicographic ranking: difficulty fit > family exposure > taxonomy-cell exposure (new, from the same attempt records) > question exposure > easier first > `questionId`. The orchestrator withholds the just-attempted question from the repair and provider tiers when another candidate exists.

**Alternatives rejected.** A weighted composite score (opaque, uncalibrated — the architecture deliberately avoids it); moving overuse avoidance across buckets (would let novelty defeat an active need); merging provider logic into adaptive selection (providers stay separate; only the candidate pool they receive changes); a "readiness" estimate of the student (an observable rule only: a demonstrated tier with no contradicting trend or latest answer).

**Consequences.** The right question can win for the right reason, and every adjustment is explainable and reproducible from persisted state. The stage order, the one-step-up band and "last answered tier" as the remediation reference are authored, provisional policy (D-021); the development pool is too small to claim calibration. No new reason codes, student copy, persistence, schema or API change.

## D-069 — Phase 3 Unit 5 (hardening and validation): close Phase 3 at technical + policy validation; fix the determinism and policy gaps the audit found; keep calibration explicitly future work

**Context.** Phase 3 (recent, accumulated, trend evidence and staged selection) needed an audit against its own documentation before closure. The development pool (3 questions, 1 concept, 1 chapter) cannot support any statistical claim.

**Decision.** Audit 24 specific questions, fix what they found, and add a validation framework instead of any new intelligence: (1) determinism fixes — recent-evidence ties ordered by the shared `finalizedAt`-then-`attemptId` order; repair-plan choice made NaN-safe with a complete tie-break chain; mastery's comparator made NaN-safe; contradictory duplicate candidate ids excluded (exact duplicates collapse); (2) one policy gap — when nothing sits at a progression-ready concept's target tier, its demonstrated tier is the exploration band; (3) internal explanations reworded as observations; (4) the obvious quadratic work removed (per-concept fit summaries, single-pass trend grouping, per-concept cell sets); (5) fixed-seed generated-scenario tests (400 selection, 400 orchestration) asserting the policy invariants, a reason-by-reason consistency matrix, real-Postgres scenarios on the real pool plus a clearly labelled synthetic TEST DATA pool (unpublished again afterwards), and a real-browser run. A three-level vocabulary is fixed: technical validation (code follows its rules), policy validation (rules behave consistently in controlled scenarios), calibration (real outcomes support the thresholds) — only the first two are claimed.

**Alternatives rejected.** Computing "metrics" from the 3-question pool (would be fake evidence); adding a score, model, or hidden state to "improve" selection; caching or restructuring for hypothetical scale; changing Phase 2 code to silence one observed concurrency-test flake.

**Consequences.** Phase 3 closes with documented, tested invariants and explicit fallbacks. Window, thresholds, tier order, the stage order and the one-step-up band remain provisional authored policy; the future calibration measures are defined in PHASE_3_ADAPTIVE_PRACTICE.md but none has been computed. Phase 4 (Question Autopsy / Repair) has not started.

## D-070 — Phase 4 Unit 1 (Autopsy evidence surfaced): an observation-only `ObservationEvidence` derived on demand from the persisted attempt, with explicit observed / derived / unknown sourcing — no diagnosis, no storage

**Context.** The Autopsy domain already derives signals and even a candidate error category, but nothing in the real student flow shows a student what was actually recorded about an attempt. Phase 4's ordering (observation → evidence → hypothesis → confirmation → diagnosis → repair) needs the first step surfaced without pre-empting the rest.

**Decision.** Add `buildObservationEvidence()` to `@ipmat/autopsy`, reusing `AttemptAutopsyEvidence`, `AutopsyQuestionContext`, `HistoricalAttemptRecord` and `deriveBehaviorSignals()`; it deliberately excludes the candidate error category, the error taxonomy and the answer key. Every field is declared `observed` or `derived` (`OBSERVATION_FIELD_SOURCES`); anything unavailable is `null` and listed in `unknown`; confidence, intent, reasoning and working steps are listed as `never_collected` with no field. **Answer changes are unknown unless ≥ 2 selections were recorded**, because the real flow records one selection at submission — reporting 0 would state an observation the system never made. `@ipmat/training-recommendation` assembles the evidence from persisted state (ownership first; history = the attempts finalized before this one), `@ipmat/practice-api` maps it to a student-safe view with fixed sentence templates and refuses in-progress attempts, `apps/api` exposes `GET /v1/attempts/:id/evidence`, and the result screen shows it as a supplementary card. Nothing is stored; nothing is written.

**Alternatives rejected.** Reusing `buildAutopsyOutput()` wholesale (it includes a candidate error category, a diagnosis-adjacent claim); storing the evidence in a new table (it is a pure function of persisted rows, and a stored copy could drift); reporting "0 answer changes" from a single recorded selection; adding UI event capture in this unit (new capability, not asked); an LLM to phrase the observations (fixed templates are auditable and cannot drift).

**Consequences.** A student can see exactly what was recorded, reproducibly, with no claim about why. The evidence object is the intended input for Unit 2's hypothesis. Limitations (unobserved answer changes/hints, page-load time inside elapsed time, unbounded history) are documented in PHASE_4_AUTOPSY_REPAIR.md.

## D-071 — Phase 4 Unit 2 (hypothesis + confirmation): a model proposes, the application validates, the student decides — nothing is persisted, and the confirmation rides in a server-sealed token

**Context.** Phase 4 needs an explanation layer between observation and diagnosis. The existing `generateHypothesis()` feeds a taxonomy-derived candidate error category; Unit 1's observation evidence (the thing a student can see) had no hypothesis path, and the persistence of a confirmed diagnosis is deliberately Unit 3.

**Decision.** Add `generateObservationHypothesis()` (`@ipmat/autopsy`): the model gets numbered observed facts (the same sentences the student sees) and unknowns, never the answer key, question text or any psychological field, and returns the existing `autopsy-hypothesis` schema. The APPLICATION owns safety (`validateHypothesisCandidate()`): possibility phrasing required; certainty, causal and private-thought/psychological language rejected; every supporting-evidence entry must be a verbatim given fact (what reaches the student is the fact, not the paraphrase); a failing proposal is dropped. The trap's name stays internal (taxonomy labels like "…_confusion" are design names, not words for a student). `@ipmat/practice-api` offers the hypothesis only for a submitted, incorrect attempt, only after submission, only to the owner; every failure yields `unavailable` with nothing fabricated. Persistence is avoided by carrying the exact offered hypothesis in an AES-256-GCM token (student + attempt bound, 6 h expiry, shared `IPMAT_HYPOTHESIS_SECRET`); the response goes through the existing `applyConfirmationResponse()`; the student's correction is returned exactly. The model is explicit and fail-closed (`IPMAT_AI_PROVIDER`: none / anthropic with key + explicit model / a dev-only scaffold refused in production).

**Alternatives rejected.** Persisting the awaiting hypothesis or the response (Unit 3's boundary, and a stored "confirmed" is a diagnosis); trusting the client to send back the hypothesis text (forgeable); regenerating on confirmation (a different hypothesis than the one shown); letting the model paraphrase its evidence (ungroundable); forcing a rejected hypothesis into a taxonomy category (the student's words are their own evidence); showing taxonomy trap names to students; a silent default model or a fake fallback hypothesis.

**Consequences.** A student can see, question and correct an explanation without anything being recorded about them; refresh regenerates it and a response is not consumed (both end with Unit 3's persistence). No real model was exercised in this unit (no key available): the validator's strictness against real output is unmeasured and an opt-in live smoke test remains. Confirmation is the student's statement, never a proof of cause.


## D-072 — Phase 4 Unit 3 (persist confirmed repairs): the offer and the student's answer are stored once; only a confirmed answer becomes a diagnosis and exactly one RepairPlan; a correction is recorded but is not a diagnosis

**Context.** Unit 2 kept everything in a sealed token and stored nothing, so a refresh regenerated the explanation and an answer was not consumed. The existing `Autopsy` / `RepairPlan` tables, mappings (`toAutopsyPersistenceRecord`, `toRepairPlanPersistenceRecord`) and planner (`buildRepairPlan`, confirmed-only per D-039) already define what a diagnosis and a plan are.

**Decision.** Reuse them. (1) The *offer* is persisted as an awaiting `autopsies` row (unique `attempt_id` → one per attempt), with the Unit 1 observation evidence as provenance. (2) The *response* is applied by one transaction with a conditional update (`confirmed IS NULL`): exactly one winner; the winner alone may create the plan. (3) Confirm → `confirmed = true` + exactly one `repair_plans` row; reject → `confirmed = false`, nothing else; correct → `confirmed = false` + the exact words, nothing else, reported as `awaiting_diagnosis` (D-039: a free-text correction needs a further diagnosis pass before it can target anything). (4) Migration 0011 makes `repair_plans.autopsy_id` unique so a second plan is impossible at the database level. (5) The token now binds student, attempt, stored offer id + text and expiry; the stored offer is the source of truth. (6) The hypothesis category is the question's designed trap category via the existing `ErrorTaxonomy`, never the model's pick. (7) A repeat response is not an error: it returns the persisted result; the first response wins.

**Alternatives rejected.** Storing the response in the token or the client (forgeable, not durable); a new diagnosis/plan table (duplicate model); "last response wins" or allowing confirm after reject (a stored decision would silently flip, and a plan could appear for a rejected explanation); building a diagnosis from a correction by string-matching (D-039); relaxing `buildRepairPlan` to accept corrected hypotheses; inventing a placeholder plan when no target resolves; using the model's category (an unvalidated model output would choose what gets practised).

**Consequences.** Refresh, restart and a second instance show the same state with no new model call; duplicates and races cannot create a second diagnosis or plan. The existing recommendation repair tier — unchanged — now reads real stored plans, so a confirmation visibly changes the next recommendation's tier; its question choice has not been designed or reviewed for repair quality (Unit 4). No real model has run; the dev-scripted scaffold produced the offers in browser/PG runs. Confirmation remains the student's statement, never proof of cause. Nothing in Unit 3 touches mastery, adaptive selection or orchestration code.


## D-073 — Phase 4 Unit 4 (targeted repair practice): reuse the existing repair tier; add a derived, conservative RepairPlan lifecycle; feed the diagnosed difficulty tier; explain honestly; do not build the correction diagnosis pass

**Context.** Unit 3 stored confirmed plans, and the existing orchestrator's repair tier (D-052, D-062) immediately began consuming them. Inspection showed: selection (explicit tiers, no score), priority over adaptive, no-repeat across tiers and the fallback chain already existed and were tested; but (a) a plan stayed `pending` forever, so the repair tier would pull the student indefinitely; (b) the selector's `targetDifficultyTier` was never supplied by composition; (c) the student-facing copy was generic ("a specific mistake last time") and said nothing about what was being repaired or how direct the match was.

**Decision.** (1) Keep selection/priority/fallback code unchanged. (2) Add `evaluateRepairLifecycle()` (`@ipmat/autopsy`): a pure, order-independent rule over persisted attempts using the existing `pending | in_progress | completed`. `completed` = two consecutive correct *direct-match* answers (`demonstrated`) or three attempts on the concept since the confirmation (`round_limit`); counted attempts must be finalized strictly after the confirmation. Both constants are provisional. (3) The derived status decides; the stored column is advanced forward-only and idempotently (`WHERE status IN (allowed-from)`), best-effort. (4) Composition supplies the diagnosed question's tier as the selector's difficulty tie-break; no escalation. (5) Repair copy is built from the match tier and the plan's own concept/pattern names; a broad fallback is stated as such. (6) The correction → diagnosis pass is **not** built: it needs a schema decision (`autopsies.attempt_id` is unique), a student-text-specific prompt/validator and a second confirmation path; Unit 4 only guarantees a correction creates no plan and affects nothing.

**Alternatives rejected.** Marking a plan repaired after one correct answer (evidence, not proof); never completing plans (an unwinnable plan would block practice forever); a round limit with no completion state, or a new status/column for the reason (vocabulary and schema stay as they are; the reason is derivable); folding repair into adaptive scoring (D-052's explicit-tier design); escalating difficulty after a confirmed error (unjustified, D-021); deriving completion from the stored status alone (a stale column would lie); writing inside the read path unconditionally (only a forward-only sync, and decisions never depend on it); implementing the correction pass by string-matching the student's text (D-039).

**Consequences.** A confirmation changes the next question, the plan advances with the student's attempts, and repair ends by demonstration or after three rounds without claiming the mistake is fixed. With the seeded content (one question per family, distinct traps) only the round-limit path is reachable in Postgres and every repair question is a broad concept fallback — stated plainly to the student. The originally diagnosed question may return after an intervening question. Lifecycle constants and the direct-match definition are uncalibrated. The correction diagnosis pass remains open (Unit 5). No real model has run.


## D-074 — Phase 4 Unit 5 (end-to-end hardening): the correction diagnosis pass is a deliberate future design, not a patch; Phase 4 is complete at technical + policy validation only

**Context.** Phase 4 delivered the chain attempt → observation evidence → hypothesis → student response → persisted diagnosis → RepairPlan → targeted repair → lifecycle. Unit 5 had to test it as one system, resolve the correction → diagnosis question, and say honestly what is validated.

**Decision.** (1) **No correction diagnosis pass is built.** It would require relaxing the one-autopsy-per-attempt uniqueness (or adding a supersedes link and a "current row" rule in every reader), a new prompt/validator for student-asserted text (grounding, prompt-injection, limits), and a second token/confirmation lifecycle — a substantial redesign. The correction stays stored exactly, as `awaiting_diagnosis`, with tests proving nothing can turn it into a diagnosis or plan; the unique-attempt constraint stays as a tripwire; the future contract (supersedes link, labelled-unverified input, fresh confirmation, plan for the new autopsy only) is written down in PHASE_4. (2) No new behavior was added to "fix" the audit's one gap (a confirmation recorded while the diagnosed question was unavailable gets no plan and no later backfill): it is safe, tested and documented. (3) A richer synthetic TEST DATA pool (same cell/family/trap, several tiers, broader, unpublished, malformed) is the validation scaffold: it reuses real cells, is unpublished again in `finally`, and the real published set is asserted unchanged. (4) Claims are split: **technical validation** (invariants), **policy validation** (controlled scenarios follow the documented policy) — both claimed; **outcome calibration** (real students improve) — not claimed. (5) No live model was exercised (no key); no live test was added because it could not be run; the suite stays provider-independent. (6) Phase 4 is marked complete on those terms.

**Alternatives rejected.** Building the correction pass now to call Unit 5 "complete" (architectural debt in a hardening unit; an unverified-text prompt with no safe design); dropping `attempt_id` uniqueness speculatively; letting a correction produce a RepairPlan through `buildRepairPlan()` relaxation (D-039); a plan backfill on republish (new behavior with no decided policy); adding a live-model smoke test that cannot be run; presenting scripted-scenario results as calibration; leaving synthetic data published after tests.

**Consequences.** The loop is safe and deterministic across restart, second instances and concurrency, with ownership, leakage and psychological-inference checks across every student-facing surface. What remains open and is named: the correction diagnosis pass, a live-model run, content depth (seed content cannot demonstrate a repair), calibration of the provisional lifecycle constants against real outcomes, stored completion reasons / served-as-repair records needed for any calibration study, and the no-backfill gap. The next roadmap phase (Phase 5, student-facing training systems) was not started.

## D-075 — Phase 5 Unit 1 (training-system foundation): a Training Session is a thin record on the existing PracticeBlock; each system's own provider decides applicability and selection; attempts stay the one lifecycle

**Context.** Phase 5 makes the deliberate-training dimensions (calculation, speed, traps, novelty, pressure, revision, overtraining) student-facing. Inspection found that most of the engine already exists: five `TrainingSystemProvider`s (`@ipmat/calculation-gym`, `speed-lab`, `trap-lab`, `novelty-training`, `pressure-training`) behind the `@ipmat/training-systems` contract, plus a persisted `PracticeSession → PracticeBlock → Attempt` grouping (D-060) that no application code used yet. What was missing was a student-facing way to *choose* a dimension and run a session on it. Adaptive practice asks "what should this student practice next?"; a training system asks "what performance dimension are we deliberately training?" — they must not collapse into one selector.

**Decision.**
1. **A catalog, not a second engine.** `@ipmat/training-session` holds a fixed catalog (system id → dimension, student words, provider id or `null`) and `runTrainingSystem()`, which routes an id to the provider in the EXISTING orchestration registry via `runTrainingSystemProvider()` and returns its outcome unmodified. Revision and Overtraining have no engine and are catalogued as `providerId: null` → "not built"; they can never start a session. The package imports no persistence client, AI package, selection engine, mastery package or concrete provider (tested).
2. **A Training Session is a thin row on the existing `PracticeBlock`** (`training_sessions`, one-to-one, `practice_block_id` UNIQUE). The block already owns lifecycle (`active` → `completed`/`abandoned`, both final), the completion rule (`targetQuestionCount` / `blockTimeBudgetSeconds`) and every attempt (`practice_block_id` + server-assigned sequence). The new row adds only the system id and the objective/config snapshot. Status, progress and ownership are never duplicated: they are derived through `block → practice session → enrollment → student` on every read (D-015 / D-060), so a restart reconstructs the identical session.
3. **Explicit objective and configuration.** The objective (`systemId`, `dimension`, authored statement, the concept the provider's own requirement names, if any) is snapshotted at start from observable facts only. Configuration is exactly one completion rule — `fixed_question_count` or `fixed_duration` — validated server-side (strict shape, integer bounds; unknown keys rejected; bounds are PROVISIONAL authored limits). Completion is derived (`completionReached`) and always applied by an explicit transition (D-060); an open question is never dropped by completion.
4. **A system must be applicable to start.** `startSession` refuses (409) unless the system's own provider reports `selected` right now (applicable AND a published question available); nothing is created otherwise. The hub shows each system's honest availability (`available | not_applicable | no_eligible_question | unavailable | not_built`) with hand-authored student-safe notes — never a provider's own explanation text, requirement or diagnostics. A student with no evidence therefore sees nothing startable; that is the intended, truthful behavior.
5. **Selection reuses the one composition.** `TrainingRecommendationService.runTrainingSystems()` composes the student's ownership-verified, published-only state once and runs only the named system(s). It ignores confirmed repair plans and never consults adaptive selection. The only addition is `excludeQuestionIds`, which narrows the candidate pool ("not a question this session already used"); it adds no ranking, and an exhausted pool is reported honestly (`no_question`) rather than repeating a question or inventing one.
6. **One attempt lifecycle.** The server starts each training attempt through the ordinary `PracticeApiService.startAttempt()` with the session's block id (an internal parameter no HTTP route reads — a client cannot place an attempt in a block; the persistence layer re-verifies the block's ownership chain). Answers use the existing `/v1/attempts/*` routes, so `chosenAnswer`/`isCorrect`/`timeSpentSeconds` stay server-derived (D-034) and evidence, history, mastery and the recommendation see training attempts like any other. Training writes no autopsy, repair or mastery row.
7. **Persistence and concurrency.** `TrainingSessionRepository.create()` is ONE `Serializable` transaction (practice session if needed, block, training-session row — no orphan block possible). At most one active training session per enrollment (checked in the transaction; a duplicate start for the SAME system resumes it, a different system is 409). Migration `0012` adds the table and a hand-written partial unique index `practice_sessions_one_active_per_enrollment`: `findActiveByEnrollmentId()` already failed closed on several active sessions, and training creates the practice session on demand, so two racing first-starts could otherwise leave an enrollment's recommendations permanently broken. Within an instance, operations are serialized per session/enrollment; across instances the database guarantees (unique open attempt, unique block link, the new index) converge on one winner, which the loser resumes.
8. **Honest scope.** `PracticeBlockState` has no abandon trigger here (no route ends a session except the student's own explicit finish, refused while a question is open); `abandoned` stays reserved. Finishing never mutates an open attempt.

**Alternatives rejected.** A new session/attempt table or a parallel "training attempt" lifecycle (a second source of truth); storing status/progress/enrollment on `training_sessions` (drift); a training-specific selector or scoring in the application layer (a second selection engine, forbidden by D-053); stub systems with fake behavior to make the hub look full; surfacing provider explanation strings to students (written for engineers, mention internal metadata); auto-completing the block on reaching the target (D-060); letting the client name a block or send a clock; relying on the in-process lock alone for the one-active-session guarantee.

**Consequences.** The foundation is real end to end (real Postgres: restart, second instance, ownership, 12-way concurrent start, 10-way concurrent next; real browser: choose → configure → question → answer → result → continue → complete, across a full API restart). Each existing provider is reachable as a student-facing system *when the evidence supports it*; their thresholds remain provisional and uncalibrated, and none was exercised against real students. Not built (named for later units): per-system student-facing presentation, rationale and progression (Unit 2 = Calculation Gym), Revision and Overtraining engines, any session abandonment/timeout trigger, and per-system objective-specific completion rules beyond fixed count/duration.

## D-076 — Phase 5 Unit 2 (Calculation Gym): reuse the existing provider; align what a stage SERVES with what counts as its progression evidence; derive the stage and every stage change from history; show an observable, claim-free summary

**Context.** Unit 1 (D-075) built the session framework. Unit 2 makes the existing `@ipmat/calculation-gym` provider genuinely student-facing inside it. Inspection of the provider (D-054) found it production-worthy — friction evidence (accuracy on high- vs low-`computationalLoad` questions, ≥ 3 graded attempts per slice, gap ≥ 0.2), independently gated stage progression derived from history on every call (nothing stored), and a deterministic three-step tie-break — with ONE defect: **a stage did not serve what counts as that stage's progression evidence.** Progression defines foundational-shaped = load < 0.5 and not `multi_step`, mixed-shaped = load ≥ 0.5 and not `time_pressured`; selection only applied a load FLOOR (0 / 0.5) and the first tie-break was least-exposure, ahead of load proximity. So a "foundational" session could be served an unseen heavy or multi-step question, and a "mixed" session a timed one — neither counts toward the stage it was served at, so progression could stall.

**Decision.**
1. **Fix the defect minimally, preserving every stage's meaning.** `CalculationGymRequirement` gains three OPTIONAL fields restating progression's own shapes — `maxComputationalLoad` (exclusive ceiling), `excludeMultiStep`, `excludeTimePressured` — set by `buildRequirement()` (foundational: load < 0.5 + exclude multi-step; mixed: load ≥ 0.5 + exclude time-pressured; time_pressured unchanged) and honored by `qualifies()`. Absent fields mean no constraint, so a requirement without them selects exactly as before (tested). No threshold, applicability rule, progression rule or tie-break changed; nothing was weakened to make content "fit". Property-style tests (300 seeded pools × 3 stages, no `Math.random`) prove: the served question always has its stage's evidence shape, selection is independent of candidate order, and repeatable.
2. **The stage is derived, never stored.** Each system whose provider is staged declares its ordered stages and hand-authored, threshold-free copy in the catalog (`TrainingSystemDefinition.stages`; keys restate the provider's vocabulary and a parity test asserts they stay equal — `@ipmat/training-session` cannot import a provider). `stageKeyOfRun()` reads the generic `requirement.stage` the provider already returns (for `selected` AND `no_eligible_question`; unknown when not applicable). No stage column, no second state machine; `training_sessions` still holds only system, objective and configuration (asserted on real rows).
3. **A stage change is derived by re-running the same system as of before the session's last finalized attempt** (`runTrainingSystems(..., { excludeAttemptIds })`), which reproduces the stage the previous question was served at from persisted history.
