After a restart or on a second instance the same rows give the same transition (tested). Copy is authored per direction ("Next stage" / "Stage changed"), states no threshold, score, reason code or improvement. Edge (documented): ordinary practice done in between could shift the reconstruction.
4. **An observable summary on every session view** — submitted / skipped / correct / incorrect counts, server-measured time on answered questions and their expected time — derived from the session's attempts on every read. No percentage, rating or score; the completion screen states it is a record of the session, not a measure of lasting progress.
5. **System-specific honest copy:** Calculation's "not applicable" note says what the student would need first (recorded answers on both lighter and heavier-arithmetic questions of the same concept) without revealing thresholds; the session screen is titled "Calculation Gym".
6. **Not changed:** applicability, thresholds, `STAGE_MASTERY_ACCURACY`, tie-break order, adaptive selection, mastery, the attempt lifecycle. No Calculation-specific attempt, session, mastery or score exists.

**Alternatives rejected.** Storing a stage (or a "stage reached" event) on the session/attempt (duplicates derivable state; a second state machine); adding Calculation logic or the provider's stage vocabulary imports to `@ipmat/training-session`/`practice-api` (boundary); weakening the friction thresholds so the 3-question seed content activates Calculation (hides the gap — the real DB suite uses a labelled synthetic TEST DATA pool in a throwaway database instead); ranking candidates by a composite score; any randomness; a percentage "accuracy" display; claiming improvement on stage advance.

**Consequences.** Calculation is the first genuinely usable training card whenever the real evidence rule is met; it serves only what its stage trains; stage and transitions survive restarts. Open and named: the provider's thresholds are PROVISIONAL and uncalibrated, no real student has used it, the seed content cannot activate it naturally, the stage-change reconstruction assumes no ordinary practice in between, and stages 2–3 depend on content tagged with `computationalLoad`/`time_pressured` (authored, uncalibrated DNA). Unit 3 (Speed Lab) was not started.

## D-077 — Phase 5 Unit 3 (Speed Lab): the second student-facing training system reuses the Unit 1/2 framework with catalog data only; align what a Speed Lab stage SERVES with what counts as its progression evidence

**Context.** Unit 2 (D-076) proved the pattern: a staged provider is exposed through the generic session framework by reading its `requirement.stage`, wording the stage from the catalog, deriving stage changes from history, and summarizing observable counts — with no provider logic in the application, API or web layers. Unit 3 applies it to `@ipmat/speed-lab` (D-055) without copying any Calculation logic: Speed Lab answers "is there observable evidence that the student takes too much time relative to expected time, independent of whether calculation is the bottleneck?" and must stay semantically independent (no `computationalLoad`, no `@ipmat/calculation-gym`, canonical `AUTOPSY_THRESHOLDS.SLOW_SPEED_RATIO`, correctness-independent denominator, incorrect-and-slow diagnostic only, count-based progression over disjoint slices, no composite score).

**Inspection finding (a defect of the same class as D-076).** Progression counts good-pace correct answers that are hint-free, valid-timed and **non-time-pressured**: steady gate = conceptualLoad < 0.5; mixed gate = conceptualLoad >= 0.5. Selection did not mirror that: `steady_pace` set only a conceptual-load ceiling (a time-pressured light question qualified, and the exposure tie-break ranks before expected-time), and `mixed_pace` set no floor and no timed exclusion (a light or timed question qualified). A served question could therefore never count toward the gate it was meant to feed, so progression could stall. `time_constrained` is the final stage (nothing further to feed) and was already correct.

**Decision.**
1. **Minimal, meaning-preserving fix.** `SpeedLabRequirement` gains two OPTIONAL fields restating progression's own shapes — `minConceptualLoad` (inclusive floor) and `excludeTimePressured` — set by `buildRequirement()` (steady: load < 0.5 + exclude timed; mixed: load >= 0.5 + exclude timed; time_constrained unchanged) and honored by `qualifies()`. Absent fields mean no constraint, so a requirement without them selects exactly as before (tested). No threshold, applicability rule, evidence definition, progression gate or tie-break changed; nothing was lowered or weakened. A 300-pool x 3-stage property test (no randomness, no clock) proves: the served question always has its stage's shape, selection is independent of candidate order and repeatable; a serving/progression-alignment test walks a student through all three stages using only what the stages serve.
2. **Catalog data only.** Speed Lab's ordered stages and authored, threshold-free copy ("Stage 1 · Steady pace", "Stage 2 · Mixed pace", "Stage 3 · Time-constrained"), its session title ("Speed Lab"), its goal sentence ("Improve solving speed: working within the expected time on concepts you already answer correctly.") and its not-applicable note ("Needs several recorded answers on straightforward questions of the same concept first.") are catalog entries; a parity test asserts the stage keys equal `SPEED_LAB_STAGES`. The application, API and web layers needed no change: stage reading, transition derivation (re-running the system as of before the session's last finalized attempt), the observable summary, ownership, concurrency and the attempt lifecycle are the generic Unit 1/2 code. The goal sentence is a training AIM, not a claim about the student; copy never states a ratio, threshold, count or "slow solver"-style trait.
3. **Stage is never stored** (no stage column, no `stage_state` table); asserted on real rows. `time_constrained` means only "the question carries the time-pressured testing mode" and is not Pressure Training.
4. **Semantic independence preserved and tested:** a student whose evidence activates Speed Lab sees Calculation unaffected (separate evidence), and no Speed view or page mentions calculation.

**Alternatives rejected.** Copying Calculation's service/UI code (duplication; the framework already is the shared path); persisting the stage or a "stage reached" marker; an outer ranking heuristic or fallback-to-adaptive in the API; lowering the slow boundary or the count gates so thin content activates it (real DB/browser QA uses a labelled synthetic pool in a throwaway database); a rate-based progression gate (D-055 explains why it contradicts applicability); exposing the slow fraction or counts to "motivate" the student.

**Consequences.** Speed Lab is the second real training card whenever the existing evidence rule is met. Behavior worth knowing (provider semantics, unchanged): good-pace answers dilute the slow fraction, so a session can end with "no further question fits" once the evidence no longer shows slow-correct dominance; heavier-question attempts are outside the applicability population, so stage-2 progress does not change applicability. Open and named: all thresholds are PROVISIONAL and uncalibrated, no real student has used Speed Lab, the seed content cannot activate it naturally, stages depend on authored `conceptualLoad`/`time_pressured` DNA, and the stage-change reconstruction assumes no ordinary practice between two training questions. Trap + Novelty (Unit 4) was not started.

## D-078 — Phase 5 Unit 4 (Trap Lab): the third provider made student-facing with NO stages; the provider is unchanged; copy is keyed by the provider's reason without exposing it

**Context.** Units 2–3 (D-076, D-077) exposed the two STAGED providers through the generic session framework. Trap Lab (D-056) is deliberately different: recurrence is evaluated at the error-taxonomy-CODE level across the whole attempt history, concept is NOT part of the trap's identity (`evaluate()` never populates `targetConceptName`; `select()` honors one only if supplied), the only hard gate is `AUTOPSY_THRESHOLDS.REPEATED_EVIDENCE_MIN_COUNT` DISTINCT failing question ids (a retried question never accumulates), surface/cell diversity and hint resistance are diagnostic-only, recurrence is cumulative with no decay, selection prefers an UNSEEN pattern-taxonomy-cell partition ("repeat the trap, vary the surface"), then least exposure, then question id, and it has **no progression vocabulary** (no honest graduated DNA dimension exists for "trap intensity").

**Inspection finding.** Reconciling the prompt, the docs and the source: the source is authoritative on every point above. There is no stage/serving mismatch to fix (no stages exist), and nothing in the provider needed to change — **`@ipmat/trap-lab` was not modified.** Two consequences of the provider's own semantics are worth recording: (a) a start-level `no_eligible_question` is effectively unreachable, because the questions that produced the recurrence are themselves published, trap-tagged candidates (and an unpublished evidence question drops its attempt from the evidence) — the honest "nothing left" state is reached INSIDE a session, when every qualifying question has been used (`no_question`); (b) because recurrence is cumulative, correct answers never end applicability, so a session ends by its configured length, the pool running out, or the student.

**Decision.**
1. **Catalog data only, plus three small generic copy hooks.** Trap Lab gets a session title ("Trap Lab"), goal sentence ("Practice a recurring trap pattern: the same kind of trap, in different question formats."), a focus sentence appended to the objective when the provider names no target concept ("This session focuses on a trap pattern that has appeared across your practice."), and NO `stages`. The hooks — `notApplicableByReason`, `noEligibleNote`, `focusSentence` — are optional catalog fields: the provider's own not-applicable REASON selects which authored sentence the hub shows ("Needs recorded incorrect answers on questions that share a trap pattern first." vs "Your recorded practice doesn't show the same trap pattern across several different questions yet."); the reason code itself is never shown. Calculation and Speed are untouched.
2. **No stage, anywhere.** The session, question and completion views carry `stage: null` and no transition; the UI renders no stage line, no stage progress and no next-stage notice (asserted in unit tests, on real Postgres and in the browser). No `TrapSession`, `TrapAttempt`, `trap_state`, trap score or recurrence snapshot exists; the session is the generic one.
3. **Nothing about the trap is shown.** No trap code, taxonomy-cell id, recurrence count, threshold, resistance, `ErrorCategory` or provider diagnostics reaches any view (asserted on JSON, raw HTTP bodies and the rendered HTML). The student sees an observational, training-oriented target, never "diagnosed", "prone", "always", "bad at", a score, or any claim about why they answered as they did.
4. **Independence preserved.** Trap Lab consumes no confirmed Autopsy, RepairPlan, confirmation state, correction text or model confidence (`TrainingSystemContext` has no such field; the session service ignores repair plans). The orchestration layer may still choose repair first by its own policy; Trap Lab's semantics do not change. It does not import or depend on Calculation, Speed, adaptive/repair selection or orchestration internals (existing boundary test).
5. **Preservation tests were added to the provider package without touching its source:** the canonical recurrence gate boundary (N−1 vs N), no recency/decay, cell diversity and resistance as diagnostics only, evaluate-is-the-only-authority (a spy proves `select()` is never reached when not applicable), a requirement not produced by `evaluate()` is an `error`, and no stage/score/confidence/randomness vocabulary in source.

**Alternatives rejected.** Inventing exposure/discrimination/resistance stages to reuse the staged UX; scoping recurrence by concept; a recency window or "resistance score"; surfacing the trap's name or count to "explain" the session; copying Calculation/Speed service code; a fixed Trap-specific question count (the framework's student-chosen length is generic, and the provider/pool stay authoritative for when questions run out).

**Consequences.** Trap Lab is the third real training card whenever the existing recurrence rule is met, varying the question surface across a session. Open and named: the recurrence threshold is the autopsy package's provisional constant and nothing is calibrated; no real student has used it; the seed content cannot activate it; trap tagging depends on authored `trapErrorTaxonomyCode` DNA; the provider groups by exact code, so a question without a code is invisible to it. Novelty Training (the other half of the old "Unit 4" idea) and everything after it was not started.

## D-079 — Phase 5 Unit 5 (Novelty Training): the fourth provider made student-facing, exposure-first with NO stages; the provider is unchanged; the objective no longer names a concept that can rotate

**Context.** Units 2–4 exposed the staged providers (D-076, D-077) and Trap Lab (D-078) through the generic session framework. Novelty Training (D-058) is the first EXPOSURE-first provider: it asks "has this student been deliberately exposed to this unfamiliar presentation style enough yet?", never "is this student weak at it". It uses ONLY the categorical Question DNA `noveltyLevel` (`novel_representation` / `novel_combination` / `novel_context` are PEER categories, not a ladder) — never `testingModes`' coincidentally-named values nor the continuous `difficultyDimensions.representationNovelty`. `evaluate()` reads only `context.studentId` and `context.attemptRecords` (never `candidates`), so applicability can never depend on available content. Gate 1 is a concept-specific baseline: ≥ `MASTERY_CONSTANTS.MIN_OBSERVATIONS_FOR_COMPONENT` (3) DISTINCT standard-level questions of THAT concept (source: distinct question ids, graded submitted attempts; another concept's activity never counts). Gate 2: at least one non-standard level of that concept has < 3 distinct questions. The target is the globally lowest-exposure (concept, level) pair, then concept name, then level name — a directly observed integer, no score. Exposure counts are correctness-independent, cumulative, with no decay. Selection is provider-local and exact (published, structurally valid, concept AND `noveltyLevel` equal), prefers an UNSEEN taxonomy cell at the target level (a boolean partition), falls back to the whole matching pool when every surface is seen, then least exposure, then question id.

**Inspection finding.** The source matches the brief and D-058 on every point; the provider has 60 tests, including a candidate-pool-invariance test and a source-level regression that `evaluate()`'s code never references `context.candidates`. **`@ipmat/novelty-training` was not modified.** One reconciliation: the baseline counts DISTINCT standard questions (a set), not raw attempts — the source is authoritative and a retried question counts once. One genuine contradiction was found in the Unit 1 layer, not the provider: the session objective is a SNAPSHOT taken at start and (since D-075) names the provider's target concept — but Novelty re-targets the globally least-exposed pair after every recorded attempt, so a start-time "Focus: <concept>" could be wrong two questions later.

**Decision.**
1. **Catalog data only for the provider, one generic fix for the contradiction.** Novelty Training gets a session title ("Novelty Training"), goal sentence ("Practice unfamiliar question styles: build exposure to different ways the exam can present a concept."), focus sentence ("This session is expanding the kinds of questions you've encountered.") and NO `stages`. Provider-reason copy: `insufficient_evidence` → "Needs several recorded answers on standard questions of one concept first."; `sufficient_novelty_exposure` → "Your recorded practice already includes several questions in each unfamiliar style, so there is nothing to add right now."; applicable-but-no-content → "No published question in an unfamiliar style is available right now."
2. **The minimal correction:** an optional catalog flag `conceptNotInObjective` (set only for Novelty) makes the objective omit a concept that can rotate; the question itself still shows its chapter and concept. Calculation and Speed (whose target does not rotate) still name theirs (tested). Two Unit 1 tests that used Novelty as the sample system were updated to the new objective, with the reason stated.
3. **An authored "stopped applying" message.** When a session's system stops applying mid-session because its OWN evidence now says nothing more is needed (every style sufficiently exposed), the session says so in authored words ("…there is nothing more to add right now. You can end the session.") via an optional `noLongerApplicableNote`; every other system keeps the generic sentence.
4. **No stage, anywhere.** The session, question and completion views carry `stage: null`; the UI shows no stage line, progress or notice; the three styles are never shown or named. No `NoveltySession`, `noveltyState`, score or exposure-snapshot table exists — exposure is reconstructed from the attempt history on every call (asserted on real rows; a start-time objective holds no novelty state).
5. **Nothing about exposure is shown.** No style name, `noveltyLevel`, taxonomy-cell id, exposure or baseline count, threshold, `representationNovelty` or provider diagnostics reaches any view (asserted on JSON, raw HTTP bodies and rendered HTML); no deficiency or trait wording ("struggle", "weak at", "lack", "not good at", "adaptability").
6. **Preservation tests added without touching the provider:** the exact gates (MIN−1 vs MIN, distinct questions), rotation through the three peer styles, correctness-independence, candidate-invariance over 25 pools, applicable + zero candidates ≠ not applicable, `select()` never reached when not applicable and an invalid requirement is an `error`, exact match on the categorical level, and no stage/score/substitute fields or randomness in source.

**Behavior worth knowing (provider semantics, unchanged).** Target selection happens BEFORE content is consulted: if the globally lowest-exposure pair has no published question, the result is `no_eligible_question` even if another style has content (nothing is substituted). Because exposure is cumulative and the target is the lowest pair, a session naturally rotates and ends once every style has enough distinct questions — the evidence, not a fixed ladder, decides. Mid-session applicability can end that way by design.

**Alternatives rejected.** Stages or "levels" for the three peer styles; gating on `representationNovelty` or `testingModes`; letting the candidate pool decide applicability; making "all surfaces seen" mean no question; naming the current style or concept in the UI; an exposure percentage or novelty score; a recency window.

**Consequences.** Novelty Training is the fourth real training card whenever the existing evidence rule is met. Open and named: the 3-question baseline and 3-question sufficiency are the shared PROVISIONAL mastery constant, nothing is calibrated, and exposure does not show comprehension; no real student has used it; the seed content cannot activate it; it depends on authored `noveltyLevel` DNA. Pressure Training and everything after it was not started.

## D-080 — Phase 5 Unit 6 (Pressure Training): the fifth provider made student-facing as a TIMED, SUSTAINED run over its own practice block; the provider is unchanged; no stages; one latent concurrency defect found and fixed

**Context.** Pressure Training (D-059 deferred it, D-060 built the Practice Session/Block prerequisite, D-061 designed and implemented the provider, D-062 wired it into orchestration) is the one provider whose evidence is not a set of single-question facts but **block-grouped sequences**. This unit exposes it through the generic session framework (D-073–D-079) without changing `@ipmat/pressure-training`.

**Inspection finding — what "pressure" means here (from D-061 and the source, not the name).** Pressure is only ever evidenced by PRACTICE BLOCKS: a block qualifies with ≥ 3 attempts, passes the 9-step fail-closed Block Evidence Validation and has a strict-majority concept; a concept needs ≥ `MIN_OBSERVATIONS_FOR_COMPONENT` (3) qualifying blocks; then ONE of three observable block facts must trigger, in fixed precedence — `within_block_degradation` (second-half accuracy ≥ 0.40 below first-half, PROVISIONAL), `reduced_recovery` (median inter-attempt gap < 5 s, PROVISIONAL) or `budget_consumption` (active solving time ≥ the block's time budget, definitional). `evaluate()` reads ONLY `practiceBlocks` and `attemptRecords` — never candidates — and is unconditionally `not_applicable: insufficient_evidence` when there are no blocks, however slow the single-question history, so it is structurally incapable of firing off Speed Lab's evidence (Speed Lab's `time_constrained` is a single-question stage; sustained sequences, cumulative budgets and reduced recovery are Pressure's). Selection (`select()`): published + structurally valid + exact target concept; prefer a taxonomy cell this student has not yet attempted at that concept; then least exposure; then question id. No weighting, no score, no stages, no provider-side notion of a timer.

**Decision.**
1. **A training session IS the block.** A Pressure Training session is one `PracticeBlock` (the existing Unit 1 mapping) and, once finished, becomes one more block of exactly the evidence the provider reads — nothing new is stored and no "pressure state" exists. Every attempt in it is an ordinary attempt, started with the internal `practiceBlockId` (never client-supplied). The composition layer already assembles `practiceBlocks` from persisted rows (only blocks whose attempts are all finalized and present in the attempt records), so no provider, composition or orchestration code changed.
2. **Pressure Training is a TIMED run.** The catalog gains one optional, generic field `completionKinds` (set only for Pressure): the server rejects any other completion rule (`invalid_config` → HTTP 400) and the hub offers only the timed presets. The session's `fixed_duration` also becomes the block's own `blockTimeBudgetSeconds` (existing Unit 1 behavior), the same budget `budget_consumption` is measured against. Time stays **server-authoritative**: the session completes when the server's clock passes the budget, an open question is never dropped, and nothing new starts afterwards. The web shows a live "Time left" line seeded from the server's remaining seconds — display only; it never decides anything.
3. **No stages.** D-061 specifies none, so the session, question and completion views carry `stage: null` and the UI shows no stage line or notice. The three evidenced dimensions are never a ladder or a label.
4. **Catalog data.** Title "Pressure Training"; goal "Practice a sustained, timed run of questions: keep working steadily across a whole sequence, not just one question."; focus "This session is a sustained, timed run of questions."; per-reason copy keyed by the provider's reason code (never shown): `insufficient_evidence` → "Needs several earlier training sessions of at least three questions each on the same concept first."; `sufficient_blocks_no_pressure_detected` → "Your recorded training sessions don't call for this right now."; applicable-but-no-published-question → "No published question for this training is available right now."; `conceptNotInObjective` (the evidenced concept can change as blocks accrue); authored `noLongerApplicableNote`.
5. **Nothing about pressure is shown.** No dimension name, threshold, gap, half-accuracy, block id, taxonomy-cell id, count of evidence or provider diagnostic reaches any student view (asserted on JSON, raw bodies and rendered HTML); no stress / fatigue / confidence / resilience / deficiency wording; no score of any kind. Pressure Training is never framed as a weakness detector: the hub copy says what the session is, and the not-applicable sentences say what is needed or that the record does not call for it.
6. **A latent concurrency defect, found and fixed (generic, not Pressure-specific).** The real-Postgres race test (12 parallel starts across two instances) failed with a 500 once a student already had an active practice session: both instances computed the same next block `sequence_number`, and the loser surfaced a unique-index violation (Prisma `P2002`) instead of a serialization failure, so it was never mapped to the "another instance won" conflict the service already resumes from. Units 2–5 never raced a student with a prior block. `PrismaTrainingSessionRepository.create()` now maps `P2002` to the same `conflict` `PersistenceError`; the service then resumes the winner as before. Covered by the Pressure race test (3 consecutive runs green).
7. **A tested limitation, disclosed.** Because evidence questions must be published to enter the attempt records, "applicable but no published question for this concept" cannot be reached in a fixture where the evidence questions themselves are published candidates of that concept; the unreachable-in-practice state has authored copy and a catalog test but no end-to-end test. Pool exhaustion (`no_question`, session still active, nothing from another concept substituted) is covered end to end.

**Alternatives rejected.** Letting Pressure accept a question-count session (it would have no time budget and `budget_consumption` could never trigger for its own blocks); stages for the three dimensions (D-061 forbids a ladder); a countdown that ends the session client-side (the server is the clock); changing the provider to need fewer blocks (would weaken a disclosed, provisional rule to make it appear); a pressure score or "pressure readiness" (no calibrated formula exists, D-021).

**Not claimed.** That timed runs improve anyone's performance under exam conditions; that the 5 s / 0.40 thresholds are calibrated (PROVISIONAL, no real attempt-timing data has ever existed); real-student validation; any live-model involvement (none).

## D-081 — Phase 5 Unit 7 (Revision): the SIXTH provider, `@ipmat/revision-training` — concept-level deliberate re-exposure after a fixed, provisional 14-day dormancy; no stages, no stored state, no decay model

**Context.** Revision had NO specification anywhere in the repository (project-memory marked it "undesigned"; the catalog said only "Revisiting concepts after time has passed"; D-041/D-056/D-067 repeatedly declined to invent recency or decay). The product owner supplied the authoritative Unit 7 specification; this entry records it and its implementation. Nothing was inferred from the name.

**Decision (the specification, implemented as written).**
1. **What Revision is.** A deliberate re-exposure of a CONCEPT the student has attempted before but has not attempted for a meaningful period. It is NOT mistake repair, Trap Lab, Novelty Training, Pressure Training, adaptive practice or Overtraining, and it reads none of their evidence.
2. **Eligibility (concept level).** A concept is eligible only when BOTH hold: (a) the student has ≥ `REVISION_CONSTANTS.MIN_GRADED_ATTEMPTS` (3) graded attempts on it, and (b) its most recent graded attempt is ≥ `REVISION_CONSTANTS.DORMANCY_DAYS` (14) days old. A graded attempt is `submitted` with a recorded correctness; skipped/abandoned never count; correctness is irrelevant (three wrong answers are as eligible as three right ones). An attempt with no usable persisted `finalizedAt` is excluded entirely (fail closed). Attempts are counted, not distinct questions (a retried question counts each time). Another student's attempts never count. A concept answered 2 + 2 across two names is not pooled.
3. **The 14-day constant is PROVISIONAL.** A fixed product constant, deterministic, NOT a learned decay model, NOT a forgetting curve or spaced-repetition interval, uncalibrated, intended for later empirical calibration. A "day" is exactly 24 h of elapsed time between the persisted attempt timestamp and the supplied `now` (no timezone/DST arithmetic); exactly 14 days is eligible, one millisecond less is not. `MIN_GRADED_ATTEMPTS` is Revision's OWN constant (3), deliberately not coupled to `@ipmat/mastery`'s observation gate.
4. **Time comes from the caller, never a clock.** `TrainingSystemContext` gains ONE additive optional field, `now?: string` (ISO-8601) — the same precedent as `practiceBlocks` (D-061) — forwarded by `TrainingOrchestrationInput.now` and assembled by the existing composition layer (which already computes `now`). The provider never reads `Date.now()`; a missing or unparseable `now` fails closed (`not_applicable: insufficient_evidence`), so the same context always gives the same answer.
5. **Applicability and target.** No eligible concept → the existing contract's `not_applicable: insufficient_evidence` (Revision's only reason). Otherwise applicable, targeting the eligible concept whose most recent graded attempt is OLDEST (dormant the longest); an exact tie breaks on `conceptName` ascending. No hidden score. `evaluate()` reads only `studentId`, `attemptRecords` and `now` — never `candidates`, trap codes, novelty levels, practice blocks, mastery or the taxonomy (tested by poisoning each and by candidate-pool invariance).
6. **Question selection.** From published, structurally valid (question id, concept, taxonomy cell, FINITE expected time) questions of the exact target concept: (1) prefer a taxonomy cell the student has not attempted at that concept, (2) then least prior exposure, (3) then `questionId` ascending. It never switches concept to fill a pool: an empty target pool is the explicit `no_eligible_question`.
7. **No stages; no stored state.** The 14-day rule is an applicability condition only, never a ladder: no `stages` in the catalog entry, `stage: null` in every view. Nothing is persisted — no `revisionDue`, `revisionState`, `nextRevisionDate` or `revisionScore` (a Postgres test asserts no such column exists); Revision is derived from the attempt history on every call.
8. **Lifecycle: the generic session framework, unchanged.** A Revision session is a thin row on the existing `PracticeBlock` (D-075); its attempts are ordinary attempts, so a revised concept's latest graded attempt becomes recent and the concept immediately stops qualifying. Consequently a session serves ONE question per dormant concept: after the first answer the target moves to the next-longest-dormant concept, and when none remains the session says so in authored words ("That concept has now been revisited, and no other concept is waiting for a revisit right now. You can end the session.") and offers to end. A SKIPPED revision question is not a graded attempt, so the concept stays dormant and the session keeps serving it. Both completion rules are allowed.
9. **Integration without disturbing completed systems.** The provider is registered in the orchestration provider registry (so `runTrainingSystem()` can route the student-chosen "revision" system) but is DELIBERATELY NOT added to `TRAINING_SYSTEM_PROVIDER_PRIORITY_ORDER`: Revision is student-chosen re-exposure, never part of the adaptive "what next?" chain, so adaptive orchestration and its tests are unchanged. The catalog entry keeps `systemId: "revision"`; its `providerId` is `"revision-training"` (a documented exception to the id-equality convention — the student-facing id predates the provider).
10. **Student-facing.** Card "Revision" — "Revisit a concept you have practiced before but not attempted for a while." Not startable: "Needs a concept you have practiced several times before and have not attempted for a while."; applicable-but-no-published-question: "No published question is available for that concept right now."; session title "Revision", objective names no concept (`conceptNotInObjective` — the target moves after each attempt). No day count, threshold, timestamp, graded-attempt count, provider id, taxonomy-cell id or diagnostics reaches any view; no forgetting, memory, ability, motivation, emotion or trait wording; no score, due date, interval or priority field.

**Distinction from the other systems.** Calculation Gym — computational fluency; Speed Lab — time efficiency; Trap Lab — recurring error-taxonomy failures; Novelty Training — exposure to novel styles; Pressure Training — performance under pressure using qualifying block evidence; Overtraining — not built. Revision reads only attempt counts, last graded time and question/cell exposure, so it is structurally independent of every one of them (tested: recent history can activate Novelty without Revision and, a month later, Revision without changing Novelty).

**Defects found and fixed.** (a) `typeof NaN === "number"` let a candidate with a NaN expected time pass the "structurally valid" check the sibling providers use; Revision's own check uses `Number.isFinite` (a test caught it before it shipped; sibling providers were not modified). (b) Placeholder tests that asserted Revision was "not built" (catalog, hub, run-system, orchestration dependency set) were updated with the reason stated.

**Alternatives rejected.** A decay curve, spaced-repetition interval, forgetting probability or "revision due" flag (explicitly excluded, and no calibration data exists); a provider-side clock; putting Revision in the adaptive priority chain (would change completed adaptive behavior); stages per interval; using correctness (that would make it a repair system); concept-agnostic "least recently seen question" (not concept-level).

**Not claimed.** That revisiting a concept improves retention or exam performance; that 14 days is the right interval; that students forget anything; real-student validation; any live-model involvement (none).

## D-082 — Product Phase 6 Prompt 1: the Exam Pack abstraction (`@ipmat/exam-pack`) and a validated, provenance-carrying Concept Universe; IPMAT as data; no migration

**Context.** Product Phase 6 starts by giving the system a structured, machine-readable representation of an exam. The repository already had `Exam -> Section -> Chapter -> Concept -> ConceptRelation` tables, the eight typed relations (D-013), a Percentages-only `ConceptGraph` fixture and name-based lookups (D-030). It did NOT have: a generic, exam-agnostic pack; any validation of the graph (nothing stopped a duplicate edge, a dangling reference, a self-loop, a prerequisite cycle or a relation across two exams); any traversal beyond direct lookups (no ancestors/descendants/learning order); any provenance or review state for exam structure; or any hierarchy deeper than chapter. The owner directed that the abstraction be exam-agnostic now. That is an explicit exception to the "no multi-exam branching" working-style rule: the ABSTRACTION is generic, but exactly one pack (IPMAT Indore) is registered and no other code branches on exam.

**Decision.**
1. **A new pure domain package, `@ipmat/exam-pack`.** Depends only on `@ipmat/concept-graph` (reusing its eight `RelationType`s, `Certainty`, `RequirementLevel`, `RelationSource`, `ConceptStatus` and `normalizeConceptNameKey`; nothing was duplicated or renamed). No Prisma, Next.js, AI, mastery or attempt import (tested). The generic modules never contain the string `IPMAT` (tested); the only exam-specific file is `src/packs/ipmatIndore.ts`.
2. **The pack is DATA with stable keys.** `ExamPack` = identity (`examCode`, `packVersion`, `name`), ordered sections, a syllabus hierarchy of `SyllabusNode`s (`parentKey` nesting, deterministic sibling order), concepts located at a node, typed relations, terminology and a provenance on every artifact. Keys are `slugKey(name)` (lowercase, non-alphanumerics become `-`) so the in-code pack and the database-assembled pack derive identical keys.
3. **Knowledge space, not student state.** No field in the package represents mastery, confidence, attempts or a student; a concept being in a pack never implies it is known. Tested on the types, the IPMAT data and the public view.
4. **Relationship semantics are explicit and per type** (`semantics.ts`): `prerequisite`, `foundational`, `advanced_extension` are ORDERING (cycles are self-contradictory and rejected, checked per type and then on their union); `directly_related`, `commonly_combined`, `related_but_distinct` are SYMMETRIC (the reversed duplicate is rejected); `application` and `dependent` are directional but NOT ordering and may legitimately be cyclic. Cycles are never checked across all types: the real curated graph connects Ratio and Percentages in opposite directions through `prerequisite` and `dependent`, which is correct, not a cycle. Self-relations, exact duplicates, unknown endpoints, blank rationale/shared-knowledge and unknown enum values are rejected.
5. **"Unlocks" is not a ninth relation type.** It is the derived inverse of `prerequisite` (`getUnlockedConcepts`). Ancestors/descendants default to the strict `prerequisite` gate; a caller opts into broader types explicitly.
6. **Provenance is two independent axes** (`kind`: canonical/authored/imported/inferred; `reviewState`: unvalidated/reviewed). Rules: every artifact needs a `sourceRef`; `imported` needs a `licenseRef` (free-to-access is not free-to-copy); `canonical` must be reviewed; `reviewed` must name a reviewer and an unreviewed artifact must not carry one; an `ai_suggested` relation must be `inferred` and a human one must not be; an inferred relation cannot be `confirmed` until reviewed (no silent promotion). `summarizePackValidationState()` reports review coverage, derived on every call, never stored.
7. **IPMAT is represented with what the repository actually holds, labelled honestly.** The repository has no official IPMAT syllabus document, so the pack contains only the Quant section, the 15 Quant chapters seeded since Phase 1 and the 12 concepts / 16 relations of the Phase 2 curated graph — all `authored` + `unvalidated`, the structure's `sourceRef` stating it is not verified against an official syllabus. No other section, no terminology, no skills, no pattern references and no importance data were invented (empty/null, tested). `IPMAT_QUANT_CHAPTERS` now lives in the pack and `seed.ts` consumes it, so seed and pack cannot drift.
8. **Concept identity.** A concept name must be unique within a pack after `normalizeConceptNameKey()` (D-030; "Percentage" and "Percentages" stay distinct). This is stricter than the database's `(chapterId, name)` uniqueness; a database holding the same name in two chapters of one exam assembles into a pack that fails validation rather than being silently merged.
9. **Persistence: no migration.** `PrismaExamPackRepository` (read-only) assembles a pack from the existing tables. The database stores no provenance/review record for exam structure, so everything read back is reported `unvalidated` — persistence never upgrades a claim. A relation whose endpoint is in ANOTHER exam is loaded with an `external:` endpoint so validation reports `cross_exam_relation`; the service then fails closed for both exams. The database cannot forbid such a row without a trigger; a trigger was rejected as a heavier, harder-to-reverse change than read-time detection for a foundation unit, and this gap is documented, not hidden.
10. **Application layer.** `ExamPackService` over an `ExamPackRepository` interface (in-memory implementation included): sections, syllabus tree/path, concepts, direct prerequisites/unlocks, ancestors/descendants with hop distances, relations, neighbors (direction-tagged), deterministic learning order, validation report and provenance summary. It validates every loaded pack (memoized per object) and throws `ExamPackInvalidError` instead of answering from an invalid pack; unknown keys are typed `ExamPackQueryError`s, never empty answers. All results are explicitly sorted (never input/hash order) and traversals terminate on cyclic non-ordering graphs.
11. **No HTTP route, no UI.** Nothing student-visible changed. `toPublicExamPackView()` is the student-safe projection (names, descriptions, order, relation type; no keys, provenance, review state, certainty, source, status, rationale or notes — tested by string scan); exposing it over HTTP is a later, deliberate step.
12. **Property tests without a new dependency.** `fast-check` is not installed; a seeded mulberry32 generator (the seed is part of the test name, so a failure is reproducible) drives 60-seed properties: generated DAG packs validate; results are input-order independent; ancestry/descent are duals; the learning order is topological; injecting a back edge always yields `forbidden_cycle`; `findCycle` agrees with a brute-force reachability check.

**Alternatives rejected.** Adding Prisma tables/columns for provenance, pack version or deeper hierarchy now (no consumer yet; deferred, see limitations); a ninth "unlocks" relation type; cycle checking across all relation types (rejects the real curated data); labelling the seeded IPMAT structure `canonical` (no official source exists); a database trigger for cross-exam rows; fuzzy concept-name folding; a student-facing concept explorer.

**Known limitations.** Only the top syllabus level (chapters) is persistable today; deeper nesting exists in the model and tests only. Pack provenance for database-assembled packs is a blanket `unvalidated`. Exam-specific terminology, skills, pattern references and importance are representable but empty for IPMAT. The non-Percentages chapters have no concepts. Cross-exam rows are detected at read time, not prevented at write time. `packVersion` is a free-form string with no migration/compatibility semantics yet.

**Not claimed.** That the IPMAT pack matches the official syllabus; that any relation is reviewed; that the concept universe is complete (only "known/mapped" language, D-007); any student-facing behavior change.

## D-083 — Product Phase 6 Prompt 2: Historical Examiner Intelligence (`@ipmat/examiner-intelligence`) and Question DNA as a validated, exam-scoped representation; historical evidence is observed testing evidence, NOT a prediction

**THE CRITICAL PRINCIPLE: HISTORICAL EVIDENCE IS OBSERVED TESTING EVIDENCE, NOT A PREDICTION OF FUTURE EXAM CONTENT.** That a pattern, combination, novelty level or trap was observed in validated historical material supports exactly one claim — "observed in validated historical material". It never supports "likely to appear again" or "important for the next exam". Nothing in this layer scores, ranks, forecasts or estimates likelihood, and no type has a field that could carry such a claim (tested by a scan of the types and the query/service code).

**Context.** The repository already had Question DNA: the `QuestionDnaData` type, the normalized `Question` columns (`noveltyLevel`, `examRelevance`, `testingModes`, `trapErrorTaxonomyId`, `difficultyDimensions`, ...), `validateQuestionDna()` (name-based, against a `ConceptGraph`), the six-dimension `DifficultyDimensions` struct (D-021, provisional), the ten-value `TestingMode` vocabulary and the four-value `NoveltyLevel` (`standard`, `novel_representation`, `novel_combination`, `novel_context` — semantics unchanged). It did NOT have: any historical/PYQ model (a repository-wide search found none and the repository holds NO authorized historical exam material), DNA validation against an Exam Pack, a way to distinguish a model's classification proposal from a reviewed one, or exam-scoped, deterministic queries over observed testing.

**Decision.**
1. **Four things stay apart.** Exam knowledge space = `@ipmat/exam-pack`; observed historical testing space = a `HistoricalQuestionRecord`; question representation = Question DNA; student evidence = attempts/training (not referenced by this package at all). No combined object, no combined score.
2. **No new vocabulary.** `HistoricalDnaClassification = Omit<QuestionDnaData, "provenanceSourceType" | "validationState" | "examRelevance">` — exactly the existing DNA, minus three fields that mean something else here (a practice question's publication lifecycle, and the editorial relevance label, carried separately with its basis). The six difficulty dimensions and four novelty levels are restated only as exhaustive `Record<Union, true>` maps (an upstream change fails to compile) and a test proves nothing was invented. "Transformation" is the existing `testingModes` vocabulary (the same one `QuestionUniverseSnapshot.transformationsInUse` already uses); no "transformation complexity" dimension was added (a test rejects an invented dimension).
3. **Pattern family != question instance.** The classification NAMES a pattern family (structure) and holds no description or numeric instance of one; the record is the instance. Queries answer "which patterns for this concept" and "which records belong to this pattern" separately.
4. **A record is a CLASSIFICATION record, never a copy of the question.** It has a locator (exam version, year, session, a "Q23"-style label) and structured metadata but NO body, options, answer key, solution or explanation — in the TypeScript type AND the database table (tested by column inspection). Copyrighted content is never persisted; free-to-access is not free-to-copy.
5. **Provenance and rights are mandatory.** `source` = `sourceType` (the existing `Provenance.sourceType` vocabulary), a required `sourceRef`, `licenseRef` (required unless `public_domain`), `attributedTo`. `original` is rejected for a real source (it means "authored by this project").
6. **Real source vs fixture is never ambiguous.** `dataOrigin: "real_source" | "fixture"`. A fixture must carry a `fixtureLabel`, source type `original` and a `fixture:` source reference; a real source must carry none of that. Default queries return real-source data only. **Currently populated with real data: nothing.** The repository contains no authorized historical IPMAT material, so no row exists outside labelled test fixtures, nothing was seeded, and no "PYQ" was invented. This layer is a contract + schema + queries, ready for authorized data.
7. **Three annotation states** (`raw_imported` -> `candidate_annotation` -> `reviewed_validated`) with enforced consistency: a raw import has no classification; a candidate has a classification and authorship (`human` | `ai_assisted`, the latter naming its proposer) and NO review; `reviewed_validated` requires a named reviewer and an ISO date. An AI proposal cannot become authoritative without a review record; re-annotating a reviewed record drops the stale review; transitions are pure functions (`proposeAnnotation`, `recordReview`) with no clock. Default queries count only `reviewed_validated`; candidates and raw imports require an explicit opt-in, and a raw import never contributes a facet.
8. **Exam relevance.** The existing `ExamRelevance` (`core`/`peripheral`/`stretch`) is an EDITORIAL label, never evidence or prediction. It is carried only as `editorialRelevance` with a rationale and annotator. Historical evidence is DERIVED by counting reviewed records (`summarizeObservedTesting`), never stored on a record. Future-paper prediction is not implemented and no field exists for it.
9. **Validation against the exam's own pack** (`validateDnaClassification`, `validateHistoricalRecord`): exam match; section/chapter/concept exist; the concept is located under the claimed chapter in the pack; subconcepts/prerequisites/combination concepts exist (normalized-name match, never fuzzy); pattern family exists for that concept; trap is an existing `ErrorTaxonomy` code or null (no free-form trap strings); difficulty dimensions finite in [0,1] with no unknown keys; expected time a positive integer; testing modes known and unique. "Contradictory metadata" is limited to claims that contradict themselves BY DEFINITION: a concept combined with or prerequisite of itself; `combined` testing mode or `novel_combination` novelty with no combination concepts. Difficulty is deliberately NOT cross-checked against time, novelty or testing modes: long != hard, novel != difficult, time-pressured != hard (tested). The rules were checked against every existing Phase 3.5 DNA fixture (all pass), and an earlier candidate rule ("combination concepts imply the `combined` mode") was dropped because existing data contradicts it.
10. **Deterministic, exam-scoped queries.** Pure functions (and `ExaminerIntelligenceService`) answer: patterns for a concept, combinations with evidence, transformations/novelty/traps/difficulty tiers observed, observed range of each difficulty dimension and expected time (reported separately, flagged provisional, no composite score), records for a pattern, and the source supporting a record. Every query requires an `examCode`; results are explicitly sorted; `summarizeObservedTesting` exposes every facet (concept, pattern, combination, transformation, novelty, difficulty, trap, time) a later coverage engine needs. The coverage engine itself is NOT built.
11. **Cross-exam isolation, in layers.** Records carry `examCode`; the repository interface has no unscoped read; the service validates a record against its OWN exam's pack (and fails closed on an invalid pack); the Prisma adapter resolves names only within the record's exam (a name that exists only elsewhere is `missing_reference`), filters every read by exam code and refuses to move an id between exams (`ownership_mismatch`). Tested at domain level (including identical concept names in two exams) and on real Postgres.
12. **Persistence: migration 0013 (additive only).** One table, `historical_question_records`, and three enums; it reuses the existing enums (`SourceType`, `DifficultyTier`, `NoveltyLevel`, `TestingMode`, `ExamRelevance`) and `Restrict` foreign keys (the D-060 discipline). Hand-written CHECK constraints (source reference present; fixture-vs-real-source; raw has no classification; classified is complete; review matches state; authorship/proposer; editorial all-or-none; plausible year) enforce the invariants even if the application layer is bypassed — each proven by a rejected insert whose error names an `hqr_` constraint. Reversal is manual and controlled: drop the table, then the three types. No existing table is altered.
13. **No HTTP route, no UI.** Nothing student-visible changed and no app imports the package (grep-verified), so provenance, review metadata, classification reasoning and source references are not exposed at any student boundary. Exposing any of it later requires an authorization-scoped admin boundary, a separate decision.

**Alternatives rejected.** Storing question text/options (copyright); a single "exam relevance score" or a likelihood field (prediction); a new difficulty dimension or novelty level; a free-text trap; letting an AI-proposed classification count by default; seeding invented "historical" IPMAT questions; a second DNA type; a DB trigger for cross-table exam consistency (enforced in the adapter and tested instead).

**Known limitations.** No real historical data exists, so every real-data claim is untested against real material; the Prisma adapter's "concept belongs to this exam" check is application-level, not a database constraint; the pattern-family list used for validation is supplied by the caller (only Percentages families exist); `candidate`/`raw` records are storable but have no review UI; difficulty dimensions on historical records are annotations, not calibrated measurements; no coverage engine, admin boundary or import pipeline exists.

**Not claimed.** That IPMAT has a historical knowledge base; that any historical frequency predicts a future paper; that any classification is correct beyond its review state; any student-facing behavior change.

## D-084 — Product Phase 6 Prompt 3: Question Universe + content authoring + layered validation gates + publication gates (`@ipmat/content-authoring`); migration 0014

**Context.** The repository already had a generation pipeline (`runGenerationPipeline`), a candidate import boundary (D-050: only a pipeline-`validated` candidate becomes a persisted `ai_validated` row), a human publish/reject decision (`decidePublication`, D-008/D-048), the persisted `ValidationState` (`draft | ai_validated | human_reviewed | published | rejected`), a published-requires-provenance DB CHECK, a pattern-family readiness ladder (`computePatternFamilyReadiness`, per-cell coverage, `QuestionUniverseSnapshot`) and deterministic validators (`verifyComputation`, `compareReverification`, `validateSingleCorrectAnswer`, `validateNoAnswerLeakageInStem`, `checkDuplicateRisk`). It did NOT have: any path for a HUMAN-authored question; any code that sets `human_reviewed` or records a reviewer; a layered, machine-readable account of WHY a question is or is not publishable; an answer-trust rule for AI content outside the pipeline; a stable-identity/duplicate rule stronger than "same cell + exact stem" (D-050 explicitly deferred a DB guarantee); or a universe model that distinguishes pattern coverage from question count.

**Decision.**
1. **Five things stay apart.** CONCEPT (a pack node) / PATTERN FAMILY (a structure) / QUESTION DNA (how one question maps into that space) / QUESTION INSTANCE (identity, DNA, source, state) / QUESTION CONTENT (wording, options, answer, solution). `AuthoredQuestion` carries DNA and content side by side as separately validated parts; there is no flattened record.
2. **No second vocabulary.** `QuestionInstanceDna = Omit<QuestionDnaData, "provenanceSourceType" | "validationState">` (the Prompt 2 DNA; the two omitted fields live ONCE on the instance as `source` and `validationState`). The lifecycle is the EXISTING `ValidationState`; nothing was added to it. "Structured" is not a stored state: it is the `structure`/`metadata`/`dna` gates passing, derived on every evaluation.
3. **Layered gates, not one boolean** (`evaluateGates`): `structure`, `metadata`, `dna`, `concept`, `pattern`, `difficulty_novelty`, `expected_time`, `answer`, `identity`, `provenance`, `review`. Each reports `passed | failed | requires_human | not_applicable` with stable machine-readable reason codes. The DNA/concept/pattern/difficulty/time gates REUSE Prompt 2's `validateDnaClassification`, partitioned by what each establishes. `publishable` means "no known gate blocks publication" — never "exam-faithful" or "pedagogically good". Schema-valid != exam-valid != pedagogically good, and the report claims no more.
4. **Answer correctness is never taken on a generator's word.** A deterministic derivation is recomputed by `verifyComputation` (arithmetic-only allowlist, D-018/D-028); a mismatch FAILS and no reviewer can override a failed recomputation. When no derivation exists the gate is `requires_human` (explicit, never skipped) until a named reviewer states they verified the answer. For `ai_generated` and `unknown` origin a matching derivation alone is NOT enough: an independent re-derivation (`compareReverification`) or a reviewer's verification is also required. An unparseable stated answer fails closed (D-024).
5. **Authoring lifecycle** (pure functions, caller-supplied time, no mutation): `createDraft` / `proposeAiQuestion` (a draft; an AI proposal is only a draft with `origin: "ai_generated"`) -> `runAutomatedValidation` (`draft -> ai_validated` iff no gate FAILED; open `requires_human` gates do not block this step but do block publication) -> `recordHumanReview` (`ai_validated -> human_reviewed | rejected`; approval is refused while any gate failed; the review record — reviewer, time, internal notes, `answerVerifiedByReviewer`, `reviewedAsDistinct` — is stored) -> `publishQuestion` (the ONLY path to `published`). `editQuestion` invalidates everything: the question returns to `draft`, a prior review is dropped, the id never changes; published/rejected questions are immutable. `rejected` and `published` are terminal.
6. **Publication gate.** `publishQuestion` re-evaluates every gate at the moment of publication (nothing stale is trusted), requires `publishable`, and then applies the EXISTING `decidePublication` (the same rule the persisted publication repository uses): Hard/Extreme/Novel need human review; a Provenance record must exist. Content cannot become published because it was authored, AI-generated, has valid JSON, or has syntactically valid DNA (tested, including a 60-seed random-walk property: published => no failed or open gate; AI-generated => independent check or reviewer-verified answer; Hard/Extreme/Novel => a review record; terminal states are final; the id never changes).
7. **AI authoring rules.** The package never calls a model and names no vendor (tested); an AI proposal is INPUT, so any provider can produce it and the provider layer stays replaceable. AI output is never published automatically and carries a recorded origin. No model is asked to make claims about a student.
8. **Provenance and rights.** Reuses the existing source vocabulary. `original` needs no external reference; anything else needs a `sourceRef`; anything except `public_domain` also needs a `licenseRef` (free-to-access is not free-to-copy); AI-generated content must reference its generation record. A deliberately narrow guard refuses a source reference that names a known unauthorized-distribution channel (Telegram, torrent, libgen, ...): it is NOT a rights determination, only a refusal to ingest the obvious.
9. **Identity.** A question's id is assigned once and never changes through authoring, validation, edit, review or publication. `computeContentFingerprint` (sha256 of exam + normalized wording + order-independent normalized options; digits and `. % -` preserved) identifies "the same question typed twice" — never "a similar question". An exact duplicate returns the EXISTING question (idempotent create) and fails the `identity` gate if it reaches validation; a NEAR-duplicate (the existing token-overlap check, D-019 — not semantic dedup) is `requires_human` and is NEVER merged automatically; the reviewer's explicit `reviewedAsDistinct` settles it. Identity is exam-scoped. The partial unique index `questions_exam_content_fingerprint_unique` is the database backstop (migration 0014); a rejected question never blocks re-authoring the same wording; editing into another question's exact content is a typed `conflict`.
10. **Question Universe** (`buildConceptUniverse` / `buildExamQuestionUniverse`, pure, derived on every call, nothing stored): per concept, mapped pattern families (zero rows kept), combinations (mapped from the Exam Pack graph — the same combinable types `getCombinationCandidates` uses — plus observed), transformations (the whole `TestingMode` vocabulary), novelty (all four levels), difficulty tiers (all five), traps (mapped plus observed), expected-time bands, and per-dimension low/mid/high counts for the six EXISTING difficulty dimensions (separate; no composite score). Each region reports `total` (non-rejected, any state) AND `published` (the trainable pool) and is `empty | underrepresented | represented`. **Pattern coverage != question count:** each pattern also reports `distinctSignatures` (how many different structural signatures its published instances span), `topSignatureShare` and `concentrated` — 100 published questions of one structure show one signature and are flagged concentrated. Only this exam's questions are counted. "Mapped space" language only; there is no completeness field (D-007).
11. **PROVISIONAL, centralized constants** (`UNIVERSE_THRESHOLDS`, `TIME_DEMAND_BANDS`): a region with 1–2 published instances is "underrepresented"; a pattern is "concentrated" at >= 8 published with one signature >= 60%; expected-time bands 45/90/150 s. Uncalibrated; they only LABEL a region, never judge a question. Time demand is not difficulty (long != hard, novel != difficult, time-pressured != hard — no cross-checks between them).
12. **Persistence: migration 0014 (additive, data-preserving).** Eight NULLABLE columns on `questions` (`authoring_origin` — new enum `QuestionAuthoringOrigin`; `content_fingerprint`; the review record; `independent_reverification_answer`) plus the partial unique index and CHECKs (review columns all-or-none; a fingerprint requires a stated origin). "`human_reviewed` carries a review record" is deliberately NOT a database CHECK: the Phase 5 integration suite (and any pre-existing flow) legitimately holds `human_reviewed` rows with no review record, so such a constraint broke existing behavior when tried (found during verification, removed); the authoring `review` gate enforces it instead. Existing rows keep NULL everywhere and read back as origin `unknown`, which is NEVER guessed to be human and is treated as cautiously as `ai_generated`. Verified on real Postgres: the 3 seeded published questions survive unchanged. Reversal is manual and controlled (drop the index/constraints, the columns, the enum).
13. **Repositories.** `QuestionAuthoringRepository` (domain interface + in-memory implementation) and `PrismaQuestionAuthoringRepository` (Serializable transactions). Names resolve to rows INSIDE the question's own exam (cell resolution mirrors the importer; a cell is looked up, never created); a question never moves between exams. It is INTERNAL (answer-bearing). The student readers are unchanged: `select`-based, published-only, and never select the new columns.
14. **No route, no UI, no CMS.** Nothing student-visible changed. The leakage audit runs against the real HTTP server on real Postgres: unpublished questions (draft / ai_validated / human_reviewed) cannot be started, recommended or listed; a published question leaks no answer key before submission and no reviewer/source/provenance/fingerprint/origin data ever; no student route reads authoring data.

**Alternatives rejected.** One big `isValid` boolean; a new lifecycle enum (the existing one suffices); trusting an AI answer because it recomputes; a reviewer override of failed arithmetic; auto-merging near-duplicates; a numeric difficulty score; a stored coverage/breadth column (derive, don't cache); a generation engine or admin CMS (out of scope); guessing legacy rows are human-authored; a DB CHECK that `human_reviewed` requires a review (tried, and it broke existing Phase 5 test data and flows — see item 12).

**Known limitations.** The persisted vocabulary names the "automated gates passed" state `ai_validated` even for human-authored content (established name; documented, not renamed). Near-duplicate detection is token overlap, not semantic (D-019), and in practice flags unrelated-but-similarly-worded questions for a human. Only Percentages pattern families and taxonomy cells exist, and authoring requires an existing taxonomy cell (never created). The importer (D-050) does not yet write fingerprints, so a pipeline-imported question is protected from duplicates by the gate, not the index. The domain's `GateContext.existing` is supplied by the caller. The unauthorized-source guard is a narrow marker check. Thresholds are provisional. No review UI/CMS, no authoring HTTP route, no real authored question corpus: every test question is a labelled fixture and nothing was seeded.

**Not claimed.** That any question is exam-faithful or pedagogically good; that a passed gate set implies quality; that the universe is complete; that a historical question is publishable or an authored one historical (the two layers stay separate); any prediction of future exam content.

## D-085 — Product Phase 6 Prompt 4: the Content Intelligence pipeline, knowledge graph and retrieval abstraction (`@ipmat/content-intelligence`); migration 0015

**Context.** Phase 6 Prompt 4 turns AUTHORIZED source material into structured, traceable exam intelligence. A repository-wide search found NO existing ingestion, source registry, document parser, chunking, embedding, vector or retrieval code (the only references were the token-overlap duplicate check, D-019, and a MASTER_PLAN non-goal). What existed and WAS reused: the `Provenance`/`SourceType` rights vocabulary, the content-authoring rights rule and lifecycle (Prompt 3), the Exam Pack and its eight typed relations (Prompts 1/D-013), the real-source/fixture distinction (Prompt 2), and the project rule that every AI call goes through `@ipmat/ai` `generateStructured` with a Zod schema. MASTER_PLAN had listed "a full coaching-material ingestion pipeline" as not-to-be-built; this unit is the owner-directed, narrower thing — a pipeline for AUTHORIZED sources only — and coaching-material/pirated/Telegram/torrent content remains permanently out (the pipeline refuses it structurally).

**Decision.**
1. **Rights are a gate at the door, not a label afterwards.** A source is registered only if it passes the SAME rules that guard a question's provenance (`evaluateSourceRights`, extracted from the Prompt 3 gate and shared, not copied): original needs no external reference; anything else needs a traceable `sourceRef`; anything except `public_domain` needs a `licenseRef` AND a named `authority` (owner); a reference naming a known unauthorized-distribution channel (Telegram, torrent, libgen, ...) is refused. A source that fails is REFUSED (`unauthorized_source`) and NOTHING is persisted — it never enters the pipeline. The same rules are database CHECKs (`content_sources_rights`, `..._no_unauthorized_marker`, `..._fixture_vs_real_source`), so they hold even if the application layer is bypassed (tested by direct inserts). free-to-access != free-to-copy.
2. **Source vs version.** `SourceRecord` has a deterministic id from (exam, key): re-registering returns the same source. A `SourceVersionRecord` is identified by the sha256 of the exact content (deterministic id from source + hash): the same content is the same version and does no work; changed content (even whitespace) is a NEW version. A version is immutable once created; a new version never overwrites earlier validated intelligence (tested: decided candidates and chunks of version 1 survive a failed and a successful version 2). Raw documents are NOT stored — only the hash and the extracted chunks (resuming from an early stage re-supplies the content and verifies it against the hash).
3. **Ingestion lifecycle.** `registered -> accepted -> extracted -> normalized -> chunked -> enriched -> reviewed -> available`, plus `failed` remembering the stage. Each stage is idempotent; a failing stage records a stable code and a message that NEVER contains source content, stops, and touches nothing already validated; running again resumes from the failed stage and converges on exactly the same rows (deterministic ids; candidates inserted without overwrite). `reviewed` requires every candidate to be decided by a person; `available` requires `reviewed`; a reviewed/available version's chunks are locked. Rights are re-checked at the `accepted` stage (nothing stale is trusted).
4. **Extraction is deterministic and honest about scope.** `extractDocument` (no model, no network, no clock) supports `plain_text` and `markdown` only: headings (with heading path), paragraphs, pipe tables (rows) and question boundaries. There was no parser in the repository; PDF/OCR/other formats are NOT supported and fail with `unsupported_format` (recorded as a failed version), never silently guessed. Every block keeps its location (1-based lines, character span in the normalized text, heading path); nothing is dropped (a property test proves every non-blank line is covered). A numbered line is a question ONLY if at least two lettered options follow; the source's own "Answer:" line is kept as a CLAIM, never verified here.
5. **Semantic chunks are structural, deterministic and traceable.** A heading starts a chunk and travels with what follows; a detected question is always its own chunk; blocks are packed to `maxChars`; an over-long paragraph splits at sentence boundaries; every chunk is exactly its span of the normalized text with a text hash. Chunk ids are sha256(version, algorithm version, max size, ordinal, text hash): the same input always yields the same ids. A chunk existing says nothing about being correct or reviewed (`validationState: unreviewed`).
6. **Everything extracted is a CANDIDATE.** Concept mentions, relationships and questions have `state: candidate | accepted | rejected`, a proposer (`deterministic | ai_assisted | human`) and, once decided, a named reviewer. Accepting a concept mention validates a MENTION LINK (chunk -> concept) and never edits the Concept Universe; a proposal for a concept that is not in the Exam Pack (`conceptKey: null`) can only be left or rejected — `canonical_concept_protected`. Matching is exact normalized-name only (D-030, "Percentage" != "Percentages"). Relationship candidates may use ONLY the eight established relation types — `example-of`, `tested-by`, `appears-in`, `related-to`, `depends-on`, ... are rejected as `unknown_relation_type`; new types are never invented from extracted text — and need a rationale. `proposeRelationPromotion` turns an ACCEPTED relationship into a pack-relation PROPOSAL (provenance `inferred`/`reviewed` for a model, `authored` for a person; certainty `probable`, never `confirmed` from a candidate) plus what `validateExamPack` says about the pack WITH it (a prerequisite cycle is caught); the pipeline never applies it. Decisions are final and a re-run never reopens or overwrites a decided candidate.
7. **Evidence is verified, never trusted.** Every candidate carries evidence — chunk, verbatim quote, span — and `verifyEvidence` requires the quote to be EXACTLY the chunk text at that span, in a chunk of that same version. A provider cannot invent a quote (tested with hostile provider output: invented quotes, unknown concepts and unestablished relation types never become candidates; they are reported as `proposalProblems`).
8. **Provider abstraction, no vendor.** `ConceptExtractionProvider` / `RelationshipExtractionProvider` are interfaces returning RAW, untrusted proposals (strings); the pipeline turns them into candidates through the validators. The default `DeterministicConceptProvider` is whole-word name matching against the pack; `StaticExtractionProvider` is the scripted test double. A model-backed provider would implement the same interfaces through `@ipmat/ai` `generateStructured` + a Zod schema (the project rule); NONE is built in this unit, the package imports no AI SDK and names no vendor (tested). Where extraction can be deterministic, it is.
9. **Question extraction enters the existing authoring lifecycle and nothing else.** `questionDraftFromCandidate` requires the candidate to have been ACCEPTED by a reviewer as a correct extraction and returns the INPUT to `ContentAuthoringService.createDraft` — there is no path from here to `published` (no code in the package produces that state or imports a publication function; tested). The stem/options/answer-claim are the source's text; no solution is invented (the structure gate says so), no derivation is attached (the `answer` gate requires a human), DNA must be supplied and goes through the DNA gates unchanged, provenance is derived from the registered source, and an AI-proposed classification gets the AI caution. Re-extracting names the same question (deterministic id) and the authoring fingerprint backstops duplicates.
10. **Knowledge graph: derived, typed, directional, evidenced.** `buildKnowledgeGraph` derives from the pack, pattern families, questions, sources/versions/chunks and candidates (never stored, so it cannot drift): Exam -> Section -> Chapter (`has_section`/`has_chapter`), Concept `located_in` Chapter, Concept `has_pattern` Pattern, Question `instance_of` Pattern / `about` Concept, Source Version `version_of` Source, Chunk `derived_from` Version, Chunk `mentions` Concept, and `concept_relation` edges that carry one of the EIGHT relation types. There is NO `relatedTo[]`; the edge kinds are a fixed set with endpoint rules (`EDGE_RULES`) enforced by `validateGraph` (dangling endpoints, cross-exam edges, wrong endpoint kinds, duplicate ids, invalid relation types, missing evidence). Every edge has a `basis`: `canonical` (pack/structured), `accepted_candidate` (extracted AND reviewed) or `candidate` (extracted, unreviewed — only on request); rejected candidates and proposals for non-pack concepts NEVER appear. `whyRelated(A, B)` answers "why does the system believe A and B are related?" with structured provenance: the pack's own provenance for a canonical relation, or source, version, location, verbatim quote, proposer kind and reviewer for an extracted one. The graph is INTERNAL (its evidence holds source quotes).
11. **STRUCTURED INTELLIGENCE IS AUTHORITATIVE; EMBEDDINGS ARE RETRIEVAL AIDS.** `RetrievalIndex` returns ranked chunk IDS only; metadata filters (exam, fixture origin, rejected chunks) are applied BEFORE scoring; the lexical BM25-style index is the deterministic baseline; `VectorRetrievalIndex` sits behind an `EmbeddingProvider` interface (any vendor or local model can implement it) and stores vectors + metadata, never text. `HashingEmbeddingProvider` is a deterministic stand-in labelled "not a semantic model" so the vector path is testable with no vendor. No vector database was introduced. A hit is resolved to content ONLY by `EvidenceRetriever`, which checks the principal FIRST (only `content_admin` / `content_reviewer` for that exam; `student` and `anonymous` are always denied) and re-resolves every hit through the exam-scoped structured repository, so an id from another exam, a deleted chunk or a chunk rejected after indexing simply disappears — an embedding or a poisoned index can never bypass authorization (tested). Embeddings are never the source of truth for concept identity, relationships, DNA, publication, rights or validation. This is NOT a chat product and there is no student tutor.
12. **Persistence: migration 0015 (additive, data-preserving).** Five enums and five NEW tables (`content_sources`, `content_source_versions`, `content_chunks`, `content_candidates`, `content_candidate_evidence`); no existing table is altered. Hand-written CHECKs: source key/rights/unauthorized-marker/fixture-vs-real-source, version shape and failure-iff-failed, chunk shape, candidate decision all-or-none, evidence shape. Verified on real Postgres: applies from an empty database, applies over a database already holding data (3 published questions, relations, a student unchanged), and a shadow-database diff shows no drift. `PrismaContentIntelligenceRepository` is idempotent and concurrency-safe (two parallel ingests of the same content converge on one version), protects decided candidates and locked chunks, and scopes every read by exam. A defect found during verification: the first draft restricted `format` to the supported values in a CHECK, which made an unsupported-format failure unrepresentable (the pipeline crashed instead of recording it); the CHECK now only requires a declared, non-blank format.
13. **No HTTP route, no UI, no CMS, no tutor.** Nothing student-visible changed. The leakage audit runs against the real HTTP server on real Postgres with sentinel-bearing source text, chunks, candidates and an extracted unpublished question: none appears in any raw response (start/refusal, recommendation, practice start/submit/result, training hub) and no student route reaches the pipeline.

**Alternatives rejected.** Storing raw documents; one big "enrich with an LLM" step; letting a model's output become canonical or auto-accepted; new relation types for `example-of`/`tested-by`/`appears-in` (those are not concept relations — the structural edges `mentions`/`about`/`instance_of` carry what they meant); a vendor SDK or vector database in this unit; embeddings as a source of truth; a PDF/OCR dependency (no authorized corpus exists to justify it); auto-publishing extracted questions; storing the graph (derive, don't cache); letting a student role read source content.

**Known limitations.** Only `plain_text` and `markdown` are extractable; no PDF/OCR. Question detection is a conservative heuristic (lettered options after a numbered line). The sole enrichment providers are deterministic (name matching, question boundaries); no model-backed provider exists, so relationship extraction has only the interface and a scripted test double. The hashing embedder is a stand-in, not semantic. The retrieval index is in-memory and rebuilt per exam (`indexExam`); there is no persistent vector store. Content-staff principals are a domain type only — no authentication for staff exists yet, so any future route must build the principal from real auth. A relation promotion is only a PROPOSAL; there is no code that writes it into the canonical Concept Universe. The DB cannot check that candidate evidence quotes match chunk text (the domain verifies at creation). The unauthorized-source guard is a narrow marker check, not a rights determination. Chunk-level review (`validationState`) has a setter but no workflow.

**Not claimed.** That any extraction is correct or any candidate true; that the graph is complete (only known/mapped); that a hashing embedding is semantic; that an extracted question is publishable; any real-exam content (every test document is a synthetic labelled fixture); any prediction of future exam content.

## D-086 — Product Phase 6 Prompt 5: Exam Intelligence integration + calibration architecture (`@ipmat/exam-intelligence`); no migration, no route

**Decision.** One new PURE domain package composes the Phase 6 layers into deterministic, auditable services — coverage, queries, calibration status and a question-selection bridge — without becoming a model, a score or a predictor. Five layers stay separate: exam model, content model, historical evidence, student evidence (NOT read here), intelligence/selection. `@ipmat/db` supplies a read-only `PrismaExamIntelligenceSource` and `PrismaOutcomeSource`.

**Coverage.** Coverage = the fraction of a NAMED, MAPPED universe with at least one qualifying item, with an explicit denominator/numerator per facet (concept, pattern, combination, transformation, difficulty tier, difficulty dimension, novelty, trap, time demand). No overall coverage number. States: `measured` / `no_universe` (nothing mapped; no invented ratio) / `insufficient_data` (historical basis with no counted record). Members report count AND distinct structures; empty, under-represented and concentrated regions are surfaced. Content tiers nest (available ⊇ validated ⊇ published); the historical basis (reviewed, real-source, valid only) is separate and never mixed. Thresholds reuse the provisional `UNIVERSE_THRESHOLDS`/`TIME_DEMAND_BANDS`.

**Data quality.** One disposition per question in fixed order: cross-exam, fixture, rejected, invalid metadata (existing `validateDnaClassification`), duplicate (content fingerprint). Excluded items are counted with reasons. A JEE row can never become IPMAT intelligence. Historical records are excluded as cross-exam / fixture / not-reviewed / invalid-classification, also counted.

**Calibration.** `provisional` (default), `observed` (enough descriptive aggregates from graded outcomes; hidden below the minimum; non-identifying), `calibrated` ONLY via an explicit sufficient `CalibrationRecord` (and sufficient measurements for outcome-measurable fields). Nothing is auto-calibrated; the minimums are PROVISIONAL. Expected-vs-observed time is a descriptive ratio, never `calibrated`, never rewrites an annotation, no ML.
