**Selection bridge.** `selectQuestions` is a monotone, ordered, traced intersection of exam-space constraints; unknown names are errors; results are DNA-level facts only. `restrictCandidates` intersects a provider's own candidates, preserving provider order. No provider was modified; tested against the real Revision provider.

**Why no migration / route.** Everything derives from existing tables on read (D-015); no student surface needs this yet.

**Alternatives rejected.** A single coverage percent; counting questions as coverage; auto-calibrating from attempt counts; blending historical frequency into a relevance score; storing coverage; rewriting providers to consume the layer.

**Known limitations.** No real historical data or calibrated difficulty exists. `CalibrationRecord`s have no persistence or workflow. Fixture detection on DB rows is by a `fixture:` provenance ref or `[TEST DATA` body marker. Duplicate detection is exact-content, not semantic (D-019).

**Not claimed.** That any annotation is calibrated; that coverage means readiness or likelihood of appearing; any prediction; population performance; that mapped means complete.

## D-087 — Product Phase 7 Unit 1: mastery EVIDENCE foundation (evidence-only; no verdict, no persistence, no route)

**Context.** Unit 1 asked for a Mastery Model. The repository defines no mastery verdict: no meaning of "mastered", threshold, category, unlock rule, recency, prerequisite, repair, revision or speed-to-mastery policy (D-040-D-043 define evidence-only component measures and explicitly decline composite scores and decay). The brief forbids inventing those semantics, so the unit stopped and asked; the owner approved the evidence-only default below.

**Decision.** Add a pure, derived evidence view, `buildMasteryEvidenceView()` in `@ipmat/mastery`, and a read-only composer over persisted attempts, `composeMasteryEvidenceView()` / `TrainingRecommendationService.readMasteryEvidence()`. Per concept it reports attempt counts ALONGSIDE distinct-question counts (never substituted), skipped/abandoned/ungraded attempts separate from graded ones, evidence indexed by pattern family, novelty level and testing mode, observed/expected time paired with correctness (no ratio or label), per-question exposure, and an auditable list of contributing attempt ids. It carries `status: "evidence_only"`.

**Rules kept.** No label, category, threshold, unlock rule or hidden/numeric score; no confidence/ability/motivation inference; no prediction; no prerequisite propagation; no recency/decay; no repair/diagnosis weighting; no Revision rule; no speed-to-mastery rule. The existing five-measure `computeMasteryState()` is unchanged and still counts attempts. An attempt id is one piece of evidence (duplicates are counted as excluded). Another student's or exam's records are excluded and counted, never merged.

**Scoping and persistence.** The composer reuses the existing ownership-verified, exam-scoped composition (published, DNA-complete pool; same-concept check) with its one possible write (RepairPlan status sync) disabled. Nothing is stored: no migration, no table, no `mastery_states` write; the view is recomputed from the `attempts` rows (canonical evidence) every call. No HTTP route and no UI exist; a future route must return only intentionally public fields.

**Alternatives rejected.** Substituting distinct-question counts into the existing measures (would silently change D-041); a mastery label with provisional thresholds; storing the view; a route for demonstration.

**Known limitations.** An attempt on a question no longer published drops out; the concept universe is concepts with published questions; mode buckets overlap; all-time unbounded history.

**Not claimed.** That any student has or lacks mastery of anything; that counts imply ability, confidence or readiness; any prediction.

## D-088 — Product Phase 7 Unit 2: Revision Intelligence (`@ipmat/revision-intelligence`); evidence-derived signals over the EXISTING providers, no priority, no persistence, no route

**Context.** Unit 2 asked for a layer that says what to revise, when, why and with which exposure type. The repository defines Revision (D-081: concept dormancy, provisional 3 graded attempts and 14 days) and each training provider's own rules, but no revision priority, no pattern- or mode-level revision type, no backlog notion and no strength/weakness verdict.

**Decision.** A new pure package composes: (1) Unit 1's evidence view, unchanged; (2) six threshold-free signal kinds - `dormant_concept` and `recurring_trap_failure` REUSE Revision's and Trap Lab's own rules and constants (never re-implemented), the others are exact facts (`attempts_exceed_distinct_questions`; pattern-family / novelty-level / testing-mode "without graded evidence" relative to the published pool, only for concepts with graded evidence; a skip is not graded evidence); (3) the raw outcome of each existing provider, run through the product's catalog-aware `runTrainingSystem()` on a context scoped to one student and exam (`scopeContextToExam`); (4) traced recommendations, only where a provider returned `selected` - provider explanation verbatim, linked signals, dimensions, contributing attempts, and the selected question re-checked against the exam's published pool (fail closed); (5) unserved signals with reasons; (6) preserved conflicts (`competing_revision_types`, `recurring_trap_with_correct_attempts`); (7) an explicit `priority: { defined: false }`.

**Rules kept.** The layer selects, filters and ranks no question; it adds no training semantics (exposure-type labels merely name what each existing provider does); no score, rank, verdict, confidence, ability, prediction, randomness or model call; no prerequisite propagation, recency weighting or repair weighting. Deterministic: same evidence, context and runs (any order) give the same result.

**Scoping and persistence.** `composeRevisionIntelligence()` / `TrainingRecommendationService.readRevisionIntelligence()` reuse the ownership-verified, exam-scoped composition with its one possible write (RepairPlan status sync) disabled. Nothing is stored: no migration, table or mastery write. No route and no UI.

**Unresolved (not invented).** Priority among revision needs; any pattern- or mode-level revision type; revision backlog, over-concentration, novel-question weakness, strength/weakness; treatment of a recurring trap with correct attempts; what repeated exposure should trigger; gap signals for never-attempted concepts; calibration of the provisional constants.

**Alternatives rejected.** A blended priority score or ranking; a new pattern-level provider; a stored revision-state table; resolving conflicts with an invented precedence rule; re-implementing dormancy/recurrence inside the new package.

**Known limitations.** Inherits the existing scoping (a no-longer-published question's attempts drop out; concept universe = concepts with published questions; all-time history). The existing Revision provider does not filter candidates by exam itself - the composer and `scopeContextToExam` guarantee isolation. `evaluatedAt` comes from the service clock.

**Not claimed.** Mastery, readiness, calibration, prediction, or any inference about confidence, ability, motivation or emotion.

## D-089 — Product Phase 7 Unit 3: Adaptive Curriculum (`@ipmat/adaptive-curriculum`); composition of existing orchestration, evidence and revision intelligence, no new ordering, no persistence, no route

**Context.** Unit 3 asked for curriculum orchestration across concepts and training systems. The repository specifies the Phase 5D orchestrator's single next action (D-062: confirmed repair, then the five providers in a fixed order, then adaptive practice) and keeps Revision outside that chain (D-081). It does not specify a "curriculum", a cross-concept order, a multi-step sequence, where Revision sits relative to repair/adaptive, or what "done"/"mastered" means.

**Decision.** A new pure package composes existing results and decides nothing: (1) the next action is `orchestrateNextTrainingAction`'s own result verbatim, re-verified against the exam's published pool (fail closed), with a fixed `whyThisTier` text naming the D-062 rule; (2) the chain lists every tier in the orchestrator's fixed order with that tier's own raw outcome and whether the orchestrator reached it; (3) steps are the selected question per tier in that order, with Revision outside the chain and no position defined for it; (4) a per-concept view carries Unit 1's evidence verbatim, Unit 2's signals and unserved needs, active repair target facts only, who serves the concept and optional Phase 6 content availability - never one number; (5) conflicts are preserved, each naming the existing rule that decides it (`existing_rule`) or marked `unresolved_product_decision`; (6) `sequencing.definedBeyondExistingChain` is false. `composeAdaptiveCurriculum()` / `TrainingRecommendationService.readAdaptiveCurriculum()` derive everything from one ownership-verified, exam-scoped read (RepairPlan status writer off); the Unit 2 composition was extracted behaviourally unchanged (`composeRevisionFromInput`) to share that read. An optional `examIntelligenceSource` dependency attaches Phase 6 availability only.

**Rules kept.** Nothing selects, filters, ranks or re-orders a question or a tier; no score, verdict, confidence, ability, ranking, prediction, randomness or model call; Phase 3 adaptive selection, Phase 5D orchestration, every provider, Unit 1 and Unit 2 are unchanged (tested). No table, migration, route or UI.

**Unresolved (not invented).** Cross-concept order; any multi-step sequence; Revision's position relative to repair/adaptive and whether revision waits for repair; completion/mastery semantics; prep-phase influence; pattern/mode-level needs.

**Alternatives rejected.** A blended priority across concepts; an invented multi-step sequence; ranking concepts by evidence; re-running the orchestrator to show hypothetical alternatives; a stored curriculum-state table.

**Known limitations.** Inherits the existing scoping limits; Pressure Training stays insufficient-evidence here; Phase 6 availability needs a supplied source (not wired into the API); `evaluatedAt` is the service clock.

**Not claimed.** Mastery, readiness, calibration, prediction, or any inference about confidence, ability, motivation or emotion.

## D-090 — Product Phase 7 Unit 4: Full Exam Simulation engine (`@ipmat/exam-simulation`); exam-rule-free mechanics, rules as data, migration 0016

**Context.** Unit 4 asked for a full exam simulation. The repository specifies NO exam rule for one: no IPMAT duration, section timing, section count/order, question counts, marking or negative marking, navigation or mark-for-review rule, pause/resume, or historical paper; the IPMAT pack holds the Quant section only, and PRODUCT_SPEC says exam structure is data. Earlier docs call Mock Simulation undesigned.

**Decision.** Build only the exam-rule-free mechanics, with every exam rule supplied as DATA and none shipped. (1) `SimulationConfig` (overall duration, sections by existing section name, question counts, provenance) is validated and supplied through a port; with no definition nothing starts (`no_simulation_configured`) - so no IPMAT simulation exists yet. (2) A paper is an editor's EXPLICIT per-section list of question ids, validated (published, this exam, right section, provenance, exact counts) and deterministic; always `origin: "assembled"`, never historical; each question records its content fingerprint and provenance source kind. (3) A pure state machine `in_progress -> submitted | expired` with an append-only answer log and pure decision functions. (4) Time is server-authoritative; the deadline is start + duration and EXCLUSIVE; expiry finalizes at the deadline on the next access; a submit racing the deadline is decided only by server time against it; finalization is idempotent. (5) One in-progress simulation per enrollment, recoverable across refresh/reconnect/restart. (6) The result is raw (per-question and per-section outcomes, counts, timing) with `scoring: { defined: false }` and `interpretation: "none"`; no score, because no marking scheme exists. (7) `toFinalizedSimulationEvidence()` is the explicit downstream contract for Unit 5, finalized simulations only; nothing calls it. (8) Persistence (migration 0016, three new tables, one enum): the config snapshot, the fixed paper with content versions, an append-only answer log with an acceptance sequence, and the finalized result written once; CHECK constraints tie finalization fields to status; a partial unique index enforces one in-progress simulation per enrollment; operations on one simulation are row-locked; answer keys are loaded before the lock and only when finalization needs them (loading them inside the lock exhausted the connection pool under concurrent submits).

**Rules kept.** No exam rule, score, readiness, mastery, confidence, ability or prediction. Simulation is separate from practice: it writes no attempt, practice session/block, training, repair, mastery or autopsy row, and imports nothing from those systems (boundary-tested); the same persisted attempts give identical Unit 1/2/3 reads and recommendation before, during and after a simulation (real Postgres). No route and no UI (none specified).

**Provisional engine mechanics (not exam rules, unspecified by any document).** Exclusive deadline; expiry at the deadline; the submit/expiry race rule; one in-progress simulation per enrollment; no pause/resume; no navigation enforcement; trimmed-string answer equality (mirrors the attempt rule).

**Unresolved (not invented).** The real IPMAT structure; the marking scheme; navigation and review rules; pause/accommodations; historical papers and generated-paper composition; post-finalization review; whether simulation evidence feeds readiness/revision/curriculum; limits on simulations per student; whether the boundary rules match the real exam.

**Alternatives rejected.** Hard-coding an IPMAT paper or scoring; automatic question selection; trusting a browser timer; a background expiry job (lazy expiry is deterministic and needs none); writing simulation answers as practice attempts; recomputing correctness from live content at read time (rejected for a stored result, since edits would change history).

**Known limitations.** Nothing can start until a configuration is supplied; no route/UI; no score; abandoned simulations stay `in_progress` in storage until touched (they can never accept an answer after the deadline); paper selection is manual.

**Not claimed.** That this is IPMAT-faithful, that any score or readiness exists, or any prediction.

## D-091 — Product Phase 7 Unit 5: Exam Simulation Intelligence + Readiness EVIDENCE (`@ipmat/simulation-intelligence`); readiness is five separate facts, never a number

**Context.** Unit 5 asked for the layer that interprets finalized simulation evidence beside training and exam-content evidence, and warned against inventing readiness. The audit found one authoritative statement: `PRODUCT_SPEC` section 3, the five readiness distinctions (syllabus completion, concept mastery, question-pattern coverage, advanced readiness, and four independent speed/accuracy/novelty/pressure axes), stored as distinct facts per (student, concept) and "not folded into one number". The repository defines no threshold, category, score, probability, verdict, simulation-to-practice feed, simulation comparison rule, marking scheme, "above exam difficulty" tier mapping, "seen" definition, or pressure classification of a timed simulation.

**Decision.** A pure package builds a readiness EVIDENCE report organised exactly around the five distinctions, per concept, from separate sources that are never merged. (1) FINALIZED-only: only `finalized_simulation_evidence_v1` of status submitted/expired for this student and exam is accepted; held by the Prisma reader (in-progress excluded in the query), Unit 4's export function and a runtime guard. (2) Per-simulation performance (outcomes, timing facts, no answer key or chosen answer); aggregation across simulations over the dimensions that exist in the contracts (section, concept, pattern family, novelty, testing mode, difficulty tier, trap code) with traceable ids. (3) Cross-simulation history ONLY within groups of identical exam, configuration version and ordered paper, as factual series (never "improved"); everything else is reported as not compared. (4) Objective observations (trap errors, repeated unanswered questions, deadline endings, unanswered sections, pattern families absent from simulations), each traceable and linked to a matching Unit 2 practice signal when one exists. (5) Read-only bridges: Unit 1 not rewritten, Unit 2 unchanged, Unit 3 curriculum shown verbatim with no reordering and no priority; simulation-derived observations are listed as unserved because existing systems read practice attempts only. (6) Phase 6 content availability and reviewed-historical-record counts are attached separately when a source is supplied. (7) `readiness: { defined: false }`, `interpretation: "none"`, and the full unresolved-policy list on every report. `composeExamPerformanceIntelligence()` / `readExamPerformanceIntelligence()` derive it on read from one ownership-verified, exam-scoped read (RepairPlan status writer off); the Unit 3 composer was split behaviour-unchanged (`buildCurriculumFromInput`) to share that read.

**Rules kept.** No score, percentage, probability, category, verdict, pass/selection likelihood, mastery score, confidence, ability, motivation or psychological inference; no prediction; no recency/decay/urgency or exam-date rule; no practice attempt is written from simulation data; nothing in Units 1-4 is mutated (real-Postgres tests show Units 1-3 and the recommendation byte-identical with and without finalized simulations). No migration, table, route or UI.

**Unresolved (not invented).** Any readiness threshold/category/score; which tiers are "above exam difficulty"; whether a timed simulation is "pressure performance"; what counts as "seen"; whether and how simulation evidence feeds practice evidence, revision, training systems or the curriculum; cross-paper/configuration comparison; the marking scheme; exam-date influence; limits on simulations and how many make a statement meaningful; whether any of this should be student-facing.

**Alternatives rejected.** A readiness percentage or "ready/almost ready" band; blending practice and simulation into one concept figure; ranking concepts or sections as weak/strong; comparing different papers; feeding simulation outcomes into practice attempts or training systems; a stored readiness-state table.

**Known limitations.** Question DNA comes from the currently published pool; strict, provisional comparability rule; no route/UI; no historical data exists (counts are zero); Pressure Training stays insufficient-evidence in the composition.

**Not claimed.** Readiness, mastery, calibration, any prediction or likelihood of any exam outcome, or any inference about confidence, ability, motivation or emotion.

## D-092 — Product Phase 8 Unit 1: AI Tutor foundation (`@ipmat/tutor`); a constrained intent-based layer, never a chatbot; no persistence, no route, no UI

**Context.** Phase 8 asks for a tutor grounded in the structured intelligence already built. The repository specifies NO tutoring behaviour: Phase 0 lists a "general-purpose AI chatbot" and a "voice tutor" as OUT of scope, and nothing defines hint levels, when an answer may be revealed, or how a tutor answer is shown. So this unit builds architecture and explicit, conservative, provisional contracts - it does not invent a tutoring product.

**Decision.** New pure domain package `@ipmat/tutor` (imports only `@ipmat/ai`, `@ipmat/concept-graph`, `@ipmat/content-intelligence`, `node:crypto`; tested). (1) EXPLICIT INTENTS, not free chat: `explain_question | explain_concept | give_hint | explain_mistake | clarify_solution`, each with a policy object (`TUTOR_INTENT_POLICIES`) fixing what context it needs and may carry. (2) CONTEXT FIREWALL (`buildTutorContext`): the exam comes from the verified enrollment, never the request; every port result is re-verified (enrollment owner, question published and in the exam, attempt owned by the student and question, evidence/source items of another student/exam dropped); "missing" and "someone else's" are one error; only what the intent needs is included. (3) THE KEY IS NEVER IN A PROMPT UNLESS AUTHORIZED: hints and concept explanations never receive it; `explain_question`/`explain_mistake`/`clarify_solution` receive it only once THIS student has a finalized attempt on THIS question. Withheld keys are held only as a `ProtectedKey` for the validator. Approach-revealing DNA (testing modes, trap label) is withheld before a finalized attempt; internal ids (taxonomy cell, trap code, student/attempt ids) are never serialized into a prompt; references are opaque (`question`, `attempt`, `answer_key`, `source:<n>`). (4) PROVIDER = the existing `@ipmat/ai` `AiProvider` + `generateStructured` (a new `tutor-response` task and Zod schema; timeout, retry, malformed handling already there); the tutor names no vendor, reads no environment, holds no key. (5) DETERMINISTIC GROUNDING VALIDATOR on every model output: references exist, hypotheses hedged and tied to the attempt, claimed concept relations are in the supplied graph, quotes verbatim, key/solution not asserted while withheld, no identifier or other-exam name, no psychological claim, no mastery/readiness/strength/weakness/outcome claim, no exam rule unless retrieved source text states it, attempt facts not misreported. Failure -> regenerate (bounded, cap 2, violation CODES fed back, never the rejected text) -> `rejected_ungrounded` with NO model text returned. (6) RESPONSE CONTRACT with epistemic classes (OBSERVED_DATA / SOURCE_CONTENT / DERIVED_EVIDENCE / AI_EXPLANATION / AI_HYPOTHESIS / UNRESOLVED), outcomes `answered | insufficient_context | rejected_ungrounded | provider_failure`, resolved references with provenance, and an audit entry that holds metadata only (no prompt, no response, no student text). No chain-of-thought field exists; the schema strips any extra key. (7) RETRIEVAL reuses `EvidenceRetriever` through `createEvidenceRetrieverSourcePort(retriever, principal)`; the principal is supplied by the caller and the retriever's existing rule (students ALWAYS denied) is not weakened, so a student request gets `sourceAccess: "denied"` and the tutor proceeds on structured context only. (8) `toStudentTutorView` is the only shape a future route may expose.

**Rules kept.** No chatbot, no persistence (no migration, table, memory or conversation history), no route, no UI, no live-model dependency in tests, no confidence/ability/mastery/readiness/psychological output, no key leakage, no cross-student or cross-exam context.

**Unresolved (not invented).** Hint levels and what each may reveal; whether the key may be shown before an attempt, or after a skip/abandon; which student evidence (revision, curriculum, simulation, confirmed autopsy) each intent may use (none do in Unit 1); whether source text may be quoted to students (corpus is internal); conversation memory and multi-turn behaviour; rate limits, quotas and budgets; tone/language/reading level; how an answer is displayed or reported; whether denied/failed tutor requests need an audit entry.

**Alternatives rejected.** A free-form chat endpoint; sending the whole student profile to the model; trusting the model's stated citations; a persistent conversation table "for context"; a second RAG/embedding system; an LLM-based grounding judge (non-deterministic and itself untrusted); loosening the student denial in `EvidenceRetriever` so the tutor can quote the corpus.

**Known limitations.** The validator is non-semantic: a paraphrased key, a subtle factual error in prose, or an invented question fact outside the checked patterns can pass (which is why the key is withheld from the prompt first). The lexicons over-reject by design (a legitimate "confidence interval" would be refused). The real Anthropic provider has still never been run (no key); the tutor has only run against deterministic doubles. Prisma-backed ports are not built, so nothing calls the tutor yet.

**Not claimed.** That the tutor teaches well, that any explanation is correct, that a live model satisfies the contracts, or any inference about a student's mastery, readiness, confidence, ability or mental state.

## D-093 — Product Phase 8 Unit 2: Explanation + Socratic teaching (`@ipmat/tutor`); teaching states with explicit disclosure, no hint ladder, student-work-first mistake teaching

**Context.** Unit 1 left tutoring policy unresolved. A re-audit found nothing newly specified. What the repository DOES specify: the practice result screen reveals the correct answer and solution steps only for a SUBMITTED attempt, never for a skip (Phase 2); an autopsy hypothesis is confirmed, rejected or corrected by the student only and a RepairPlan is confirmed-only (D-006, D-039). Hint levels, hint content and any escalation order are specified nowhere.

**Decision.** (1) Six TEACHING STATES (hint, guided question, explanation, full solution, mistake explanation, concept clarification), each a contract in `TUTOR_INTENT_POLICIES` (disclosure, context sections, required and forbidden explanation parts, provisional length cap), selected by the intent only. (2) NO LADDER: one hint mode, no levels, no escalation, no "next mode" - the hint-level policy is essential and undefined, so it is not implemented. A repeated request is not escalated; an identical repeat of a caller-supplied prior action is refused. (3) ANSWER REVEAL aligned to the existing result screen: the key enters a prompt only for `explain_question`/`explain_mistake`/`clarify_solution` AND a SUBMITTED attempt; hints, guided questions and concept explanations never receive it; a skipped/abandoned attempt never unlocks it (this TIGHTENS Unit 1, whose "any finalized attempt" rule was looser than the specified screen). The missing-attempt code is now `submitted_attempt`. (4) SOCRATIC STEP as a deterministic teaching ACTION `{checks, question, conceptRef, evidenceRefs, learnsFromReply}` validated mechanically; only the question reaches the student; no reasoning is recorded and extra model fields are stripped. (5) EXPLANATION QUALITY CONTRACT as named `parts` (asked, concept, steps, whyCorrect, whyIncorrectPathFails, takeaway, tryNext) with per-mode required/forbidden sets - `steps`/`whyCorrect` are forbidden while the key is withheld. (6) MISTAKE TEACHING via `TutorDiagnosisPort` (the student's OWN autopsy outcome in student-facing wording only; no rationale, evidence, model confidence or taxonomy code): unconfirmed -> must be put as a hedged hypothesis; confirmed -> may be built on (with a confirmed repair-target label); rejected/corrected -> the rejected wording is excluded from the prompt and checked in the output; cross-student diagnoses refused; only `explain_mistake` and `guide_with_question` receive one; the tutor never writes a diagnosis, mastery or repair row. (7) CONVERSATION STATE only as an explicit caller-supplied `priorInteraction` (<= 3 validated, capped, delimited actions), never stored, never able to widen disclosure. (8) GROUNDING extended: mode contract, Socratic validity, key consistency, diagnosis status, repeats; all text fields (including parts and step fields) go through every text check. Prompt version `tutor-response-v2`; schema gained `guided_question`, `parts`, `socraticStep`.

**Rules kept.** No chatbot, persistence, route, UI, migration, second provider, confidence/ability/mastery/readiness/psychological output, chain-of-thought, evidence beyond the student's own attempt and autopsy outcome, or Unit 3+ work.

**Unresolved (not invented).** Hint levels and content; any reveal before a submission; whether revision/curriculum/simulation/mastery evidence may inform the tutor; source quoting; memory and escalation between modes; rate limits/budgets; tone/reading level; display/reporting; refusal auditing; whether a tutor reply may open or confirm an autopsy hypothesis.

**Alternatives rejected.** A five-level hint ladder; letting a prior-action claim unlock disclosure; revealing the key after a skip; giving the model the diagnostic rationale "to be more helpful"; an LLM-based pedagogy judge; storing Socratic reasoning; a stored conversation.

**Known limitations.** Validation is against supplied structured context and policy, not a proof of mathematical truth; lexicons over-reject; caps (500 characters, two question marks) are PROVISIONAL; no live model has run; nothing calls the tutor yet.

**Not claimed.** That any explanation is correct, that the tutor teaches effectively, or any inference about mastery, readiness, confidence, ability or mental state.

## D-094 — Product Phase 8 Unit 3: AI question generation (`@ipmat/question-generation`); generation is authoring, never publication; no new table, provider, duplicate system or source ingestion

**Context.** Phase 3 already built a constrained generation pipeline (blueprint -> generate -> structural -> recompute -> independent re-derivation -> judge) and Phase 6 built the authoring lifecycle (AI proposal = `ai_generated` draft; 11 gates; exact/near-duplicate identity; `publishQuestion` the only path to `published`). Unit 3 composes them; it does not rebuild either.

**Decision.** (1) `GenerationSpec` = the existing `QuestionBlueprint` + `noveltyLevel` + `examRelevance` (editorial) + `provenanceSourceType: "original"`, with a deterministic `specId` (hash of the canonical form, set-valued fields sorted) recomputed on validation; built from an EXISTING taxonomy cell (`buildBlueprintFromCell`) and planned only over cells the existing coverage reports `uncovered`. (2) A spec is validated BEFORE any model call (exam pack present and valid, the existing DNA classification validator, pattern family belongs to the concept, trap/modes/combination inside the family's documented space, calibration `provisional`, source-backed generation refused). (3) Generation runs the EXISTING `runGenerationPipeline` (three additive, backward-compatible changes: expose the independent re-derivation's answer, accept requested novelty/relevance for the prompt, accept a per-call timeout). (4) Spec compliance adds novelty, relevance, answer-format and a two-option minimum (the authoring `structure` gate accepts a one-option "multiple choice"). (5) Only CONTENT flows into the draft; the spec's DNA is what is recorded (after compliance proved the model's claim agrees); the model's reasoning, explanation, claimed DNA and any extra field are dropped. (6) The draft is `proposeAiQuestion` -> `createDraft` (exact duplicate refused, existing untouched); a blocking pipeline/compliance failure stores the candidate as REJECTED with reasons traced; otherwise the EXISTING `validate` runs all 11 gates (`ai_validated` iff none FAILED, else stays `draft`); the pipeline's near-duplicate screen is NOT blocking - the authoring identity gate routes it to a human. A rejected row never blocks the same wording (numbered id). (7) The independent re-derivation reaches the authoring `answer` gate as `independentReverification` (the SECOND call's answer, never the generator's). (8) `GenerationTrace` (metadata only: spec, provider/model/prompt versions, usage, per-check codes, requested vs claimed vs recorded DNA, identity outcome, provenance, all gates, review requirement, publishability with reasons) goes to an optional sink; no prompt, response, reasoning or credential. (9) `generateBatch` is deterministic (specId order), sequential, bounded by the existing `GenerationLimits` including running budget, and returns exactly one outcome per input (repeat specs and budget skips are recorded, nothing dropped or merged). (10) The service has no publish operation.

**Rules kept.** AI generation never equals publication; no second provider abstraction, duplicate system, table or migration; no external source text; historical evidence is not consumed and never framed as prediction; Hard/Extreme/Novel always need a human; difficulty stays provisional; no chain-of-thought stored.

**Unresolved (not invented).** Cell/tier/novelty priority; reviewer workflow; calibrated difficulty; source-backed generation and its rights basis; how historical records may steer generation; a pedagogical-quality rubric; durable trace storage; production batch sizes/budgets; semantic duplicate detection; `examRelevance` assignment; whether the one-option guard belongs in the authoring gate; whether the model's mandatory `reasoning` field should leave the Phase 3 schema.

**Alternatives rejected.** A generation table; a generation-only validator that bypasses the gates; trusting the model's DNA or difficulty claim; storing the model's reasoning for audit; a similarity score of our own; auto-promoting `ai_validated` to `published`; ingesting source text to "ground" questions; consuming historical records to choose what to generate.

**Known limitations.** Token-overlap duplicate detection; Percentages-only cells; non-durable traces; no live-model run; structural validity is not pedagogical quality.

**Not claimed.** That a generated question is correct, exam-faithful, well calibrated or good teaching; or any prediction of future exam content.

## D-095 — Product Phase 8 Unit 4: Deep personalization (`@ipmat/personalization`); explicit preferences only, presentation not policy, no profile, no inference, no table

**Context.** The repository specifies no personalization and no preference model (`20_STUDENT_MODEL.md`: "no profile, no self-reported data, no preferences"). The brief asks for personalization that is evidence-based and explicit, with no psychological inference and no hidden student model.

**Decision.** (1) FOUR KINDS OF INFORMATION kept apart: explicit preference (the only personalization input), observable training evidence and derived training signals (consumed by the EXISTING engines, never by this layer), and psychological/latent attributes (never inferred, stored or output). (2) A MINIMAL explicit `StudentPreferences { language, verbosity, preferredHelp }`, each nullable (null = existing behaviour); validation is strict and REFUSES any other field, with a distinct `inferred_attribute_not_allowed` for trait-like names (confidence, intelligence, motivation, personality, anxiety, emotion, learning style, ability...); no "learning style" categories. (3) PERSISTENCE: a `PreferenceStore` port keyed by an ownership-verified student id (no list / find-by-value), in-memory implementation only - NO table, NO migration, because nothing can write a preference yet (no route or settings screen); a durable store is a recorded unresolved decision (one row per student, three nullable columns). (4) PRESENTATION, NOT POLICY: the tutor receives only `presentation { language, verbosity }`, validated strictly (extra keys refused); it never reaches the answer-key, ownership, exam-scope, context-selection or grounding rules; the default is byte-identical to the pre-Unit-4 prompt and keeps `tutor-response-v2`, a non-default presentation adds one `PRESENTATION` block and uses `v3`. (5) VERBOSITY: `concise` = only the parts the mode requires (a deterministic check; required parts are never dropped); `detailed` = the optional `tryNext` part within the same caps. (6) LANGUAGE: the canonical English `text` keeps the FULL validator; a `localizedText` is an add-on with its own checks (script, strict whole-token key ban, no solution steps, no identifiers or other-exam names, every number must already occur in the English answer or the context, plus a best-effort Hindi/Hinglish lexicon backstop) and is DROPPED, with the English shown, if any fails; a good translation never rescues a bad English answer. Only the main message is localized. (7) HELP INTENT: explicit intent > stored preference (HELP-0); with none, the preference maps to `give_hint`/`guide_with_question`/`explain_question`; an explanation preference before a submitted attempt is `limited` by the answer-key policy (HELP-2). (8) TRAINING/REVISION: personalization is composed, not rebuilt - `personalizeCurriculum`/`personalizeRevision` return the existing result by reference with an explicit "nothing altered" report, because no preference constrains selection today; a future one would narrow the pool BEFORE the orchestrator and could never widen it. (9) SIMULATION: never personalized (`SIM-0`); the package cannot reach the engine and the engine has no preference field. (10) AUDIT: every decision is `{dimension, rule, input, effect, limitedBy, output}` with a one-line explanation, from a rule catalogue with no weights; every conflict has a named rule or is recorded `unresolved`. (11) Fixed along the way: a literal-control-byte regex in Unit 2's `stripControl` (made the file look binary) is now written with escapes - behaviour unchanged.

**Rules kept.** No profile, learner type, hidden score, confidence/ability/motivation/emotion inference, second provider, second selection engine, key weakening or simulation override; no preference or personalization data ever enters a tutor prompt except the presentation request.

**Unresolved (not invented).** Durable storage and the surface that writes preferences; selection-constraining, difficulty, pacing, practice-intensity and interface preferences; terminology translation; accommodations; evidence-driven personalization; localizing fixed product copy.

**Alternatives rejected.** Inferring a "type" or level from attempts; a difficulty preference with a priority score against adaptive evidence; a blended personalization score; localizing by letting the model's translation bypass English validation; persisting preferences "for later" without a writer; putting preferences in the prompt so the model can "adapt".

**Known limitations.** Hindi/Hinglish validation is best-effort (recall below English); localization covers the main message only; nothing can set a preference yet; no live model has run.

**Not claimed.** That any localized text is faithful or fluent, that preferences improve learning, or any inference about a student's mastery, ability, confidence, motivation or mental state.

## D-096 — Product Phase 8 Unit 5: AI intelligence orchestration (`@ipmat/ai-orchestration`); a deterministic router over existing capabilities, never an agent

**Context.** Phase 8 asks for one controlled pipeline over the tutor, generation, personalization and the Phase 6-7 intelligence. The repository specifies no AI orchestration (no agent, planner, tool-use or loop anywhere); its only orchestration is deterministic and fixed (D-052/D-062, D-088/D-089). Revision's position relative to the adaptive chain is explicitly unspecified (D-081).

**Decision.** (1) A CLOSED capability registry of the seven capabilities that exist (`tutor_response`, `personalization`, `question_generation`, `adaptive_curriculum`, `revision_intelligence`, `simulation_intelligence`, `exam_intelligence`), each descriptor stating purpose, required inputs, output type, allowed actors/roles, exam and student scope, `mayCallLlm`, `mayMutateState` and its validation; only the tutor and generation may call a model, only generation may mutate (candidates only, `content_admin` only), none publishes. (2) A closed task list and a FIXED route table (task -> code-defined workflow); workflows are explicit step lists with declared dependencies (earlier steps only), `onFailure`, `optional` and (never shipped) fallbacks, validated at construction (invalid = construction fails). No semantic routing, no model-chosen capability, no loop, recursion, planner or memory. (3) AUTHORIZATION BEFORE EXECUTION: strict per-task params (escalation keys - capability, workflow, actor, scope, examCode, studentId, enrollmentId, intent, fallback, provider... - refused by name); actor kind/role checked against every capability in the workflow; a student's enrollment verified through the tutor's ownership port (one answer for missing and foreign), the exam being the ENROLLMENT's; a staff spec's exam must be in the actor's exams; re-checked per step. (4) CONTEXT ROUTER: each capability receives only its minimum input (readers: three ids; tutor: one request; generation: one spec). (5) STRUCTURED FAILURES: 13 distinct kinds; thrown errors are classified by code and their messages are never copied; a failed capability's own result is preserved; `completed | partial | failed | refused` where a failed `stop` step is `failed`, only `continue` failures are `partial`, and a fallback that ran is never `completed`. (6) FALLBACK ONLY WHEN DECLARED: a declared fallback must be registered, not the step's own capability, non-mutating, same actors and scope, with a stated reason, and may never cover authorization / policy / request failures. (7) COMPOSITION: outputs are preserved independently, unmodified and by reference, keyed by step id; the revision/curriculum review returns both with order and priority UNSPECIFIED; no score, ranking or merged verdict exists. (8) AUDIT: concise selection rule, per-step input digests (never inputs), named validations, failure kind/code, fallback flag and the deciding policy; metadata only; no reasoning, plan or scratchpad field exists. (9) PUBLIC VIEW built field by field: the tutor's own student view, the student's applied preference decisions, step statuses with failure kind/code, staff candidate codes; Phase 7 intelligence results have no defined student presentation and are reported as withheld. (10) Adapters wrap the existing services without adding policy; the orchestrator imports no model, `@ipmat/ai`, database or engine.

**Rules kept.** The orchestrator coordinates, never replaces or overrides: tutor disclosure, grounding, authoring publication, training order, simulation mechanics and content rights remain the existing systems'. Model output can never select, grant or escalate a capability. No second provider, retrieval system, adaptive engine, memory or table; no migration.

**Unresolved (not invented).** Preference among valid capability paths; Revision's position; any fallback; student presentation of the Phase 7 results; durable audit storage; whole-workflow budgets, timeouts and rate limits; whether a model may suggest a capability.

**Alternatives rejected.** An LLM planner or router; a tool-use loop; a capability score or "most useful" ranking; a generic agent memory; letting a model resolve conflicts between capabilities; silently retrying a failed capability as another; merging capability outputs into one answer or score; storing prompts or traces for audit.

**Known limitations.** No route or UI; readers are injected (production binding to the `@ipmat/training-recommendation` composers is not wired here); only as strong as each capability's own validation (the non-semantic grounding and Hindi/Hinglish backstop caveats still apply); no live model has run.

**Not claimed.** That any orchestrated answer is correct or well taught, or any inference about a student.

## D-097 — Product Phase 9 Unit 1: Production persistence foundation; two tables, a source-of-truth rule, a durable orchestration audit

**Context.** Phase 9 productionizes the existing system. An audit of migrations 0001-0016 found every authoritative state already persisted (identity, enrollment, attempts/events, autopsy, repair, practice/training, simulations, content and its lifecycle). Exactly two things were explicitly left unpersisted: a student's explicit preferences (D-095: "no durable store until a writer exists") and the orchestration audit (D-096: "durable audit storage unresolved"). Both already had domain ports (`PreferenceStore`, `OrchestrationAuditSink`).

**Decision.** (1) Persist ONLY those two, in migration 0017 (additive; two tables, CHECKs, one trigger). (2) State classes: AUTHORITATIVE (original facts, written once or append-only), DERIVED (functions of authoritative data - mastery evidence, revision, curriculum, readiness; never stored, never read back as evidence), EPHEMERAL (orchestration outputs, prompts, tutor replies; never stored). Nothing derived was added to storage; nothing authoritative was re-derived. (3) `student_preferences`: PK = student id, three nullable CHECK-constrained columns, no column that could hold a trait; `PrismaPreferenceStore` implements the domain port, patches only mentioned fields (per-field upsert, no read-modify-write), validates through the domain validator first. (4) `orchestration_audits`: execution facts only (request id as correlation + idempotency key, task, workflow, actor kind, student, exam, status, deciding policy, step facts, timestamps); NO prompt/response/input/output/params/reasoning column; the store is an ALLOWLIST that refuses an unexpected key rather than storing it; append-only by trigger (the only permitted UPDATE is the FK detaching a deleted student); idempotent per request id, a different audit under the same id is a `conflict`. (5) Student reads take the authenticated student id and filter on it in the query; another's row is indistinguishable from a missing one; operator reads are a separate interface type; staff and unverified-claim audits (`student_id` NULL) are unreachable by students. (6) Persistence classes live in `@ipmat/db` only; the domain boundary is unchanged; in-memory references run the same contracts as the Prisma implementations.

**Rules kept.** No new mastery/score/inference, no AI behaviour, no agent, no route/UI, no change to the answer-key policy, authorization, or existing product semantics; migrations 0001-0016 untouched; student/enrollment/exam scoping preserved.

**Unresolved (not invented).** Audit retention period; whether a student deletion request must delete audit rows (detach-and-retain is an engineering default, not a legal position); whether audit identifiers are personal data; student access to/export of their own audit; preferences per student vs per exam; whether the refused-before-routing audit `task` default (`explain_question`) should become nullable; data-export format.

**Alternatives rejected.** Persisting derived intelligence "for speed" (a second source of truth); an audit column for reasoning/outputs/params; storing the unverified student claim; a generic "events" table; making audit rows deletable by cascade without a decision; a preference column for anything that describes a student.

**Known limitations.** No route or UI uses either table; `apps/api` does not yet construct the stores; no retention job; the trigger is not tamper-proof against a database owner.

**Not claimed.** That the retention defaults meet any legal requirement, that the audit is tamper-proof, or that any new capability reaches a student. See [PHASE_9_UNIT_1_REVIEW.md](PHASE_9_UNIT_1_REVIEW.md).

## D-098 — Product Phase 9 Unit 2: Application integration (`@ipmat/assistant-api`); tutor, preferences and simulation behind the existing auth boundary; no new policy

**Context.** Phase 8 built the tutor, personalization, generation and orchestration over injected ports with no route and no production binding; Phase 7 built the simulation engine and Unit 1 of Phase 9 the durable preference/audit stores. The HTTP boundary (`apps/api`) derives identity only from the session cookie (`resolvePracticeClaim`). There is no staff identity, no authoritative IPMAT simulation configuration, no student-facing presentation for the Phase 7 intelligence, and no provider credential in this environment.

**Decision.** (1) A new application package `@ipmat/assistant-api` (domain + `@ipmat/ai` only; no Prisma, vendor SDK, HTTP or environment access - tested) holds `TutorApiService`, `PreferencesApiService`, `SimulationApiService` and `ContentGenerationApiService`; `apps/api/server.ts` gains one route table whose handlers each call one service method. (2) The browser requests a NAMED OPERATION; a fixed table maps it to a Phase 8 task; the actor is the session's student and current enrollment; every other key (identity, enrollment, exam, capability, workflow, task, actor, role, permissions, presentation, spec, fallback) is refused with 400 before anything runs. The model never chooses a capability, workflow, permission, identity or fallback; the Phase 8 orchestrator is unchanged. (3) Presentation (language/verbosity) comes only from the student's stored explicit preference through the personalization capability; the client cannot supply it. (4) The tutor response is the existing `toStudentTutorView` inside a smaller DTO (`status`, `tutor`, `preferenceNotes`, `failure`); failures use FIXED student-safe wording per failure kind - the domain's codes and messages, validation details, ids, audit, capability names and provider text never reach the client. (5) Production bindings for the tutor's read ports live in `@ipmat/db` (`PrismaTutorOwnershipPort`/`QuestionPort`/`AttemptPort`: scoped by student and exam in the query itself) and the concept port is built from the existing exam pack; no diagnosis/evidence/source port is bound. (6) Provider selection reuses `resolveAiConfig`: `anthropic` only with an explicit key and model, otherwise the tutor is honestly `503 not_available` - never faked; the application layer never reads or logs a key. (7) Simulation routes wrap the existing service (server clock only; answer accepts `{position, answer}` only; no result route; production configuration source is empty until the owner supplies one). (8) Question generation is an application service with NO HTTP route (no staff identity exists); it accepts only a trusted `StaffClaim`, returns reason codes only, always `published: false`, and has no publish operation. (9) The Phase 7 intelligence readers are not bound and no route reads them. (10) Web: one client module that narrows responses to a closed field set and a small panel on the result screen of a submitted attempt.

**Rules kept.** Answer-key policy, submitted-attempt requirement, context firewall, grounding validation, ownership/exam/enrollment scoping, no chain-of-thought, no psychological inference, no auto-publication, no new mastery/readiness metric, no new table or migration, no second provider abstraction.

**Unresolved (not invented).** Staff authentication and a generation route; the IPMAT simulation configuration; simulation result/review presentation; student presentation of revision/curriculum/readiness/exam intelligence; binding diagnosis/evidence/sources to the tutor; HTTP-level rate limits and per-request budgets; a live-model run (blocked: no credential).

**Alternatives rejected.** Letting the client name a task or capability; accepting `presentation` from the client; returning the orchestration public view (it lists capability names); a staff route with a made-up role check; exposing the simulation result; binding Phase 7 readers "to have something to show"; a dev-scripted tutor model.

**Known limitations.** No live-model validation; the in-memory development wiring has preferences only (tutor and simulations answer 503); a prompt-injection defence is the existing tutor policy plus deterministic validation, not a guarantee; tests use deterministic provider doubles.

**Not claimed.** That tutor output is correct or well calibrated, that doubles equal a real model, or that the simulation reflects the real exam. See [PHASE_9_UNIT_2_REVIEW.md](PHASE_9_UNIT_2_REVIEW.md).

## D-099 — Product Phase 9 Unit 3: Security, observability and reliability; server-side limits, redacting observability, one correlation id, bounded AI budgets

**Context.** Units 1-2 exposed the tutor, preferences and simulation through the session boundary. The audit found no body-size limit, rate limiting, request id, logging, metrics, health endpoint, security headers or origin check; a malformed cookie could throw out of the parser; the Anthropic client kept the SDK's silent retries and 10-minute timeout on top of `generateStructured`'s own retries (a worst-case tutor request of about three minutes); and an unreachable database surfaced as a generic 500. The repository has no staff identity, no shared store for counters and no operator-authenticated channel.

**Decision.** (1) A zero-dependency `@ipmat/observability` package: a request context carried by `AsyncLocalStorage`, a REDACTING logger over a closed field allowlist (anything else is dropped and counted; objects dropped; strings truncated, control-stripped and credential-scanned; errors logged by NAME and CODE only; the student only as a one-way reference), bounded-cardinality counters and fixed-bucket histograms, a fixed-window rate limiter and a concurrency gate. (2) Request correlation: the id is ALWAYS generated by the server (an inbound `x-request-id` is ignored), is returned only as a response header, and is the orchestration audit's `request_id`, so one request has one id across HTTP, service, orchestration, provider and audit. (3) Transport hardening in `apps/api`: 32 KiB body cap (413) and JSON-typed bodies only (415), security headers on every response, an `Origin` allowlist for state-changing methods (production refuses to start without `IPMAT_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`), a session-token shape check before any lookup, a safe cookie decoder, server request/headers timeouts, and a closed whitelist of route templates for labels. (4) Rate limiting is server-controlled and keyed by values the SERVER derives (peer address, authenticated student, normalized login email); `X-Forwarded-For` counts only with `IPMAT_TRUST_PROXY=true` and then only its last hop; tutor requests are also capped at one in flight per student; a rejected tutor request never reaches the model; the limiter is in-process and bounded and evicts the oldest window rather than failing open. (5) Explicit AI budget: the SDK client no longer retries (`maxRetries: 0`, 60 s transport cap); per tutor request 20 s per provider call, one explicit retry, one grounding retry and a 45 s overall deadline, all reported as one safe "temporarily unavailable" result; no fallback capability exists. A deterministic output guard rejects a reply reproducing 48+ characters of the tutor's system prompt. (6) Provider calls are observed by a transparent decorator (latency, outcome, coarse failure category; never prompts, responses or provider messages). (7) `/healthz` (liveness) and `/readyz` (readiness, generic `ready`/`not_ready`, 2 s probe cap) with no dependency detail; metrics are held in memory and NOT served over HTTP. (8) A database that is unreachable or overloaded maps to a retryable 503 with `Retry-After` in the auth, practice, enrollment and assistant error mappers (name/code check; no new coupling).

**Rules kept.** Existing authentication architecture, ownership checks, the answer-key policy, student/exam isolation and the Unit 1-2 contracts are unchanged; no new table or migration; no agent, loop or model-chosen routing; nothing the client reports becomes trusted.

**Unresolved (not invented).** A global (multi-instance) limiter and a metrics export channel (need shared infrastructure and an operator identity); calibration of the provisional limits; whether attempt routes should stop distinguishing 403 from 404 (D-060 contract; reported, not changed); log and audit retention/access (D-097); a live-model run (no credential); a staff identity.

**Alternatives rejected.** Trusting a client request id or forwarded address; logging "just the error message" for diagnosis; an unauthenticated metrics endpoint; a failing-open limiter under key flooding; letting the SDK retry silently; a distributed-limiter claim without a shared store; changing the 403/404 attempt contract inside a hardening unit.

**Known limitations.** Limits are per process; the output guard is verbatim-only; provider failure handling is verified against failure doubles, not a real provider's behaviour; connection and pool timeouts remain `DATABASE_URL` settings.

**Not claimed.** DoS resistance, tamper-proof logs, or that prompt injection is eliminated. See [PHASE_9_UNIT_3_REVIEW.md](PHASE_9_UNIT_3_REVIEW.md).


## D-100 — Product Phase 9 Unit 4: Monetization and entitlements; identity, billing, entitlement and usage kept separate; entitlement derived, never stored; the browser names a plan id and nothing else

**Context.** Until this unit the only gate was authentication -> onboarding -> enrollment, so every enrolled student could use every feature, including paid model calls (tutor) and full simulations; there was no plan, price, subscription, payment record, usage record or entitlement concept, no payment provider, and no usage persistence (Unit 3's limiter is per-process and counts requests, not billable units). The audit (PHASE_9_UNIT_4_REVIEW.md section 1) found the commercial state neither derivable nor already persisted, except entitlement, which IS derivable.

**Decision.** (1) Two packages: `@ipmat/billing` (pure domain: plan catalog, subscription state machine, `EntitlementService`, `UsageService`, the `PaymentProvider` interface, persistence ports; no Prisma/HTTP/vendor/env/clock) and `@ipmat/billing-api` (`CommerceGuard`, `BillingApiService`, `BillingWebhookService`); implementations in `@ipmat/db`. (2) Migration 0018, additive: `billing_subscriptions`, `billing_events` (append-only), `usage_events` (ledger), `ai_usage_records` (facts only, no cost). (3) Entitlement is DERIVED on every call from subscription status + paid period + the plan catalog + the server clock; there is no entitlement column. (4) Plans are configuration (`IPMAT_BILLING_CATALOG`), validated strictly; the repository ships no plan, price or baseline; `IPMAT_ENTITLEMENTS=open|enforced` is mandatory in production and `enforced` requires a catalog. (5) A subscription's status changes only through a verified provider event and the pure `applyBillingEvent` (amount/currency must match the server's checkout snapshot; refund is final and order-independent; out-of-order events are ignored or monotone). (6) Webhook: signature over the exact raw body, atomic idempotent `processEvent` (row lock + unique event id + body digest), generic responses. (7) Usage is reserved BEFORE the work under a per-(student, meter) advisory lock and settled consumed/released; provider-side failure releases the unit. (8) Checkout accepts a plan id only, checked against the active+priced allowlist; one pending subscription per (student, plan). (9) Gated: tutor (metered), simulation start (metered), advanced-training start; exam access is a no-op in open mode. (10) No provider adapter ships; any named provider is refused at startup.

**Rules kept.** Existing authentication/enrollment/ownership, the answer-key and tutor policies, Phase 1-8 semantics, the Unit 1-3 contracts (including 403/404 and the redaction allowlist) are unchanged; commercial logic never enters an engine; no browser-supplied identity, price, amount, currency, entitlement or status is ever read; no card data is stored; AI decides nothing commercial.

**Unresolved (not invented).** Real plans/prices/baseline/limits/durations; whether core practice should be free; which features are advanced; grace period; cancel semantics (implemented as end-of-period, provisional); catalog-change vs existing subscriptions; billing-record retention and personal-data status; manual grant/revoke (needs staff identity); a real provider adapter and its live verification; reconciliation; gating of hypothesis generation; Phase 10 multi-exam bundles.

**Alternatives rejected.** Storing an `entitlements`/`is_paid` column (a second source of truth that goes stale); trusting a redirect/return URL or a client "paid" flag; accepting an amount, plan terms or duration from the client; one global paid boolean; per-plan `if` branches; shipping a mock provider in production wiring; counting usage after the work (races past the limit); a counter row instead of a ledger (no audit, drift); returning the provider's objects or references to the client; retrieval from the provider as a second state channel.

**Known limitations.** No live provider run (the signed-event double is invented); no reconciliation job; per-instance rate buckets; an expired provider session is not detected; operator reads exist only as a type. **Not claimed.** That any price, plan or limit is right, that the double equals a real provider, or that billing records satisfy any legal retention requirement. See [PHASE_9_UNIT_4_REVIEW.md](PHASE_9_UNIT_4_REVIEW.md).
